# Enterprise HRMS Project Status

_Last updated: 2026-08-20 — **Phase 3C, W80 — Internal HR/Manager Review List shipped in development.** The internal HR/manager operational workspace over W75–W79, exactly per §35's own frozen row, which assigns this workstream both the `/performance-reviews` org-wide list/detail page **and** the reopen-with-reason dialog — reconciling W79's own deliberate prior decision to build zero frontend, since that entire HR-facing surface belongs here, not W79. **Explicitly not a new business-rules engine** — every mutation (finalize, reopen) reuses W79's routes verbatim; every read (review detail, goals, competencies, scoring) reuses W76–W78's existing widened `GET .../reviews/:id`; only the org-wide list route itself was extended, never duplicated. **`GET .../performance/reviews` gained pagination and three new filters, in place**: `page`/`pageSize` (matching `listEmployees`' own established `{items,total,page,pageSize}` envelope convention exactly — `count()` aggregate + `limit()/offset()` items query, `desc(createdAt)` default order) and `departmentId`/`positionId`/`reviewerId`, each filtering the review's own historical SNAPSHOT column (`departmentIdSnapshot`/`positionIdSnapshot`/`reviewerEmployeeId`) — never live employee/department/position data, so a later transfer or reassignment can never change which reviews a filter surfaces, consistent with every prior workstream's own historical-integrity discipline. Still gated `performance.manage`-only, exactly as W75 originally built it. **A deliberate, flagged non-widening**: §27's own permission line for this surface reads `performance.manage (org-wide) or performance.read.own (scoped)` — but the "scoped" half is already correctly served by W78's own dedicated `GET .../team-reviews`, and building a second manager-scope resolver into this same route would be exactly the duplication the master brief explicitly warned against; the manager's own view therefore remains entirely W78's `/performance-team`, and the new HR workspace only *links* to it (when a review is still `manager_review`) rather than duplicating manager scoring controls in an HR list row. **The response-shape change is a confirmed-safe breaking change**: the bare-array → paginated-envelope change on `GET .../performance/reviews` was verified via a full-repo grep to have zero existing frontend consumers before this workstream — nothing could regress. New `/performance-reviews` page: filters (cycle/status/employee/reviewer/department/position, each narrowing the already-org-scoped result set, never broadening it — a cross-org ID in any filter yields an empty result, never another organization's data), a paginated review table, and a review-detail dialog showing the review's own historical snapshot context plus goals/competencies/self-assessment/manager-review entirely read-only (editing those remains ESS/W78's own territory, never pulled into this internal workspace). **Score display keeps manager/override/final permanently distinct** — an HR override is never shown as if it were the manager's own result — rendered exactly per the frozen examples: `"Manager score: 88.00 / HR override: 92.50 / Final score: 92.50"` with an override present, `"Manager score: 88.00 / Final score: 88.00"` without one, formatted using the review's own `scoringPrecisionSnapshot`. Finalize (optional override score + mandatory-together reason) shown only in `hr_review`; reopen (explicit target-stage dropdown restricted to stages strictly earlier than the review's current status, mandatory reason) shown only from `manager_review`/`hr_review`/`finalized` and gated `performance.manage` — not `performance.finalize` — carrying forward W79's own §10.1-row-6/§16-derived permission decision unchanged. Acknowledgement is shown strictly read-only (`acknowledgedAt`, or "Not yet acknowledged") — W80 does not own or implement the acknowledgement action itself; the frozen plan is silent on this workstream owning it, so none was invented. Every reused mutation invalidates/refetches the list, detail, and W78 team-reviews queries on success — no locally fabricated workflow state that could drift from the backend. No new table, no new permission key, no migration `0039` (still `0038`, 39 migration files, confirmed unchanged). A real, caught-in-review type bug is worth naming: the root `tsc --build` project-reference typecheck passed cleanly on an incorrectly-typed test-mock `.then()` signature that the per-package `tsc -p tsconfig.json --noEmit` typecheck (the one the actual 3-build pipeline runs) correctly rejected — fixed by binding the mock's `then` directly to a real `Promise`'s own `.then` rather than hand-writing a looser signature, a reminder that project-reference incremental builds are not a substitute for each package's own full typecheck. 15 new frontend tests (`performance-reviews.test.tsx`); the backend pagination/filter extension needed no new backend test file, only one existing `performanceCyclesAndAssignment.test.ts` test widened to assert the new envelope shape (with its own mock harness extended to support real `.where().orderBy().limit().offset()` chaining and inert `count()`/`desc()` passthroughs, matching `employees.test.ts`'s own established precedent of never asserting on a mocked `total` value, since this harness cannot simulate a real SQL aggregate) — full suite: 1115 backend (unchanged) / 343 frontend (+15) tests pass, typecheck clean across all four workspace packages, OpenAPI/codegen byte-identical across two runs, zero migration drift, all three production builds succeed (`mockup-sandbox`, `api-server`, `hrms`). **Lint: NOT AVAILABLE** — pre-existing repository tooling gap, unchanged. **Live QA**: real HTTP throughout (dev server started via `node --env-file=.env`) against `vkvirwdxoiwsiftaarox` — 27/27 real assertions passed outright. A disposable review was built entirely through real routes — rating scale + levels, template + competency, cycle, `generate-reviews` — then carried through a genuine `self_assessment → manager_review → hr_review` progression (real `computedOverallScore`, not staged) before the new HR workspace routes were exercised: the paginated list correctly returned it inside `{items,total,page,pageSize}`; `status`+`reviewerId` filters correctly narrowed to exactly that one review; an `employeeId` filter for the wrong employee and a `departmentId` filter with no match both correctly narrowed to an empty result rather than ever broadening; HR finalize with a `92.50` override correctly preserved the distinct `88.00` manager score alongside it; HR reopen back to `hr_review` correctly incremented `revisionNumber` from 1 to 2; the reviewer correctly saw the review through W78's own `/team-reviews` but was correctly denied (403) the new org-wide HR list, lacking `performance.manage`. Full WWM↔Acme tenant isolation confirmed both directions: WWM HR denied listing Acme's reviews and Acme HR denied listing WWM's (403/404 both ways, no membership), a cross-org `employeeId` filter value returning an empty result rather than leaking another organization's data, a nonexistent/foreign review ID returning 404 (IDOR guard), and an unauthenticated request returning 401. WWM's and Acme's Performance module state was confirmed zero-row before any change and confirmed restored to that exact zero-row state afterward (independently re-verified via a fresh query); all disposable QA rows (5 users/memberships/employees across both organizations, 1 rating scale + levels, 1 template + competency, 1 cycle, 1 review) were deleted and independently confirmed zero remaining; genuine audit history this QA itself generated (real finalize/reopen events) was left untouched, per the platform's own never-delete-audit convention; migration count confirmed unchanged (39 files). No password or session-token value was ever displayed, echoed, logged, or committed — disposable sessions were inserted directly via a local, uncommitted scratch script (deleted immediately after this QA pass, along with its companion HTTP-driver and cleanup scripts) and consumed only in-process by that same local tooling, never printed to any output this conversation could see. Production untouched throughout. **Phase 3C remains IN PROGRESS** — W73 through W80 done; next is **W81 — Performance Dashboard & Reporting** (frozen plan's own next section)._

_Last updated: 2026-08-20 — **Phase 3C, W79 — HR Review, Finalization, Override & Reopen shipped in development.** The authoritative `hr_review → finalized` transition (§10.1 row 4, §16) plus the controlled HR reopen rule (§10.4), exactly per the frozen plan's own W79 scope — **no schema change, still migration `0038`**. **A real, doubly-corroborated conflict in the frozen text was found and resolved, not silently picked**: §10.1 row 4 and §16 both literally say `computedOverallScore` is "computed at the `hr_review → finalized` transition" — but W78 (already shipped) computes and persists it one transition earlier, at `manager_review → hr_review`. Three independent pieces of evidence were weighed against that recurring phrase and all point the same way: (1) §8.7's own schema comment for the per-goal `computedScore` column says it is "computed at manager-submit time," and the review-level aggregate is built from exactly those per-item scores; (2) §10.4's own literal reopen-to-`manager_review` worked example lists `computedOverallScore` among the fields **cleared** when reopening from `hr_review`/`finalized` back to `manager_review` — a field can only need clearing if it was already set, which is only possible if W78 set it; (3) this workstream's own master brief treats `computedOverallScore` as an already-existing, must-be-preserved-exactly value throughout, never something W79 computes. **W79 therefore never recomputes it** — it is read-only historical input here, exactly as W78 left it. **Permissions deliberately diverge from a generic hint**: finalize (+override) is `performance.finalize`; reopen is `performance.manage`, not `performance.finalize` — per §10.1 row 6 and §16's own explicit parenthetical, two independent, mutually-consistent locations in the frozen document. No new permission key. **Finalize and override are ONE route, not two** — §27 names exactly one mutation ("finalize (+ optional override)"); `hrOverrideScore`/`hrOverrideReason` are accepted directly in the finalize body, applied atomically with the status transition in the same call; there is no separate "set override" or "clear override" operation. **Reopen field-clearing**, derived from §10.4's own single worked example and applied consistently to all three valid targets: → `self_assessment` clears `selfAssessmentSubmittedAt`/`managerReviewSubmittedAt`/`hrFinalizedAt`/`acknowledgedAt`/`computedOverallScore`/`hrOverrideScore`/`hrOverrideReason`; → `manager_review` clears the same set minus `selfAssessmentSubmittedAt` (§10.4's own literal example, verbatim); → `hr_review` clears only `hrFinalizedAt`/`acknowledgedAt`/`hrOverrideScore`/`hrOverrideReason` — `computedOverallScore` is preserved since the manager's own work isn't being redone. A prior override does **not** remain active after any reopen; it must be explicitly re-supplied on the next finalize. Goal/competency/self-assessment *content* is never blanked — only these review-row timestamp/decision columns. **2 new routes**: `POST .../reviews/:id/finalize` and `POST .../reviews/:id/reopen`, both atomic conditional updates (`WHERE status = '<expected>'`); reopen also increments `revisionNumber` in the same write. Self-approval: the frozen document contains no rule restricting HR from finalizing a review they were also the reviewer for — none was invented, matching the master brief's own "follow the source of truth" instruction where that source is simply silent. Acknowledgement boundary respected — finalize transitions only to `finalized`, never automatically to `acknowledged` (a later workstream's own job). Audit: exactly `performance_review.finalized`, `.score_overridden` (fired only when an override is supplied, distinct from `.finalized`), and `.reopened` (metadata `{targetStage, reason, revisionNumber}`, full `beforeState`/`afterState`) — §22's own literal names. **No frontend for this workstream**: §35's own workstream table assigns the entire HR-facing surface — the review list/detail page *and* the reopen-with-reason dialog — to **W80** ("Internal HR/Manager Review List"), not W79; building any UI here would pull W80 forward, so none was built. 31 new backend tests, plus full suite: 1115 backend (+31) / 328 frontend (unchanged) tests pass, typecheck clean, OpenAPI/codegen byte-identical across two runs, zero migration drift, all three production builds succeed. **Lint: NOT AVAILABLE** — pre-existing repository tooling gap, unchanged. **Live QA**: every lifecycle transition performed for real (no SQL staging anywhere, per this workstream's own explicit instruction) against `vkvirwdxoiwsiftaarox` — 24/25 real assertions passed outright, the remaining 1 an identical numeric-column string-format false negative to every prior workstream's own, independently confirmed correct by the resulting recomputed score itself. A single disposable review was carried through the **entire real lifecycle twice**: generated → real self-assessment → real manager review (`computedOverallScore = 88.00`) → HR read the preserved manager score → HR overrode to `92.50` and finalized in one call, both scores independently readable and distinguishable → repeat finalization 409 → post-finalization edits blocked for both employee and manager (409) → HR reopened to `manager_review` with a reason (`revisionNumber` → 2, `computedOverallScore`/override cleared, employee's self-assessment content confirmed still intact) → the manager made a real edit and genuinely resubmitted through the real W78 route, producing a **different** recomputed score (`100.00`, proving a real recalculation, not a cached value) → HR finalized again **without** an override, confirming the prior override did not carry over and `revisionNumber` stayed at 2 (only reopen increments it). Full cross-org and role-denial isolation confirmed (employee/manager/Acme all correctly denied finalize/reopen). WWM's and Acme's Performance module state was confirmed zero-row before any change and confirmed restored to that exact state afterward (independently re-verified); all disposable QA rows deleted, verified zero remaining; genuine audit history (4 real W79 events from this QA — 2 `.finalized`, 1 `.score_overridden`, 1 `.reopened`) left untouched; migration count confirmed unchanged (39 rows). No password or session-token value was ever displayed, echoed, logged, or committed. Production untouched throughout. **Phase 3C remains IN PROGRESS** — W73 through W79 done; next is **W80 — Internal HR/Manager Review List** (frozen plan's own next section)._

_Last updated: 2026-08-20 — **Phase 3C, W78 — Manager Review shipped in development.** The authoritative `manager_review → hr_review` transition (§10.1 row 3) plus the deterministic §11 scoring model, exactly per the frozen plan's own W78 scope (`docs/PHASE_3C_PERFORMANCE_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §11/§15/§27) — **no schema change, still migration `0038`**. **Scoring is a new, pure, DB-free module** (`performanceScoring.ts`) implementing §11.1–§11.4/§11.6 exactly: per-item normalization (numeric/percentage/currency clamp `actualResult/target×100` to 0–100; boolean 100/0 from `actualResult≥1`; rating `chosen level/max level×100`, reusing `actualResult` as the "chosen level" column since §8.7 defines no separate one; qualitative excluded entirely, weight forced to 0); §11.3's proportional N/A redistribution within each section (`effectiveWeight = storedWeight/(100−ΣN/A weights)×100`), computed at calculation time only, stored weights never mutated; whole-section N/A shifts that section's weight to 100% on the other; §11.4's weighted average and **round-half-up applied exactly once**, at the final `computedOverallScore` only, via a small dependency-free helper that corrects for IEEE754 binary-representation error (e.g. `1.005×100` naively evaluating to `100.49999999999999`) rather than adding a decimal library. Employee self-ratings are structurally excluded — the scoring interfaces carry no `employeeRatingValue` field at all. **Readiness (§10.1 row 3 + §11.6, both applied)**: every `proposed` goal resolved (row 3's own literal text, not in §11.6); every accepted, non-qualitative, non-N/A goal has `actualResult` (§11.6's more precise text preferred over row 3's shorter summary, which would otherwise impossibly demand a qualitative goal have a score); every non-N/A competency has `managerRatingValue`; accepted, non-qualitative goal weights (regardless of N/A — that only affects redistribution, never the stored-weight invariant) sum to exactly 100 (§11.5's own "and again at manager-review submission," since the manager can edit weights after W77's own check); not both sections end up scoreable-empty (§11.6). Competency weight-sum is not re-validated (already guaranteed at W75's snapshot time). **2 genuine, disclosed reconciliations of gaps in §27's own frozen route table** — the same discipline every prior workstream applied: (1) no competency-manager-rating route was ever named, so W77's employee-only `PATCH .../competencies/:competencyId` gained a second, disjoint reviewer path (dispatched by caller relationship, exactly like the goal-creation route's own employee-vs-reviewer split), (2) no goal-result-entry route was named either, so W76's reviewer-edit-any-goal path (`PATCH .../goals/:goalId`, already `manager_review`-only) was extended with `actualResult`/`managerComment`/`notApplicable`/`notApplicableReason`, each validated against the goal's own `measurementType` (rejected for qualitative, 0/1 only for boolean, must match a configured rating-scale level for rating). **2 new routes**: `GET .../team-reviews` (§27's own exact name — reviews where the caller is the snapshotted `reviewerEmployeeId`, server-resolved) and `POST .../reviews/:id/manager-review` (the transition — readiness-then-score-then-atomic-`UPDATE...WHERE status='manager_review'` inside one transaction, persisting `computedOverallScore` and each scoreable goal's own `computedScore` together; a concurrent or repeat submission affects zero rows, 409). `hrOverrideScore`/`hrOverrideReason` are never written here — confirmed untouched, W79's own fields. Manager authority is exclusively reviewer-of-record — `performance.manage` grants nothing here, matching W76's own precedent (§27 names no HR-override path for any of these routes). Audit: exactly `performance_review.manager_review_submitted` (§22's literal name); goal accept/reject/create audit remains entirely W76's; PATCH/autosave (competency/goal result entry) emits none. **New `/performance-team` page** (§18's own frozen surface) — deliberately NOT inside employee ESS, nav-gated `isHrCapable` matching every other Performance HR/manager nav entry's own established convention (backend authorization remains reviewer-of-record regardless of nav visibility) — shows only the caller's assigned reviews, the employee's submitted self-assessment read-only, goal accept/reject/create/result-entry, competency manager-rating/N/A, and submission with backend-authoritative readiness errors; no HR override/finalize/reopen/acknowledgement control anywhere in it. 24 new pure-scoring unit tests + 31 new backend integration tests + 13 new frontend tests, plus full suite: 1084 backend (+55) / 328 frontend (+13) tests pass, typecheck clean, OpenAPI/codegen byte-identical across two runs, zero migration drift, all three production builds succeed. **Lint: NOT AVAILABLE** — pre-existing repository tooling gap, unchanged. **Live QA**: real HTTP for both lifecycle transitions per this workstream's own explicit instruction (no SQL staging anywhere) against `vkvirwdxoiwsiftaarox` — 25/27 real assertions passed outright, the remaining 2 an identical numeric-column string-format false negative to prior workstreams' own (`"8.00"`/`"5.00"` vs `"8"`/`"5"`), independently confirmed correct by the exact deterministic score match. A disposable review was carried through the entire real lifecycle — generated, self-assessed and submitted by the employee for real, then the manager saw only their own assigned review (an unrelated manager saw zero and was denied direct access), accepted the employee's proposed goal, was genuinely blocked from submitting with two real readiness problems listed, then recorded a goal result (8 of target 10) and a competency rating (5 of max 5) and submitted for real — the API returned `computedOverallScore = 88.00`, matching an independently pre-computed expected value (`80×0.6 + 100×0.4 = 88`) exactly, with the employee's own deliberately-low self-rating (1) confirmed to have no effect on it. Post-submit edits and a repeat submission both correctly 409'd. Full cross-org isolation confirmed both directions. WWM's and Acme's Performance module state was confirmed zero-row before any change and confirmed restored to that exact state afterward (independently re-verified); all disposable QA rows deleted, verified zero remaining; genuine audit history (including the one real `manager_review_submitted` row this QA itself generated) left untouched; migration count confirmed unchanged (39 rows). No password or session-token value was ever displayed, echoed, logged, or committed. Production untouched throughout. **Phase 3C remains IN PROGRESS** — W73 through W78 done; next is **W79 — HR Review, Finalization, Override & Reopen** (frozen plan's own next section)._

_Last updated: 2026-08-20 — **Phase 3C, W77 — Self-Assessment (ESS) shipped in development.** The authoritative `self_assessment → manager_review` transition (§10.1 row 2) plus the employee-side ESS surface it depends on, exactly per the frozen plan's own W77 scope (`docs/PHASE_3C_PERFORMANCE_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §14/§10.1/§11.5/§27) — **no schema change, still migration `0038`**. **§27 named no competency-rating route — a genuine, disclosed gap, reconciled with the smallest possible addition**: `PATCH .../reviews/:id/competencies/:competencyId` (new, own review only, `self_assessment` only, `performance.write.own`), shaped identically to W76's own goal-PATCH route. only `employeeComment` is employee-writable, `self_assessment` only). **3 further new/widened routes**: `GET .../my-reviews` (ESS — caller's own reviews, employeeId always server-resolved, never client-supplied), `POST .../reviews/:id/self-assessment` (the transition itself), and `GET .../reviews/:id` (already widened in W76 to own/reviewer/org-wide visibility; a goal's `managerComment` is deliberately NOT redacted, since it frequently carries a reject-reason the employee needs to see immediately, §12 — a different concept from a scoring "rating"). **Submission readiness (§10.1 row 2, §11.5)**, validated once at submission (400, every problem listed at once, not just the first): every competency has `employeeRatingValue` set; every `accepted`, non-`qualitative`, non-N/A goal has a non-empty `employeeComment`; if any such goal exists, its section's weights sum to exactly 100 (§11.5's "validated at self-assessment submission ... too"). §11.6's "not both sections may be entirely empty" check is deliberately NOT applied here — its own text scopes it explicitly to manager submission (W78's gate); competency weight-sum-to-100 is not re-validated either, already guaranteed at W75's snapshot time. **A real, disclosed baseline fact**: no goal can ever be `accepted` before self-assessment submission (acceptance/manager-creation both require `draft`/`manager_review`, unreachable pre-submission) — so the goal-weight-readiness path is structurally unexercisable through live HTTP in this workstream's own QA; WHERE status = 'self_assessment'`, setting `selfAssessmentSubmittedAt`; a concurrent or repeat submission affects zero rows and returns 409, never a silent double-transition. **This closes the structural gap W76 flagged**: once a review genuinely transitions via this real route (not a fixture stage), every W76 manager-side route (create/edit/accept/reject) becomes live-reachable for the first time — verified for real in both the unit suite and live QA. `/self-service` page (reusing its established tab pattern exactly — Performance is gated independently of `employee_self_service`, degrading only this tab when disabled), showing review status, goals (with employee goal-proposal/edit controls carried over from W76), competency self-rating against the review's real configured scale levels (never hardcoded 1–5), and the submit action with backend-authoritative readiness-problem display — no manager scoring, HR override, or acknowledgement control anywhere in it. 24 new backend tests (`performanceSelfAssessment.test.ts`) + 11 new frontend tests, plus full suite: 1029 backend (+24) / 315 frontend (+11) tests pass, typecheck clean, OpenAPI/codegen byte-identical across two runs, zero migration drift, all three production builds _Last updated: 2026-08-20 — **Phase 3C, W76 — Goals/Objectives Management shipped in development.** Employee-proposal / manager-decision goal workflow over W73's schema, exactly per the frozen plan's own W76 scope (`docs/PHASE_3C_PERFORMANCE_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §8.7/§12/§27) — **no schema change, still migration `0038`**. **Origin/approval model (§12), followed literally**: a goal is either manager-created (`originType: 'manager'`, inserted `approvalStatus: 'accepted'` immediately — official on save, no separate approval step) or employee-proposed (`originType: 'employee_proposed'`, inserted `approvalStatus: 'proposed'` — becomes official only once the reviewer of record explicitly accepts it during `manager_review`; a `rejected` proposal is retained forever, never deleted, permanently excluded from weighting). **4 new routes**: `POST .../reviews/:id/goals` (employee proposes — `performance.write.own`, own review, `self_assessment` only — or the reviewer of record creates an official goal — `performance.review.write`, `draft`/`manager_review` only; which path applies is resolved entirely from the caller's own server-derived relationship to the review via `resolvePerformanceActorEmployeeId`, never a client-supplied flag), `PATCH .../goals/:goalId` (employee edits their own still-`proposed` goal pre-decision, `self_assessment` only; or the reviewer edits any goal, `manager_review` only), `POST .../goals/:goalId/accept` and `/reject` (reviewer of record only, `manager_review` only, atomic conditional `WHERE approvalStatus = 'proposed'` update — a concurrent double-decision or terminal-state redecision returns a controlled 409, never a silent no-op; reject requires a reason, stored as `managerComment`). No new permission key — every route is gated by the union of the two existing `performance.write.own`/`performance.review.write` keys (coarse route gate + fine-grained own/reviewer-of-record scoping in the handler, mirroring `leaveRequests.ts`'s own established precedent). **A real, disclosed consequence of the frozen sequencing**: W75 already creates every review directly in `self_assessment` (never leaving an externally-observable `draft` row), and W77 — which owns the `self_assessment → manager_review` transition — does not exist yet; per §10.2's own editability table ("Manager | nothing on this review" while `self_assessment`) and §15's "accepts ... during `manager_review`", every manager-side action (create/edit/accept/reject) is therefore correctly gated to a review stage no review can reach through any *transition* route in the current baseline. Manager-side routes were nonetheless fully built, and live-verified for real by directly staging one disposable review's `status` column to `manager_review` via SQL (disclosed, not a fabricated business transition — the same single-column fixture stage the unit tests use) — this proved every manager route genuinely works end-to-end over a real database row, not merely against a mock. **Measurement-type validation (§11.1/§8.7)**: `qualitative` (weight forced to 0, no target) and `boolean`/`rating` (no target — boolean scores from `actualResult` alone, rating scores against the review's own rating-scale levels) carry no numeric target; only `numeric`/`percentage`/`currency` require a positive target (percentage additionally capped at 100). **Weight-sum-to-100 across accepted goals is deliberately NOT enforced by any W76 route** — §11.5 assigns that check to "self-assessment submission and again at manager-review submission," both W77/W78 transitions, not W76; only the per-goal, per-type field rules above are validated here. **`GET .../reviews/:id` widened** (the smallest W76-owned adjustment permitted by its own brief): now `performance.read.own` (own employee or reviewer-of-record, resolved via `performanceAuthorization.ts`'s W73-built helpers, used for the first time) or `performance.manage` (org-wide) — was `performance.manage`-only since W75; the org-wide list route (`GET .../reviews`) is unchanged. Response now also carries `goals`. **Audit** follows §22's literal 3-event list (`performance_review_goal.proposed`/`.accepted`/`.rejected` — no `.created`/`.updated` for goals): a manager-created goal's insertion is audited as `.accepted` too (metadata `action: 'manager_created'` vs `'proposal_accepted'`) rather than inventing an unlisted event, matching W74's own "reuse the closest frozen name" precedent; PATCH/edit emits no audit event, matching W74's rating-scale-edit precedent. 31 new backend tests (`performanceReviewGoals.test.ts`) plus full suite: 1005 backend (+31, no new frontend — W76 is backend-only per §35's own scope line, since goal-proposal UI is explicitly W77's and the manager decision-queue UI is explicitly W78's) / 306 frontend (unchanged) tests pass, typecheck clean, OpenAPI/codegen byte-identical across two runs, zero migration drift, all three production builds succeed. **Lint: NOT AVAILABLE** — pre-existing repository tooling gap, unchanged. **Live QA**: unlike W75 (whose dev server failed to load `DATABASE_URL`), the server started successfully this session via `node --env-file=.env`, so live QA ran as real HTTP against the actual running server and development database (`vkvirwdxoiwsiftaarox`) rather than direct SQL — 24/24 real HTTP assertions passed: full setup chain (scale/levels/template/competencies/cycle/generate-reviews), a review created directly in `self_assessment` with zero goals and a correctly-snapshotted `reviewerEmployeeId`, an employee proposing two goals (visible, not yet official), a live 409 when the manager attempted to act while still `self_assessment` (proving the stage gate for real before any fixture staging), then — after the one disclosed SQL stage to `manager_review` — the reviewer accepting one goal (editing its weight in the same action, per §12), rejecting the other with a reason, and creating a third goal directly as an official, already-accepted manager goal; an unrelated caller (HR, not reviewer of record) denied accept (403); a duplicate accept on an already-decided goal correctly 409'd; **the rating-scale immutability lock genuinely activated for the first time against a real review** (a levels-PATCH attempt now correctly 409'd); a repeat `generate-reviews` call on the same cycle correctly 409'd; and full cross-org isolation confirmed both directions (Acme denied 404 on its own org route against WWM's review/goal ids, denied 403 entirely on WWM's org route). WWM's and Acme's Performance module state was confirmed zero-row before any change and confirmed restored to that exact state afterward (independently re-verified via a separate query after cleanup); all disposable QA rows (4 users, 4 memberships, 2 employees, 1 scale/levels, 1 template/competency, 1 cycle, 3 reviews' worth of goals) were deleted, verified zero remaining; genuine audit history (including the 5 real goal-event rows this QA itself generated) was left untouched, per the platform's own never-delete-audit convention; migration count confirmed unchanged (39 rows). No password or session-token value was ever displayed, echoed, logged, or committed — disposable QA sessions were inserted directly (bypassing password login entirely) and generated/consumed only within local, uncommitted scratch scripts deleted immediately after this QA pass. Production untouched throughout. **Phase 3C remains IN PROGRESS** — W73, W74, W75, and W76 done; next is **W77 — Self-Assessment (ESS)** (frozen plan's own next section)._

_Last updated: 2026-08-20 — **Phase 3C, W74 — Rating Scales & Review Templates (HR Configuration) shipped in development.** CRUD + level/competency management over W73's schema, exactly per the frozen plan's own W74 scope (`docs/PHASE_3C_PERFORMANCE_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §27) — **no schema change, still migration `0038`**. **10 new routes** under `.../performance/rating-scales` (list/create broad-read+manage-write, read/update, replace-levels) and `.../performance/templates` (list/create, read/update, replace-competencies) — GET is "read broad" (`performance.read.own`, seeded to every role, per §27's own explicit line); every mutating route requires `performance.manage`. **Rating scale immutability**: a scale's *level set* becomes structurally locked (409) once any `performance_reviews` row references it — derived live via a query, never a stored counter; the scale's own name/description remain always editable and status can always move to/from `archived` regardless of lock state, since only the level set (values/labels/ordering/count) could alter a historical review's meaning. The new `GET .../rating-scales/:id` response carries a `levelsLocked` boolean so the frontend can show this proactively, not only after a failed save. **Templates carry no usage lock at all** — a review snapshots competencies at *creation* time (a later workstream), so a template edit can never rewrite review history; editing is instead blocked only while the template's own `status` is `archived` (a W74-local business-rule choice, not itself frozen, chosen for consistency with every other "archived means retired" resource on this platform — reactivating first is required to keep editing). Template creation/update validates `ratingScaleId` belongs to the same organization (`orgScopedRefs.ts`'s `assertBelongsToOrganization`, extended with one new table reference) and that `goalsWeight + competenciesWeight` sum to exactly 100 (§11.5); the competency-set PATCH validates weights sum to 100 when `competenciesWeight > 0`, and must be empty when `competenciesWeight === 0` — a deliberate, documented consistency rule beyond the frozen document's own literal text, defense-in-depth matching this codebase's established "validate at save time and again downstream" discipline. **Audit** follows §22's own exhaustive list literally, including its asymmetry: rating scales get only `.created`/`.archived` (no `.updated` event exists for scales in the frozen list — name/description/level edits are deliberately unaudited, flagged rather than silently invented); templates get `.created`/`.updated`/`.archived` (competency-set replacement also emits `.updated`, since no separate competency-specific event name exists). GET/list routes generate zero audit noise. No W73 schema changes were needed — the new `levelsLocked` response field is API-shape only, computed live, not a stored column. Two new HR configuration pages, `/performance-rating-scales` and `/performance-templates` (frozen §19's own exact route names), both `ModuleGate`-wrapped and nav-gated `isHrCapable`, each with a list + create dialog + a "Manage" detail dialog combining base-field editing with an inline, replace-all level/competency-row editor; the locked/archived states are shown as clear inline messages, never a raw error. 32 new backend tests (`performanceRatingScalesAndTemplates.test.ts`) + 20 new frontend tests (`performance-rating-scales.test.tsx` + `performance-templates.test.tsx`) plus full suite: 948 backend (+32) + 294 frontend (+20) tests pass, typecheck clean across `api-server`/`hrms`, OpenAPI/codegen updated and byte-identical across two runs, zero migration drift, all three production builds succeed. **Lint: NOT AVAILABLE** — pre-existing repository tooling gap, unchanged. Supabase advisors re-run: still 79 public tables, 79 RLS-enabled, 0 RLS-disabled, 0 policies — unchanged (no new schema). Live-verified against development (`vkvirwdxoiwsiftaarox`) with WWM's and Acme's Performance module state explicitly read (zero `organization_modules` rows for either, confirming W73's own baseline held) *before* any change and restored to that exact zero-row state afterward: HR created a scale, replaced its levels with a valid set, and confirmed an empty level set and a duplicate value were both rejected (400); a plain employee was denied scale creation (403) but could still list scales (broad read, 200); HR created a template referencing the same-org scale, rejected mismatched weights (400), replaced its competency set validly, rejected competencies not summing to 100 (400), archived it, confirmed further edits were blocked (409), then reactivated it; an Acme org-admin account could not create a template referencing WWM's `ratingScaleId` (400), could not read or update WWM's scale through Acme's own org route (404 both), and could not read WWM's template through Acme's own org route (404); a WWM token was denied entirely on Acme's org route (403, membership-level); an unauthenticated request was denied (401) — **20/20 real assertions passed**. The scale-immutability-once-used lock was verified via the fixture-based backend unit test rather than live QA, per explicit instruction not to create a disposable dependent Performance review (which would pull W75 forward) — the fixture directly seeds a `performance_reviews` row referencing the scale and confirms the levels PATCH returns `409`. All disposable QA data (3 users, 3 memberships, 1 rating scale, 1 template, 2 temporary `organization_modules` rows) deleted afterward; both organizations' Performance module-enablement restored to the exact confirmed-zero-row prior state, verified by direct re-query. No CHECK constraints beyond NOT NULL/FK/UNIQUE were added for business rules explicitly assigned to later workstreams' service layers (weight-sum-to-100, qualitative-weight-must-be-zero) — matching how the frozen plan itself assigns those validations to save-time service logic (§11.5), not the schema. 11 new focused tests (`performanceAuthorization.test.ts`) plus full suite: 916 backend (+11) + 274 frontend (unchanged, no frontend touched) tests pass, typecheck clean across `db`/`api-server`/`hrms`, zero migration drift, all three production builds succeed. _Last updated: 2026-08-19 — **Phase 3B, W66 — Daily Summary Read-Model & Leave/Holiday Integration shipped in development.** `lib/attendanceDailySummary.ts` — a pure computed read-model over `attendance_events`, approved `attendance_adjustments`, approved `leave_requests`, `public_holidays`, and W38's Attendance/timezone configuration, exactly per the frozen plan's §3.3/§3.7 (`docs/PHASE_3B_ATTENDANCE_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`) — never a stored table, nothing is persisted or mutated by a read. One route, two modes: `GET .../organizations/:id/employees/:id/attendance/summary` — `?date=` for a single day, `?from=&to=` (max 100 days, reusing `leaveCalendar.ts`'s own established range limit) for a dense array. **Precedence** (date-level exclusions first, then person-level, then event-derived): pre-hireDate/on-or-after-separationDate → `status: null` (no summary generated, per §3.7's literal wording) → holiday → non-configured-work-day → approved leave (`on_leave`; a merely-*pending* leave request is correctly not treated as approved, live-verified) → an approved whole-day adjustment (`mark_present`/`mark_absent`/`excuse_absence`) → the effective first-clock-in/last-clock-out pair (an approved `manual_clock_in`/`manual_clock_out` adjustment overrides the matching raw event; pending/rejected adjustments never affect the read) → `present`/`late`/`partial`/`absent`, with a final suppression: an otherwise-`absent` day is returned as `null` instead when the employee's *current* `employmentStatus` is `on_leave`/`suspended`, per §3.7's explicit "no absent ever generated for these days." Civil dates are always derived server-side via the organization's configured `general.timezone` (Node's `Intl.DateTimeFormat`, real IANA conversion, inherently DST-correct — no new dependency, no hand-rolled offset math); a missing timezone is a hard `409`, never a silent UTC fallback, per Open Decision 2's own recommended default. **Two interpretive gaps in the frozen document, both resolved and flagged rather than guessed silently:** (1) "partial" is listed in §3.3's status enum with no definition — implemented as an incomplete clock pair (clock-in with no matching clock-out, or vice versa); "late" is used instead of "present" only when `lateMinutes > 0`, with no separate status for an early departure alone. (2) `excuse_absence` has no literal match among the 7 frozen status values — mapped to `on_leave`, the closest existing authorized-absence status, rather than inventing an 8th label. **No new database table or migration** (pure read-model, confirmed zero drift, still `0037`). Performance: a fixed 7 queries per request regardless of range length (employee, general config, attendance config, events, adjustments, leave, holidays), all range-batched — no per-day queries. 37 new tests (14 pure-function + 23 route-integration) plus full suite: 853 backend (+37) + 240 frontend tests pass, typecheck/lint clean, OpenAPI/codegen updated and byte-identical across two runs, all three builds succeed. Supabase advisors re-run: still 70 public tables, 0 with RLS disabled (unchanged — no new tables). Live-verified against development (`vkvirwdxoiwsiftaarox`) with a non-UTC organization timezone (Asia/Tokyo, UTC+9) specifically: an event stored at UTC 2026-03-04T20:00 correctly bucketed to local civil date 2026-03-05 (not the UTC date), confirmed via both a direct-hit date and its UTC-adjacent neighbor; a missing-timezone `409` confirmed before configuring one; approved-vs-pending leave distinction confirmed; a WWM holiday confirmed invisible to Acme's summary and vice versa (both directions, live); an approved HR `mark_present` adjustment confirmed to override an otherwise-`absent` day; cross-organization summary access denied both directions; own/team/org-wide visibility tiers all confirmed; zero audit events generated by any of the many reads performed. Disposable QA data (employees, users, memberships, leave records, holiday records, attendance events/adjustments) all deleted afterward; both orgs' temporary Attendance enablement reverted to disabled. **Issue found and flagged, not silently fixed:** Acme's `organization_settings` `general` namespace row was overwritten (to set a QA timezone) and later deleted during cleanup without first confirming whether it held genuine pre-existing data — WWM's own equivalent row was confirmed empty beforehand, but Acme's was not checked first; since Acme is a long-standing development-only QA tenant and the `general` namespace holds only `timezone`/`defaultLocale`/`contactEmail`/`contactPhone`, practical impact is expected to be minimal, but this is flagged transparently rather than omitted. ## Phase 3C Progress

Status: **IN PROGRESS** (frozen: `docs/PHASE_3C_PERFORMANCE_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`).

- **W73 — Performance Foundation & Module Activation — Done (2026-08-20).** All 9 tables (`performance_rating_scales`, `performance_rating_scale_levels`, `performance_review_templates`, `performance_template_competencies`, `performance_cycles`, `performance_reviews`, `performance_review_goals`, `performance_review_competencies`, `performance_review_evidence`), migration `0038`, all RLS-enabled with zero policies; 6 permissions seeded (`performance.read.own`, `performance.write.own`, `performance.review.write`, `performance.manage`, `performance.finalize`, `performance.reports.read`); `lib/performanceAuthorization.ts` foundation (own/reviewer-of-record/organization-wide tiers, mirrors `attendanceAuthorization.ts`); `performance` `organization_settings` namespace registered; `performance` module flipped `hidden` → `active`. No route, no frontend, no CRUD/workflow logic — exactly the frozen plan's own W73 scope. See this file's header paragraph above for full detail, including the one deliberate beyond-the-frozen-list column addition (`createdAt`/`updatedAt` on goals/competencies) and the schema decisions for immutable-once-used rating scales and N/A/scoring/snapshot support.
- **W74 — Rating Scales & Review Templates (HR Configuration) — Done (2026-08-20).** 10 new routes: `GET/POST .../performance/rating-scales`, `GET/PATCH .../performance/rating-scales/:id`, `PATCH .../performance/rating-scales/:id/levels`, `GET/POST .../performance/templates`, `GET/PATCH .../performance/templates/:id`, `PATCH .../performance/templates/:id/competencies` — GET routes gated `performance.read.own` (broad, matches §27's "read broad" line), POST/PATCH gated `performance.manage`. No new table, no migration `0039` (W73's 9 tables remain final). Rating-scale levels become structurally immutable once any `performance_reviews` row references the scale — live-derived via `isRatingScaleUsed()`, not a stored counter, surfaced proactively to the frontend as a new `levelsLocked: boolean` field on the single-scale read (a deliberate, non-frozen-literal API addition); only the level *set* locks — name/description/status (archive/reactivate) stay editable regardless. Review templates carry NO usage lock (competencies snapshot into `performance_review_competencies` at review-creation time, a later workstream); instead a template is blocked from editing only while its own `status === 'archived'` (a W74-local, non-frozen-literal business decision, consistent with the platform's other "archived = retired" resources). Weight validation: `goalsWeight + competenciesWeight` must equal 100; competency weights must sum to exactly 100 when `competenciesWeight > 0`, and the competency array must be empty when it is 0 (an interpretive, defense-in-depth addition). Audit events follow §22 literally: rating scales emit only `.created`/`.archived` (no `.updated`, by design — level/name edits are silent); templates emit `.created`/`.updated`/`.archived`. Two new HR pages, `/performance-rating-scales` and `/performance-templates`, both `ModuleGate`-wrapped and nav-gated `isHrCapable`, showing the locked/archived state proactively rather than surfacing a raw 409. 32 new backend tests + 20 new frontend tests, all green; full regression (948 backend / 294 frontend), typecheck, lint (still "NOT AVAILABLE — pre-existing repository tooling gap"), codegen (byte-identical across two runs), 3 builds, and live QA against `vkvirwdxoiwsiftaarox` (WWM + Acme, 20/20 real assertions, cross-org 400/404s confirmed) all passed; the immutable-once-used lock itself was verified only via a fixture-based unit test (not live QA), per explicit instruction not to pull W75's review-creation logic forward.
- **W75 — Performance Cycles & Review Assignment — Done (2026-08-20).** 8 new routes: `GET/POST .../performance/cycles`, `GET/PATCH .../performance/cycles/:id`, `POST .../performance/cycles/:id/generate-reviews`, `GET .../performance/reviews`, `GET .../performance/reviews/:id` — every route, including reads, gated `performance.manage` (unlike W74's broader `performance.read.own` reads, since cycle/assignment configuration is HR/admin-only territory per this workstream's own brief). No new table, no migration `0039`. Two reconciliation notes against the frozen plan's own imprecise prose (§10.1 rows 0+1 combined into one atomic HR action since no separate "open reviews" endpoint exists in §27 and no cron infra is available; goals never snapshotted at assignment time since templates own only competencies, not goals) — both documented in `lib/performanceCycles.ts`'s own file header. Cycle lifecycle `draft → open → closed → archived`, editable only while `draft`; `generate-reviews` is a one-time-per-cycle atomic action (guarded by a conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE status = 'draft'`) that resolves eligible employees (`all_active`/`department`/`position` auto-resolved from `employmentStatus = 'active'`, `manual` requires an explicit same-org/active `employeeIds` list) and creates each review directly in `self_assessment` status with `reviewerEmployeeId`/`departmentIdSnapshot`/`positionIdSnapshot`/weights/`scoringPrecisionSnapshot`/`acknowledgementRequiredSnapshot` all captured at that moment and competencies snapshotted from the template. New `/performance-cycles` HR page, `ModuleGate`-wrapped, nav-gated `isHrCapable`. 26 new backend tests + 12 new frontend tests, all green; full regression (974 backend / 306 frontend), typecheck, codegen (byte-identical across two runs, plus new top-level `performance-cycles`/`performance-reviews` OpenAPI tag entries closing a gap W73/W74 had left open), zero migration drift, 3 builds all passed. Live QA performed via direct SQL against `vkvirwdxoiwsiftaarox` rather than HTTP (the dev server's `DATABASE_URL` wasn't auto-loaded in this environment; its `.env` was never read, per this workstream's strict secret-preservation instruction) — reviewer resolution, department/position/weight snapshots, the duplicate-assignment unique constraint, the rating-scale usage-lock activation, and historical-integrity-after-mutation were all confirmed live against real WWM (org 3) data, then fully cleaned up and the exact prior baseline (zero `organization_modules`/`organization_settings` rows) restored. See this file's header paragraph above for full detail.
- **W76 — Goals/Objectives Management — Done (2026-08-20).** 4 new routes: `POST .../reviews/:id/goals` (employee proposes — own review, `self_assessment` only — or the reviewer of record creates an official goal — `draft`/`manager_review` only; the caller's own server-derived relationship to the review decides which, never a client flag), `PATCH .../goals/:goalId` (employee edits their own still-`proposed` goal, or the reviewer edits any goal during `manager_review`), `POST .../goals/:goalId/accept` and `/reject` (reviewer of record only, `manager_review` only, atomic conditional update — concurrent/terminal-state redecision is a controlled 409). No new permission key — gated by the union of `performance.write.own`/`performance.review.write`. Manager-created goals are inserted `accepted` immediately (Owner Decision 4, no separate approval step); employee proposals start `proposed` and become official only once accepted. Weight-sum-to-100 across accepted goals is deliberately not enforced here — §11.5 assigns that to the W77/W78 submission transitions. `GET .../reviews/:id` widened from `performance.manage`-only to also allow the review's own employee or reviewer of record (`performance.read.own`), now also returning `goals`. Audit follows §22's literal 3-event list (`.proposed`/`.accepted`/`.rejected`; manager-goal-creation reuses `.accepted` since no `.created` event is named). 31 new backend tests, all green; full regression (1005 backend / 306 frontend, no new frontend — W76 is backend-only per §35, since goal-proposal/decision UI belongs to W77/W78), typecheck, codegen (byte-identical), zero migration drift, 3 builds all passed. Live QA ran as real HTTP (the dev server started successfully this session via `node --env-file=.env`) against `vkvirwdxoiwsiftaarox` — 24/24 real assertions, including the rating-scale immutability lock genuinely activating for the first time against a real review, and one disclosed direct-SQL stage of a single review's `status` column to `manager_review` (W77's own transition doesn't exist yet) to prove the manager routes work end-to-end for real. WHERE status = 'self_assessment'`, sets `selfAssessmentSubmittedAt`, concurrent/repeat submission returns 409), and `GET .../reviews/:id` (widened further: an own-employee viewer never sees competency `managerRatingValue`/`managerComment` before `hr_review`, per §14 — a goal's `managerComment` is never redacted, since it may carry a reject-reason the employee needs). ## Phase 3B Completion Report (W72)

All nine workstreams (W64–W72) are complete. Checking against `docs/PHASE_3B_ATTENDANCE_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s own per-workstream "Definition of done" lines (§10 — this document has no single numbered Phase Acceptance Criteria section the way Phase 3A's §24 does, so each workstream's own line is the frozen bar), item by item:

Yes — migration `0037` applied to development (not merely generated; see Database below), four permissions seeded and confirmed live, `attendance` module flips active/enabled independently, 11 foundation tests green.

- **W66** — "summary output verified correct against every documented status/eligibility rule in §3.3/§3.7." Yes — 14 pure-function + 23 route-integration tests, plus a non-UTC-timezone live QA pass (Asia/Tokyo) specifically exercising the civil-date boundary.
- **W67** — "full request→decide cycle works, correctly distinct from W65's immediate HR-entry path." Yes — pending→approved/rejected, terminal-state `409`, concurrent-decision safety all live-verified, re-confirmed in W71's terminal-conflict re-test.
- **W68** — "an employee can clock in/out and see their own attendance entirely through ESS." Yes — RTL suite plus live HTTP QA of the exact endpoints/payloads the tab sends.
- **W69** — "HR can see and filter a real attendance register end to end." Yes.

### Executive Summary

Phase 3B delivered a complete, tenant-isolated, live-computed Attendance module end to end: module activation → self-service clock-in/out and HR manual entry → a timezone-aware, precedence-ordered daily summary read-model integrated with Leave and Public Holidays → employee-initiated correction requests with atomic HR approve/reject → an ESS "My Attendance" tab → an internal HR/manager Attendance Register → an Attendance Dashboard → four CSV-exportable Attendance Reports — on top of the completed Foundation, Phase 2A, Phase 2B (which already delivered Leave and Employee Self Service ahead of Phase 3's own nominal ordering — see Next Roadmap Step below), and Phase 3A Recruitment. Nine workstreams (W64–W70) each shipped independently reviewed, tested, and live-QA'd against the real development database; W71 verified the phase as a whole with zero defects and zero source changes; W72 (this report) closes it.

**A load-bearing distinction from every prior phase's completion report:** Phase 3B's migrations (`0036`, `0037`) are **already applied to the development database**, not merely generated and awaiting approval like every earlier phase's migrations. This reflects an explicit, separately-approved rollout discipline this session established starting with the Supabase RLS remediation (`0036`) and carried through Attendance (`0037`) — migrate → verify → seed → live QA against real data — documented in full in `docs/SUPABASE_SECURITY_REMEDIATION.md` and this file's own W64–W71 entries. **Production received none of this** — no migration, no data, no configuration change of any kind.

### Workstreams Completed

- **W64 — Attendance Foundation & Module Activation.** `attendance_events`/`attendance_adjustments` schema (migration `0037`), module flipped `hidden`→`active`, four permissions seeded, `lib/attendanceAuthorization.ts` foundation.
- **W65 — Attendance Event Capture.** Self-service clock-in/out (server-derived identity, no sequencing rejection per Open Decision 1) and HR direct-entry (auto-approved via `attendance_adjustments`, not a raw `hr_manual` event — a deliberate reconciliation, see below).
- **W66 — Daily Summary Read-Model & Leave/Holiday Integration.** `lib/attendanceDailySummary.ts`, precedence-ordered, timezone-aware, never persisted.
- **W67 — Attendance Adjustments.** Employee-initiated requests, atomic approve/reject, organization-wide-only approval authority (Open Decision 3).
- **W68 — Employee Self-Service Attendance.** "My Attendance" ESS tab, zero new backend routes.
- **W69 — Attendance Register.** Batched, paginated internal HR/manager list view over the W66 read-model.
- **W70 — Attendance Dashboard & Reporting.** Live tile breakdown + four CSV-exportable reports, reusing the W17/ADR-016 registry.
- **W71 — Phase 3B Verification.** Full repo-wide verification pass; zero defects found, zero source changes.
- **W72 — Phase 3B Completion Report.** This report.

### Features Delivered

**Capture:** an employee clocks themselves in/out through ESS (server-authoritative timestamp, server-derived identity — a client-supplied `employeeId` is always ignored); HR can enter/correct attendance directly for any organization employee via the same underlying mechanism, auto-approved immediately.

**Computed status:** every day's attendance status (`present`/`late`/`partial`/`absent`/`on_leave`/`holiday`/`non_working_day`/`null`) is computed live on every read from raw events, approved adjustments, approved Leave, and Public Holidays — never stored, never a second calculation engine, always timezone-correct via the organization's own configured IANA timezone.

**Correction workflow:** an employee can request a correction to their own attendance (always `pending`, never self-approving); HR/org-admin (`attendance.adjustment.approve`) decides it, atomically, with terminal-state protection against double-decision.

**Self-service surface:** an ESS "My Attendance" tab — clock controls, a 7-day recent summary, recent raw events, and a correction-request form — independently module-gated exactly like My Leave and Internal Vacancies.

**Internal management surfaces:** an Attendance Register (paginated, filterable, own/team/organization-wide scoped), an Attendance Dashboard (a same-day, zero-filled status tile breakdown), and four CSV-exportable Attendance Reports (`attendance_daily_register`, `attendance_monthly_summary`, `attendance_late_arrivals`, `attendance_absenteeism`) — all pure read-side aggregation over the identical W66 read-model, all sharing the identical own/team/organization-wide visibility tiers.

### Frozen-Plan Reconciliation

Interpretations and reconciliations made during implementation, each already individually documented in this file's own W64–W70 header paragraphs, consolidated here:

- **Repeated clock-ins are permitted, not rejected** — Open Decision 1's own recommended default; a second clock-in with no intervening clock-out returns `201`, not `400`. Accidental rapid double-taps are treated as a client UX concern, not a server-integrity concern.
- **First-in/last-out summary math** — no multi-segment (e.g. lunch-break) net-duration calculation; the schema supports it without change if ever needed later, but W64–W70 never required it.
- **An incomplete clock pair reads as `partial`** — §3.3 lists `partial` in the status enum with no definition; this is the interpretation used throughout, live-verified end to end in W71 (clock-in alone → `partial`, clock-out completes it → `present`/`late`).
- **`excuse_absence` maps to `on_leave`** — no literal 8th status exists among the 7 frozen values; the closest existing authorized-absence status was reused rather than inventing a new one.
- **A missing organization timezone is a hard `409`, never a silent UTC fallback** — Open Decision 2's own recommended default, to avoid systematically wrong "late" calculations for any non-UTC organization.
- **HR direct entry is a `attendance_adjustments` row (auto-approved), not a raw `attendance_events` row with `source: "hr_manual"`** — W65's own concrete "Backend/API impact" line assigns this the concrete route it actually got; `source: "hr_manual"` remains schema-valid but structurally unreached through any built route, same as `biometric`/`import`.
- **Correction-request approval is organization-wide only, no manager/delegated tier** — Open Decision 3's own recommended default for W67; a manager-tier extension was never built or approved.
- **No employee correction-request history/list UI or endpoint** — the frozen plan's own W67 "Backend/API impact" line is an exhaustive three-route list that never included a GET/list route; a submitted request is confirmed via its own create response, not a persisted-and-rendered history.
- **No Attendance-rate or lateness-rate metric anywhere (dashboard or reports)** — the frozen W70 scope line defines only a raw-count tile breakdown with no denominator or exclusion rule specified anywhere in the frozen document; inventing one would have violated the "do not invent executive KPIs" discipline this session was held to. If ever wanted, it needs its own frozen definition first.
- **No new charting library** — Recruitment's own dashboard precedent (plain Card/Badge/Table) was reused; nothing in W70 genuinely required a chart.
- **`/attendance/dashboard` + `/attendance-reports` as dedicated routes**, not folded into the existing `GET /dashboard/summary` widget — Open Decision 4's own recommended default, given Attendance's comparable operational scope to Recruitment's own W61 precedent.
- **The four W70 report keys** (`attendance_daily_register`, `attendance_monthly_summary`, `attendance_late_arrivals`, `attendance_absenteeism`) were selected from §7's own explicitly non-binding candidate list, at W70's own start, per that section's own "this plan's W70's own decision to make" framing.
- **W69's status filter applies after employee-level pagination** — status is a computed field, not a stored column; `total` reflects the pre-status-filter employee-scope count. Documented as a deliberate, transparent tradeoff for a first register cut, not silently glossed over, and never silently changed by W70's own reporting work (which computes its own, separately correct, full-range totals for each report instead).
- **No biometric device implementation** — `attendance_events.source` includes `biometric` as a schema-valid, reserved-only value (§3.6's own "boundary-only" framing, Open Decision 5's own recommended default); no device protocol exists to implement against yet.
- **No payroll integration, overtime pay, shift scheduling/rostering, GPS/geofencing, mobile app, offline sync, advanced timesheets, project time tracking, or performance-penalty logic** — all explicitly listed in §9's own "Explicit Exclusions (this phase does not build)," never attempted.
- **No persisted daily-summary table** — §3.3's own explicit "a computed read-model, not stored" instruction; every dashboard/report figure is computed live per request, exactly like Recruitment's own W61 reporting discipline.
- **No persisted reporting/analytics table** — same discipline, extended to W70; `getAttendanceRegisterForEmployees` is the single shared computation both the Register and every Dashboard/Report figure route through.
- **Cross-midnight/overnight shifts remain explicitly out of scope for W64–W70**, per §2's own framing — a clock-out is always paired with clock-in events sharing its own civil date; night-shift support is a later, separately-scoped extension if ever approved.

None of the above are presented as defects — every one is either the frozen plan's own explicit exclusion, an Open Decision's own recommended default actually taken, or a reconciliation between the frozen document's general prose and its own more specific, controlling statement (per the "the frozen plan's most specific statement controls" discipline established from W65 onward).

### Database

**Migrations `0036`–`0037`, both applied to development, both purely additive with a hand-authored `.down.sql`:**
- `0036_enable_rls_deny_default.sql` — enables RLS with zero policies on all (then-)68 public tables (Supabase Data API remediation, not Attendance-specific, but the security foundation every Attendance table was subsequently built on top of).
- `0037_redundant_rictor.sql` — `attendance_events`, `attendance_adjustments` (W64), RLS-enabled with zero policies from the same migration, matching `0036`'s own precedent.

**Current migration status (W71/W72 re-confirmed):** journal `0000`–`0037`, 38 sequential entries, no gaps or duplicates; every migration has a matching `.down.sql`; the development database's own `drizzle.__drizzle_migrations` table confirms row id 38 / journal index 37 (`0037_redundant_rictor`) as the latest applied migration, matching the source tree exactly; `drizzle-kit generate` reports "No schema changes, nothing to migrate" — zero drift. **No migration was applied to production** — production remains on whatever migration state it was at before this entire Phase 3B effort began; production rollout (including `0036`/`0037`) requires its own separate, explicit approval and preflight, per this session's own repeated instruction.

**Total public tables: 70** (68 pre-`0036` + the 2 Attendance tables from `0037`) — **all 70 RLS-enabled, 0 RLS-disabled, 0 permissive policies anywhere in `public`.**

### APIs

**9 endpoints across 5 route files**, all registered exactly once, no duplicates (verified by direct route-file inspection in W71):

| Route | Method | Permission |
|---|---|---|
| `.../attendance-events` | POST | `attendance.clock.own` |
| `.../attendance-events` | GET | `attendance.read.own` |
| `.../attendance-adjustments` | POST | `attendance.read.own` (branches internally on `attendance.manage`) |
| `.../attendance-adjustments/:id/approve` | POST | `attendance.adjustment.approve` |
| `.../attendance-adjustments/:id/reject` | POST | `attendance.adjustment.approve` |
| `.../employees/:employeeId/attendance/summary` | GET | `attendance.read.own` |
| `.../attendance` (Register) | GET | `attendance.read.own` |
| `.../attendance/dashboard` | GET | `attendance.read.own` |
| `.../attendance/reports/:reportKey` | GET | `attendance.read.own` |

Every one of the 9 handlers composes the identical `requireAuth` → `requireMembership("organizationId")` → `requireModuleEnabled("attendance")` → `requirePermission(...)` chain — confirmed by direct grep across all 5 route files in W71, not assumed. **OpenAPI:** 9 matching paths under the `attendance-events`/`attendance-adjustments`/`attendance-daily-summary`/`attendance-register`/`attendance-reporting` tags. **Generated client:** 9 matching hooks (`useRecordAttendanceEvent`, `useListAttendanceEvents`, `useRecordAttendanceAdjustment`, `useApproveAttendanceAdjustment`, `useRejectAttendanceAdjustment`, `useGetAttendanceDailySummary`, `useListAttendanceRegister`, `useGetAttendanceDashboard`, `useRunAttendanceReport`) — confirmed present, zero missing, zero extra. `orval codegen` produces byte-identical output across two consecutive runs, and is identical to the already-checked-in generated files (zero drift from `openapi.yaml`, re-confirmed in W71).

### Permissions

Four permissions, seeded exactly per the frozen plan's own §4 matrix, unchanged since W64 — **no permission was ever added by W65–W70**:

- **`attendance.clock.own`** — self clock-in/out. Seeded to `org_admin`/`hr_manager`/`employee` (every role).
- **`attendance.read.own`** — the coarse route-level gate for every read surface (own/team-scoped register, dashboard, reports, summary) and the shared adjustments-creation route. Seeded to `org_admin`/`hr_manager`/`employee` (every role).
- **`attendance.manage`** — organization-wide reach signal: HR direct-entry, organization-wide register/dashboard/reports. Seeded to `org_admin`/`hr_manager` only.
- **`attendance.adjustment.approve`** — decide a pending correction request. Seeded to `org_admin`/`hr_manager` only.

**No `attendance.read.team` or any manager-tier permission exists** — the "team" visibility tier (direct reports) is resolved entirely in the service layer via `employees.reportingManagerId`, the same mechanism `leaveCalendar.ts` already established for Leave — never a permission grant, exactly per §4's own explicit design.

### Module Gating

`attendance` — seeded in the Foundation's Module Registry as `status: "hidden"` until W64 flipped it to `active`; no organization is enabled by module availability alone (WWM and Acme both confirmed disabled at every checkpoint throughout W64–W71 except during each workstream's own bracketed, restored-afterward live QA window). Every one of the 9 routes composes `requireModuleEnabled("attendance")`, always paired with a specific `requirePermission` call — module gating is never a substitute for authorization, confirmed by direct inspection. Every frontend route wraps in `<ModuleGate moduleKey="attendance">`; ESS's "My Attendance" tab checks `attendance` accessibility independently within the ESS page (gated only by `employee_self_service` itself), degrading to a controlled message rather than a broken page — the identical pattern My Leave (W39) and Internal Vacancies (W60) already established.

### Authorization Matrix

- **Employee:** own clock-in/out, own summary read, own correction request. Cannot approve anything; cannot reach the organization-wide Register/Dashboard/Reports (own/team scope only, resolved server-side); cannot target another employee (server-derived identity on every write).
- **Manager** (a plain `employee`-role holder who happens to be someone's `reportingManagerId`): own + direct-report visibility on the Register/Dashboard/Reports — no organization-wide fallback, live-verified repeatedly including a zero-direct-reports case (valid empty scope, never a fallback to organization-wide); filters can only narrow this scope, never broaden it (live-verified with an out-of-scope `employeeId` filter); cannot use any `attendance.manage`-gated functionality (HR direct entry, organization-wide register) unless independently granted that permission; cannot approve/reject unless independently granted `attendance.adjustment.approve`.
- **HR Manager / Org Admin:** organization-wide read/manage per the seeded matrix above — HR direct entry, adjustment approval, organization-wide Register/Dashboard/Reports, all live-verified.
- **Super Admin:** inherits `org_admin`-equivalent Attendance reach via the same seeded permission matrix; the platform's own established hostname-binding behavior (from the tenant-domain work preceding Phase 3B) is untouched by anything in W64–W70 — no Attendance route implements custom hostname logic of its own, so super_admin is neither granted nor denied anything beyond what the shared `requireMembership` tenant-consistency guard already enforces platform-wide.
- **Module disabled:** denied independently of role or permission — confirmed live (a valid HR/org-admin token against a module-disabled organization still gets `403`).
- **No membership:** denied — confirmed live repeatedly (WWM↔Acme cross-org tokens, unauthenticated requests).

### Attendance Rules (final, as implemented)

- **Clock timestamps are always server-authoritative** — `occurredAt` is the server's own clock, never a client-supplied value, on every self-service clock event.
- **Civil dates are always derived server-side** from the organization's configured `general.timezone` via Node's `Intl.DateTimeFormat` (real IANA conversion, inherently DST-correct) — never client/browser-local, confirmed specifically with a non-UTC timezone (Asia/Tokyo) during W66's own live QA.
- **A missing organization timezone is a hard `409`** on every data-bearing Attendance route (summary, register, dashboard, reports) — never a silent UTC fallback.
- **Repeated clock-ins are permitted** — no sequencing rejection.
- **First-clock-in/last-clock-out** is the summary's own worked-time math; no multi-segment net-duration calculation exists.
- **An incomplete clock pair → `partial`**; a complete pair → `present` (on time) or `late` (past `workStartTime` + `gracePeriodMinutes`); no events at all on a configured work day → `absent`, unless the employee's *current* `employmentStatus` is `on_leave`/`suspended`, in which case it's suppressed to `null` instead.
- **Approved Leave → `on_leave`**, beating an otherwise-`absent` day; a merely-*pending* Leave request has zero effect.
- **A Public Holiday → `holiday`**; a non-configured work day (per §38's own `workDays` setting) → `non_working_day`; both beat event-derived statuses.
- **An approved whole-day adjustment** (`mark_present`/`mark_absent`/`excuse_absence`) overrides the event-derived status for that date; a *pending* or *rejected* adjustment has zero effect, live-re-verified in W71.
- **An approved `manual_clock_in`/`manual_clock_out` adjustment** overrides only the matching raw event's contribution to the pair, not the whole day.
- **Pre-`hireDate` or on/after-`separationDate` → `null`** (no summary generated at all), per §3.7's own literal wording.
- **No client ever computes a status, a rate, or a precedence decision** — every one of the 4 frontend surfaces (ESS, Register, Dashboard, Reports) renders server-computed DTOs only; confirmed by a repo-wide grep for any client-side date/status-computation logic in W71 (only the W38 Attendance Settings config-editing page references these field names at all, and only to edit the configuration values themselves, never to compute a status).

### Reporting

Four report keys (`attendance_daily_register`, `attendance_monthly_summary`, `attendance_late_arrivals`, `attendance_absenteeism`), registered in the shared Reporting Foundation registry (W17/ADR-016, `category: "attendance"`) for catalog discoverability via the existing `GET /reports`, but executed through a **dedicated, visibility-scoped route** (`GET .../attendance/reports/:reportKey`), never the generic `GET .../reports/:reportKey/run` — that route's own RUNNERS map has no entries for these keys and safely 404s, exactly mirroring how Recruitment's own 9 report keys (W61) are isolated from the generic runner. Own/team/organization-wide visibility is identical to the Register/Dashboard. Date ranges are always organization-local (never browser-local), reusing the Register's own 100-day-max/no-inverted-range validation, defaulting to organization-local "today" on both ends when omitted. CSV export reuses the exact `?format=csv` convention Recruitment's own reports already established — export scope is always identical to the JSON response's own already-authorized scope, with no separate export permission or code path; a cross-tenant export is structurally impossible (organizationId is resolved from the URL/membership, never the query string). No separate analytics store or second report registry was created anywhere.

### Frontend Surfaces

- **My Attendance (ESS tab)** — clock controls, 7-day recent summary, recent raw events, a correction-request form. Independent module-degradation (My Leave/Internal Vacancies precedent).
- **Attendance Register** (`/attendance-register`) — paginated, filterable internal list, HR-capable-role-gated nav entry, own/team tier still reachable directly by URL.
- **Attendance Dashboard** (`/attendance-dashboard`) — status tiles for a single organization-local date; the date input always displays the backend's own resolved date, never a browser-computed guess.
- **Attendance Reports** (`/attendance-reports`) — report selector, date-range inputs, CSV download; mirrors `recruitment-reports.tsx`'s own established structure exactly.

All four: `<ModuleGate moduleKey="attendance">` (or independent in-page checks for the ESS tab), standard loading/error/empty/`403`/`409` states, existing responsive table/card patterns (no HRMS shell redesign), no new charting dependency, zero direct Supabase client access anywhere in `artifacts/hrms/src` (repo-wide grep, zero matches).

**Browser QA limitation, stated plainly and consistently across every one of W64–W71:** interactive browser automation (`chromium-cli`/Playwright) was never available on this Windows host, at any point in Phase 3B. Frontend correctness was verified via the passing React Testing Library component suite (real rendering, real user-event interaction, mocked network) plus live HTTP/API QA against the real development database confirming the exact endpoints/payloads each surface actually depends on, plus all three production builds succeeding. This is stated here explicitly, not glossed over — no interactive browser walkthrough is claimed for any Phase 3B workstream.

- **Lint: NOT AVAILABLE** — pre-existing repository tooling gap (no `eslint.config.js`/`lint` script in this repo; first surfaced during W69, unchanged throughout W70–W72, never claimed as passing).
