Acme cross-tenant checks (WWM→Acme and Acme→WWM denied on the Register/Dashboard; a cross-org adjustment ID and a cross-org employee ID both structurally unreachable through the other tenant's own org URL; a missing-timezone `409` independently confirmed on Acme) all passed. **Cleanup:** every disposable QA row deleted; both organizations' `organization_settings` and Attendance module-enablement restored to their exact confirmed prior state (empty/disabled) by direct re-query; zero stray organizations found.

### Security Verification

- **Tenant resolver fail-closed** — unchanged since the pre-Phase-3B tenant-domain work; no Attendance route weakens or bypasses it.
- **Hostname consistency / organization-route hostname binding** — unchanged since the `773bdb5`/`4685832` fixes; no Attendance route implements a hostname check of its own, so none can regress independently of the shared guard.
- **Membership checks** — `requireMembership("organizationId")` on all 9 routes; cross-org access denied `403`, re-confirmed live throughout W64–W71.
- **Module gating never substitutes for permission checks** — every route pairs `requireModuleEnabled` with a specific `requirePermission`, confirmed by direct grep (not assumption) in W71.
- **RLS:** all 70 public tables RLS-enabled, 0 disabled, 0 policies anywhere — deny-by-default has no exceptions.
- **PostgREST:** a direct anon-key request against `attendance_events`/`attendance_adjustments` returns `200 []` — RLS silently denies, the same behavior the whole platform already relies on for every other table.
- **Frontend data path:** zero direct Supabase client usage anywhere in `artifacts/hrms/src` — every request goes through the generated API client against the trusted server, which alone holds `BYPASSRLS`.
- **Development session rotation:** completed once, immediately after `0036`'s RLS remediation (documented in this file's own tenant-security entry, `4685832`) — all pre-remediation sessions invalidated, fresh login/membership/tenant-resolution re-confirmed functional; no further rotation was needed or performed during Phase 3B itself.
- **WWM/Acme isolation:** verified live, both directions, in every one of W64–W71's own QA passes — event capture, daily summary, adjustments, approval, ESS-related APIs, Register, Dashboard, Reports, CSV export, employee filters, holiday influence, and Leave influence all independently confirmed tenant-isolated at least once across the phase.
- **Production security rollout remains separately gated** — nothing in Phase 3B assessed, touched, or altered production's security posture in any way; migrations `0036`/`0037` were applied to development only.

**This is a code-level and automated-test-suite verification, plus disposable-account live QA against a real development database — not a penetration test or a production security certification.**

### Integrations

- **W38 (Organization Configuration):** `general.timezone` and the `attendance` namespace (`workStartTime`/`workEndTime`/`gracePeriodMinutes`/`workDays`) are reused unchanged, via the same `getNamespaceConfig`/`organization_settings` engine every other namespace already uses — no duplicate settings architecture was created.
- **Leave:** approved requests only ever produce `on_leave`; pending/rejected requests have zero effect; Attendance never writes to `leave_requests` or any Leave table.
- **Public Holidays:** organization/date-scoped, reused read-only via the existing holiday-occurrence resolution; no duplicated holiday model.
- **Employees:** identity resolution reuses W33's `resolveOwnEmployeeId`/`employee_user_links` (never a second "which employee is me" mechanism); `reportingManagerId` is the sole "team" resolution mechanism; hire-date/separation-date/employment-status eligibility rules are enforced exactly per §3.7.
- **ESS:** My Leave and Internal Vacancies/My Applications tabs are unaffected — confirmed by the full, unchanged frontend suite passing alongside the new Attendance tests throughout W68–W71.
- **Reporting Framework (W17/ADR-016):** the shared `reports` registry is reused for catalog discoverability; no second report registry exists anywhere.
- **Tenant Infrastructure:** shared-mode compatible by construction (every route scopes by `organizationId` resolved from membership); dedicated-mode deployments run the identical, unmodified Attendance code path — nothing in W64–W70 introduces a mode-specific branch.

### Known Non-Blocking Items

1. **No repository-local ESLint configuration/script** — tooling gap, pre-existing (predates Phase 3B, first surfaced and documented during W69), not fixed here per this workstream's own explicit instruction not to build lint infrastructure as a side effect of a documentation-only report.
2. **No browser automation tooling (Playwright/`chromium-cli`) available on this host** — environment limitation, stated explicitly and consistently across every one of W64–W71 rather than glossed over; RTL + live HTTP QA substituted throughout.
3. **W69's Register status filter applies after employee-level pagination** (`total` reflects the pre-status-filter employee-scope count) — implementation choice, documented at the time as a deliberate, transparent tradeoff for a first register cut; W70's own reports compute correct full-range totals independently rather than inheriting this tradeoff.
4. **~10 lines of scope-resolution logic duplicated between `attendanceRegister.ts` (W69) and `attendanceReporting.ts` (W70)** — implementation choice, deliberately made to guarantee W70 could never risk altering W69's already-shipped, already-tested behavior.
5. **No employee correction-request history/list UI or endpoint** — frozen-scope deferral; the frozen plan's own W67 API list never included one.
6. **No biometric device integration** — frozen-scope deferral (§3.6/§9); `source: "biometric"` remains schema-valid and reserved-only.
7. **No payroll integration, overtime pay, or performance-penalty logic** — frozen-scope deferral (§9), an explicit phase exclusion, not a gap.
8. **No shift scheduling/rostering or cross-midnight/overnight-shift model** — frozen-scope deferral (§2/§9); the schema supports either without a breaking change if ever approved later.
9. **No persisted daily-summary table** — deliberate architectural choice (§3.3's own explicit instruction), not a limitation to be "fixed."
10. **No persisted reporting/analytics table or caching layer** — deliberate architectural choice, matching Recruitment's own W61 discipline exactly.
11. **No Attendance-rate or lateness-rate metric** — frozen-scope gap (undefined denominator anywhere in the frozen document); a future enhancement if a product decision defines one, not built speculatively here.
12. **No mobile app, offline sync, advanced timesheets, project time tracking, or GPS/geofencing** — frozen-scope deferral (§9).

None of the above are classified as defects or blockers — items 1–2 are tooling/environment gaps outside this phase's control; items 3–4 are deliberate implementation choices, each documented at the time it was made; items 5–12 are explicit frozen-plan scope exclusions the document itself never assigned to Phase 3B.

### Technical Debt

None beyond the twelve known non-blocking items above, all of which are either pre-existing tooling/environment gaps or deliberate, already-approved scope exclusions — no workaround, shortcut, or quality compromise was introduced anywhere in W64–W72.

## Phase 3A Progress

Authoritative, frozen plan: see `docs/PHASE_3A_RECRUITMENT_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (21 dependency-ordered workstreams, W43–W63).

- **W61 — Recruitment Dashboard & Reporting — Complete.** Read-only metrics computed live over W43–W60's existing tables — no cache table, no stored aggregate, no recruitment-state mutation anywhere in this workstream (verified directly: `lib/recruitmentReporting.ts` contains zero `insert`/`update`/`delete` calls). **Dashboard** (`GET .../organizations/:organizationId/recruitment/dashboard`): six real-time tiles — `openRequisitionsCount` (status = `approved`, the only status this codebase's pipeline ever actually reaches for an actively-recruiting requisition — `partially_filled`/`filled` are schema-valid but no route anywhere sets them, a pre-existing gap this workstream doesn't fix, so "filled" metrics are honestly empty rather than fabricated), `openVacanciesCount` (status = `published`), `totalApplicantsCount`, `candidatesByStage` (a stage-category breakdown, a never-triaged application reading as "Applied (unstaged)" per `applicationPipeline.ts`'s own display-only convention), `recruiterWorkload`/`hiringManagerWorkload` (open-requisition counts grouped by `recruiterEmployeeId`/`hiringManagerEmployeeId`, resolved to staff names). **Reports** (`GET .../organizations/:organizationId/recruitment/reports/:reportKey`): nine keys registered as ordinary rows in W17's existing `reports` registry (category `"recruitment"`, so they appear in the existing `GET /reports` catalog) but executed only through this workstream's own dedicated, visibility-scoped route — the generic `GET .../reports/:reportKey/run` (lib/reporting.ts) has no runner entries for these keys and safely 404s "Unknown report" if ever hit directly, since it has no concept of the assigned-vs-organization-wide tier these reports require. The nine: `recruitment_applicants_by_vacancy`, `recruitment_applicants_by_source`, `recruitment_time_to_fill` (requisition-approval `decidedAt` → `updatedAt` on a `filled`-status requisition — honestly empty today, same pipeline gap as above), `recruitment_time_to_hire` (`applications.submittedAt` → the latest `application_stage_history` row moving into a hired-category stage, averaged per vacancy), `recruitment_age_in_stage` (now − the application's most recent stage-history entry, or `submittedAt` if never moved, averaged per non-terminal stage), `recruitment_conversion_funnel` (applied→screening→interview→assessment→offer→hired sequential reach counts plus separate rejected/withdrawn outcome counts), `recruitment_interview_to_offer_ratio`, `recruitment_offer_acceptance_rate` (`offer_versions.status` `accepted`/`declined` — schema-valid values no route in this codebase ever sets per W57's own documented scope boundary, so this is honestly null, not zero, until that flow exists), `recruitment_rejection_reasons`. **Null-vs-zero discipline enforced throughout:** every ratio/average returns `null` (never a fabricated `0`) when its denominator is zero or no rows exist to average — verified directly for the interview-to-offer ratio in both the zero-data and real-data cases. **Explicitly excluded:** §18's "vacancy performance (views/applicants)" — the views half requires a new view-count column on `vacancies` (a schema change), which contradicts W61's own frozen `Database impact: none` line; the applicants half is already covered by `recruitment_applicants_by_vacancy`. This is the same "frozen schema wins over speculative prose" discipline W44/W47/W58 already established, not an oversight. **Permissions — the single frozen key, nothing else:** `recruitment.reports.read`, seeded to org_admin, hr_manager, **and** employee — the same broad-rollout-plus-service-layer-narrowing shape every other Recruitment *read* permission this phase already uses (`requisition.read`, `vacancy.read`, `application.read`, `candidate.read`, `interview.read`, `offer.read`), since any employee could be the assigned recruiter/hiring manager on some requisition. **Visibility, resolved in `lib/recruitmentReporting.ts`, never by the permission grant alone:** organization-wide reach is signaled by holding `requisition.update` — reused, not a new permission, the exact same org_admin/hr_manager-only signal `jobRequisitions.ts` already established for this purpose; everyone else is scoped to `job_requisitions` rows where `recruiterEmployeeId` or `hiringManagerEmployeeId` matches their own linked employee id, then vacancies/applications/history/interviews/offers are all resolved through that requisition scope — verified directly that an assigned recruiter with no `requisition.update` sees only their own workload and never another recruiter's, and that a caller with `recruitment.reports.read` but no linked employee and no organization-wide reach gets a real empty scope, never an error or another caller's data. **Privacy:** no candidate name, email, phone, national identifier, referee contact, background-check result, recruiter note, interview/scorecard comment, or document storage key is read or returned anywhere in this workstream — every metric aggregates to vacancy/requisition/stage/reason-code granularity; the only names ever returned are recruiting *staff* (via `employeesTable`, for workload attribution), never a candidate. **A real, non-obvious frontend bug found and fixed during this session's own verification, before commit:** the Recruitment Reports page's original "auto-select the first report" logic compared the freshly-`.filter()`-ed `reports` array against a `useState`-held "previous" copy by reference to detect when to pick a default — but since `.filter()` returns a new array every render, and the initial "previous" value was captured from that very same first render, the two were reference-different-yet-conceptually-identical, and the comparison could never detect the very first arrival of real data when it happened to be available synchronously (exactly what the test harness surfaced, and a case a real cached/pre-populated React Query response could hit too). Fixed by deriving the effective `reportKey` during render (`reports.some(...) ? selectedReportKey : reports[0]?.key ?? ''`) instead of syncing state from an effect — also resolved a `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint error an earlier effect-based fix attempt introduced. **API/OpenAPI:** new `recruitment-reporting` tag; `RecruitmentDashboard`/`RecruitmentStageBreakdownItem`/`RecruitmentWorkloadItem` schemas; the report-run response reuses W17's existing `ReportRunResult`/`ReportColumn` schemas unchanged (same `{key, label, description, generatedAt, columns, rows}` shape, `?format=csv` convention, and `text/csv` content-type) — codegen verified zero-diff across two consecutive runs. **Frontend:** `/recruitment` (dashboard: tiles, stage-breakdown badges, two workload tables) and `/recruitment-reports` (catalog dropdown reused from the existing `reports` registry filtered to `category === "recruitment"`, result table, CSV download) — both `<ModuleGate moduleKey="recruitment">`-wrapped per §8; nav entries gated `isHrCapable`-only, the same precedent every other Recruitment nav entry (`/offers`, `/interviews`, ...) already established — an assigned recruiter/hiring manager without an HR-capable role can still reach both pages directly by URL. Both pages render a distinct "Access denied" state for a `403` (duck-typed via the thrown `ApiError`'s `status` field, since `ApiError` itself isn't part of the generated client's public exports) separately from a generic error state, plus loading and empty states — real zeros/an explicit "no data yet" message, never a crash, for an organization with the module enabled but nothing to report yet. **Audit/notifications:** none — a pure read path, nothing here changes state or is itself an event worth recording, and neither is named anywhere in W61's frozen scope. **Database impact: none** — confirmed via `drizzle-kit generate` reporting "No schema changes, nothing to migrate"; no migration file exists for this workstream. Backend: 17 new tests (`recruitmentReporting.test.ts`) covering unauthenticated/permission-denied/module-disabled statuses, organization-wide vs. assigned-vs.-empty visibility on the dashboard, stage-breakdown bucketing of a never-triaged application, cross-registry isolation (a non-recruitment report key 404s through this route), null-vs-real-percentage for the interview-to-offer ratio, the honestly-empty time-to-fill report, CSV content-type, and assigned-recruiter cross-recruiter isolation on a report (not just the dashboard). Frontend: 13 new tests (7 `recruitment-dashboard.test.tsx`, 6 `recruitment-reports.test.tsx`) covering loading/denied/error/empty/populated dashboard states and catalog-filtering/denied/error/empty/populated-with-a-null-cell/CSV-button-enabled report states. Full suite: 706 backend + 234 frontend tests pass, typecheck and lint clean, OpenAPI codegen zero-diff, zero migration drift, production builds (`api-server`, `hrms`, `mockup-sandbox`) all succeed. No live browser walkthrough was performed — this environment has no provisioned dev database, the same limitation every prior Recruitment workstream in this session has had; verification relied on the automated test suites, typecheck, lint, `drizzle-kit generate`, codegen, and production builds only. Does not implement vacancy view tracking, a diversity/EEO breakdown, PDF export, scheduled/emailed reports, or any change to existing recruitment records — all remain out of this workstream's frozen scope or explicitly excluded by §21/§25.

- **W60 — Employee Self-Service Internal Applications — Complete.** Lets an authenticated employee view internal vacancies and apply to them from inside ESS — `GET /me/internal-vacancies`, `POST /me/internal-vacancies/:publicId/apply`, `GET /me/applications` (§10's own exact three lines; no vacancy-detail or withdraw route exists in the frozen API — see scope notes below). **Database impact is genuinely zero**, exactly as the frozen plan's own scope line states — reuses `candidates`/`applications`/`vacancies`/`employee_user_links` unchanged, no migration generated. **Identity is always derived server-side**, the same `resolveOwnEmployeeId` (W14/W33) chain every other ESS/Recruitment-assigned-tier resolver in this codebase already reuses — no route accepts a client-supplied employeeId/candidateId. **A reused, not duplicated, employee-creation-service-style extraction:** `findOrCreateCandidate` (private in `candidateApplications.ts`, W49) was exported unchanged for reuse here — the exact same "extract rather than reimplement" discipline W59 already established for `createEmployee`. **Candidate resolution/linkage — the one genuinely new piece of logic this workstream adds:** an applying employee's `candidates` row is resolved in priority order — (1) an existing row already linked via `candidates.linkedInternalEmployeeId` (e.g. this employee was originally hired through external recruitment and their original candidate record survives, per ADR "never hard-delete"); else (2) `findOrCreateCandidate` resolved by the employee's own email (`workEmail ?? personalEmail`; a missing email on both is a clear 400, not an invented placeholder), then permanently linked by setting `linkedInternalEmployeeId` — but only if it isn't already set on some *other* employee (a defensive no-op that never overwrites another employee's provenance or any other field on an existing candidate row). **W59's conversion is never invoked** — an internal application does not change the employee record or create a second one; verified by a dedicated test. **Vacancy eligibility** mirrors `publicCareers.ts`'s own `isVacancyPubliclyEligible` predicate exactly, with the visibility condition inverted (`external`-only excluded instead of `internal`-only) — published status, openDate/closeDate window, per W47's frozen model; only an `active` employee may browse or apply (the literal, narrowest reading of "the employee is active" — no frozen rule distinguishes probation/on_leave/suspended, so that reading was chosen and documented as a judgment call). **Two read endpoints return a controlled `{linked, active, items}` 200-OK state** for an unlinked or non-active caller — mirroring `GET /me/employee`'s own established `linked: false` precedent exactly, per this workstream's own explicit instruction — never a 403/error for a passive view; the one write action (`apply`) still 403s for the same conditions, since a blocked write is conventionally an error, not a 200-with-flag response. **Submission** reuses the `applications` table's own `(candidateId, vacancyId)` unique constraint for duplicate/idempotent handling (mirrors `submitPublicApplication`'s "return the existing row, no second write" rule exactly) — enters W51's existing pipeline unchanged, `source` marked `"internal_ess"` (the table's own free-text column, same convention as `candidate_documents.categoryCode`) so recruiters can distinguish it, but no second pipeline exists. Screening answers reuse W52's existing optional `application_answers` capture unchanged (a vacancy's questions apply uniformly regardless of applicant source); no resume upload is required and no `candidate_consents` row is written — neither is named anywhere in W60's frozen scope, and an employee already has an existing employment relationship distinct from an anonymous external applicant's need for explicit consent capture. **Permissions — none added or changed:** every route is gated purely by `employee_self_service` AND `recruitment` module enablement (checked independently, §8 — a disabled `recruitment` module degrades only the two new ESS tab sections, never blocks the rest of the page) plus self-scoped identity resolution; no `requirePermission` call exists on any of the three routes, mirroring `GET /me/employee`'s own permission-key-independent design exactly. Ordinary employees receive no new or broadened recruitment permission — `seed-roles-permissions.ts` was not touched by this workstream at all. **Scope boundaries — deliberately not built, since §10's frozen API line names only three routes:** no `GET /me/internal-vacancies/:publicId` detail route (the list response already carries every field a detail view needs — job description, responsibilities, requirements, screening questions — so "detail" is a client-side selection from the already-fetched list, never a second fetch) and no withdraw route for internal applications (no such route is named anywhere in §10, and the existing internal `POST .../applications/:id/withdraw` from W51 is gated behind assigned-recruiter/hiring-manager/org-wide permissions an ordinary applicant could never hold). **API:** new `InternalVacancySummary`/`InternalVacancyQuestion`/`InternalVacanciesResponse`/`SubmitInternalApplicationInput`/`MyInternalApplicationSubmitResult`/`MyInternalApplicationSummary`/`MyInternalApplicationsResponse` OpenAPI schemas under the existing `self-service` tag (reused, not a new tag), codegen verified zero-diff across two consecutive runs. **Frontend:** two new tabs on the existing `/self-service` page — "Internal Vacancies" (card grid, a detail-and-apply dialog with inline screening-question inputs where a vacancy has any) and "My Applications" (status-badged list) — reusing the exact same independent-module-degradation pattern (`isModuleAccessible(modules, 'recruitment')`) W39's own "My Leave" tab already established for the `leave` module; the existing My Profile/My Leave/My Documents tabs are untouched and remain fully functional. **Audit:** `internal_application.submitted` only (on a genuinely new submission, never on an idempotent repeat) — no per-vacancy-view audit event exists, since none is required by the frozen plan. **No notifications** — none are explicitly assigned to W60. **Database impact:** none — no migration file, no schema change, confirmed via a clean `pnpm run typecheck`/build with zero drizzle-kit drift. Backend: 25 new tests (`meInternalApplications.test.ts`) covering unauthenticated/module-disabled 401/403s, the unlinked and non-active controlled states on both read endpoints, internal-vacancy eligibility (published/visibility/open-close-window, including an explicit external-only-exclusion test and a "both" visibility inclusion test), the full candidate-resolution-and-link flow (including reusing a pre-existing `linkedInternalEmployeeId`-linked candidate), idempotent repeat submission, optional screening-answer capture with an out-of-vacancy answer silently ignored, the no-employee-record-mutation/no-conversion-invoked proof, cross-employee application-list isolation, and restricted-DTO-field assertions. Frontend: 8 new tests added to `employee-self-service.test.tsx` covering the two new tabs' rendering, the disabled-recruitment-module degrade, the not-eligible controlled state, empty states, the list-to-apply-dialog flow with a no-recruiter-controls assertion, application submission, and My Applications rendering — all pre-existing ESS tests continue to pass unchanged. Full suite: 689 backend + 221 frontend tests pass. No live browser walkthrough was performed — this environment has no provisioned dev database, the same limitation every prior Recruitment workstream in this session has had; verification relied on the automated test suites, typecheck, lint, and production build only. Does not implement an internal-vacancy detail route, internal-application withdrawal, resume/document upload for internal applications, consent capture, notifications, or any change to the existing anonymous public careers flow (verified untouched) — all remain out of this workstream's frozen scope or later workstreams' concern entirely.

- **W59 — Employee Conversion — Complete.** The controlled hand-off from Recruitment to HR: a new `candidate_employee_links` provenance table plus a `convert-to-employee` transaction. **Core principle enforced structurally, not just by convention:** Recruitment selects a candidate; this workstream creates an employee — those stayed two different responsibilities in two different files, and there remains exactly ONE authoritative employee-creation pathway in the system. **A real gap found before this workstream could even begin:** the frozen plan's own §13 text assumes a reusable `createEmployee` **service** exists ("reuses the existing `createEmployee` service... same service `POST /organizations/:id/employees` already uses"), but the actual codebase had that exact logic (reference validation, employee-number generation, the insert itself) written *inline* inside `routes/employees.ts`'s POST handler, not extracted anywhere reusable. Building convert-to-employee without first fixing this would have meant either duplicating that logic (explicitly forbidden by this workstream's own instructions) or reaching into a route handler from a lib file (an architectural inversion no other workstream this phase does). Resolved by extracting the exact existing logic, unchanged, into a new `createEmployee(client, params)` export in `lib/employees.ts` — accepting a `QueryClient` (the same `db`-or-`tx` pattern `leaveBalances.ts`/`offers.ts` already established) so it can run inside convert-to-employee's own transaction — then updating `routes/employees.ts`'s POST handler to call it instead of inlining the insert. Confirmed behavior-preserving: all 30 pre-existing `employees.test.ts` tests pass unchanged against the refactored route. **Eligibility is exactly §13's own two-check formula, nothing more:** the application must be in a `hired`-category stage (§4.3, resolved via `recruitment_stages.category`, never an org-configurable stage *name*) AND every non-waived `pre_employment_requirements` row must be `satisfied` (reuses the identical `readyForConversion` computation W58 already established). **No offer-status check gates eligibility at all** — despite the task's own "typical examples" suggesting "offer accepted," §13's literal text names only the two checks above; an offer is used purely as an *optional data source* for the employee mapping, never a gate, since W57 built no candidate-facing acceptance flow. **Data mapping — only what's actually available, nothing invented:** name/contact/nationality from `candidates`, `residentialAddress` from `candidates.address` (§9 itself says the two mirror the same jsonb shape), department/branch/position from the linked requisition (the same application→vacancy→requisition three-hop chain every other Recruitment resource this phase reuses), employmentType/hireDate/workLocation from the current offer version if one exists. `candidates.nationalIdentifierType`/`Value` is deliberately NOT mapped to `employees.nationalId`/`passportNumber` — the candidate-side field is a generic type+value pair with no frozen rule for which employee column a given type resolves to; inventing that rule was out of scope. `emergencyContacts` is deliberately not mapped — no such field exists on `candidates` in this frozen model at all. **Duplicate/concurrent-conversion protection** is the same "no pre-check, just catch the DB race" discipline this phase already established (`offers.ts`'s `createOffer`, W58's `createPreEmploymentRequirement`) — `candidate_employee_links` carries BOTH a unique `(applicationId)` constraint (idempotent retry of the same application) AND a unique `(employeeId)` constraint ("one recruitment provenance per employee," §9), both translated to a single typed `AlreadyConvertedError` (mirrors `DuplicateLedgerEntryError`'s precedent, per §13's own text). **A real design gap found and closed during this workstream's own security review, before commit:** an internal candidate's `candidates.linkedInternalEmployeeId` needs to be *set* somewhere the first time a candidate converts, or the "does not create a second employee record" guarantee only holds for candidates who were already marked internal by some external means — a *second* application from a candidate who converted *through this very flow* would otherwise hit the "not yet linked" branch again and create a genuine duplicate employee. Fixed by setting `candidates.linkedInternalEmployeeId` to the newly created employee's id inside the same transaction, immediately after `createEmployee` succeeds. This surfaced a second, subtler consequence, caught by the workstream's own test suite rather than assumed: because `candidate_employee_links.employeeId` is *also* unique, a second application from an already-self-converted candidate correctly 409s as already-converted (inserting a second link row for the same employee is exactly what that constraint forbids) — the "reuse an existing employee" success path is therefore only ever reachable for a candidate whose `linkedInternalEmployeeId` was set by some means *other* than a prior conversion through this same function (e.g. a genuinely pre-existing employee who separately has a candidate record, with no `candidate_employee_links` row of their own yet) — a real, non-obvious interaction between two unique constraints, verified by a dedicated test for each branch. **Recruitment history is never touched:** no delete anywhere in this workstream — candidate, application, interview, scorecard, reference/background-check, offer, and pre-employment-requirement rows are all left exactly as they were; conversion only *adds* the provenance link, verified by a dedicated test. **Permissions:** §7's own row — `candidate.convert_to_employee`, no own/assigned tier, organization-wide only ("mirrors `employee.write`'s existing gate") — seeded to the exact same roles as `employee.write` (org_admin, hr_manager only, never `employee`). The route requires **both** `candidate.convert_to_employee` AND `employee.write` — neither alone is sufficient, verified by two separate 403 tests, satisfying this workstream's own explicit "neither permission alone should bypass the other" requirement. **API:** `POST .../applications/:id/convert-to-employee` (§10's own single line — no separate "conversion status"/"conversion detail" GET route exists in the frozen plan, so the frontend derives eligibility entirely from data it already fetches: `application.currentStageCategory` and the pre-employment-requirements summary's `readyForConversion`, both already wired into `/applications/:id` by W51/W58) — new OpenAPI schemas (`CandidateEmployeeLink`, `ConversionResult`) under a new `employee-conversion` tag, codegen verified zero-diff across two consecutive runs. **Frontend:** a "Convert to Employee" action on `/applications/:id` (§11's own frontend-impact line), shown only in a hired-category stage, disabled until every pre-employment requirement resolves, and replaced with a "View Employee" link to `/employees/:id` after a successful conversion. **Audit:** `employee_conversion.started`/`.completed`/`.failed` (targetType `application`) plus a distinct `employee_conversion.employee_linked` (targetType `employee`) — two perspectives on the same event (Recruitment's view vs. HR's view), not a duplicate. **Database impact:** migration `0034_bitter_microbe.sql` (one new table, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied — confirmed zero further drift after generation. Backend: 15 new tests (`employeeConversion.test.ts`, covering both dual-permission 403s, tenant isolation, both eligibility checks' 400s, a from-scratch conversion with full field mapping including requisition placement and offer data, the `linkedInternalEmployeeId`-set-on-first-conversion behavior and its "second application now 409s" consequence, the pre-existing-internal-employee reuse path, idempotent-retry 409, and the no-history-lost proof) plus 30 pre-existing `employees.test.ts` tests confirmed unchanged after the `createEmployee` extraction. Frontend: 4 new tests added to `application-detail.test.tsx` covering the action's hidden/disabled/enabled states and the post-conversion "View Employee" link. Full suite: 664 backend + 213 frontend tests pass. No live browser walkthrough was performed — this environment has no provisioned dev database, the same limitation every prior Recruitment workstream in this session has had; verification relied on the automated test suites, typecheck, lint, and production build only. Does not implement onboarding, payroll/leave/attendance setup, asset assignment, orientation, training, user-account/email provisioning, offer generation/approval, or candidate accounts — all remain later workstreams' scope or explicitly out of the frozen plan entirely. This is the last workstream before Phase 3A's own numbering moves to Employee Self-Service integration (W60) and reporting (W61+) — Recruitment's core hire-to-employee pipeline (W43 through W59) is now feature-complete end to end.

- **W58 — Pre-Employment Requirements — Complete.** A checklist tracked against an application before employee conversion — `pre_employment_requirements`, one row per `(applicationId, requirementCode)`. **Completion of a requirement, or of every requirement, never creates an employee, activates payroll/leave/attendance/HR records, or creates an organization membership** — this workstream only tracks status; §13's own `convert-to-employee` (W59, not yet built) is the only thing that will ever read these rows to decide readiness, by re-querying them itself, not by calling anything this workstream exports. **The frozen §9 row is deliberately minimal, and this workstream builds nothing beyond it — the same "frozen schema wins over speculative prose" discipline W44/W47/W56/W57 already established:** columns are exactly `applicationId`/`requirementCode`/`status`/`satisfiedAt`/`notes`, nothing more. **Status model:** only three values — `pending`/`satisfied`/`waived` — not the richer submitted/verified/rejected/expired lifecycle a generic pre-employment-checklist feature might suggest; §9's own "status derivation" column reads "n/a" for this resource (unlike e.g. `reference_checks`, which cites §4.7's lifecycle diagram), meaning there is no frozen one-way transition model at all. Status is therefore freely settable between all three values — reverting a mistaken "satisfied" back to "pending" is allowed — a deliberate difference from reference/background checks' terminal-once-decided immutability. `satisfiedAt` reflects only the most recent transition into "satisfied" (set on entry, cleared on exit), never stale evidence of a since-reverted decision. **No document/storage handling at all:** the frozen §9 row has no storageKey column (unlike e.g. `background_checks.documentStorageKey`), and no internal candidate-document-upload route exists yet in this codebase (§10's `GET/POST/DELETE .../candidates/:id/documents` line belongs to a different workstream's own `Scope:`, not this one's, which names only `pre_employment_requirements`) — so this workstream builds no upload/storage mechanism whatsoever, a deliberate scope boundary. Any evidence a candidate already uploaded at application time (W49's `candidate_documents`) remains visible via the application detail's existing `documents` list (W51) without this resource owning or referencing any storage key. **Permissions — none new:** §7's matrix has no row for this resource at all (unlike `background_checks`, which gets its own dedicated org-wide-only pair) — reuses the existing `application.read`/`.manage` pair, the same precedent W56's `reference_checks` already established for an identical "org-scoped (via application)" RLS descriptor and comparably low sensitivity (this row's own sensitive-data column reads "none directly"). No `seed-roles-permissions.ts` change was needed — the first Recruitment workstream this phase that required zero permission-seeding changes. **Computed summary:** the list endpoint returns a `summary` object (`totalCount`/`pendingCount`/`satisfiedCount`/`waivedCount`/`readyForConversion`) computed on every read, never stored (the same discipline as W52's `scoreRollup`/W55's `panelSummary`) — `readyForConversion` mirrors §13's own convert-to-employee readiness rule exactly ("every non-waived row is satisfied"), vacuously true when no requirements are tracked at all; it is purely informational and does not itself gate or trigger anything. **API:** `GET`/`POST .../applications/:id/pre-employment-requirements`, `PATCH .../pre-employment-requirements/:id` — new OpenAPI schemas (`PreEmploymentRequirement`, `PreEmploymentRequirementSummary`, `PreEmploymentRequirementListResponse`, `CreatePreEmploymentRequirementInput`, `UpdatePreEmploymentRequirementStatusInput`) under a new `pre-employment-requirements` tag, codegen verified zero-diff across two consecutive runs. **Frontend:** a new "Pre-Employment Requirements" checklist section on `/applications/:id` (mirroring the Reference/Background Checks sections' subcomponent style) — add-requirement dialog (free-text `requirementCode`, matching `candidate_documents.categoryCode`'s own "Master Data, not FK-validated" convention; no hard-coded Ghana-specific or any other fixed list), a resolved-count summary line, and a per-row "Update Status" action always shown (never hidden once "terminal," since none exists here). **Audit:** `pre_employment_requirement.created`/`.status_changed` — not in §20's self-documenting exemption list (that list names only `application_stage_history`/`requisition_approvals`/`offer_approvals`/finalized `interview_scorecards`), so every creation and status change gets its own `recordAuditEvent` call, per §9's own "on status change" audit-trigger note. **Database impact:** migration `0033_fat_dorian_gray.sql` (one new table + one new enum, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied — confirmed zero further drift after generation. Backend: 15 new tests (`preEmploymentRequirements.test.ts`) covering permission gating, tenant isolation, assigned/org-wide application visibility, duplicate-requirementCode 400 (a real DB unique-constraint race, mirroring `offers.ts`'s own catch-the-race discipline rather than a service-layer pre-check), the freely-bidirectional status transitions (including reverting satisfied back to pending and confirming `satisfiedAt` is set/cleared correctly), and all three `summary.readyForConversion` cases (all-resolved true, any-pending false, zero-rows vacuously true). Frontend: 4 new tests added to `application-detail.test.tsx` covering the new section's empty state, a pending row's Update Status action, a satisfied row still showing Update Status (no terminal hiding), and the resolved-count summary line. Full suite: 649 backend + 209 frontend tests pass. No live browser walkthrough was performed — this environment has no provisioned dev database, the same limitation every prior Recruitment workstream in this session has had; verification relied on the automated test suites, typecheck, lint, and production build only. Does not implement employee conversion, onboarding, user account creation, payroll/leave/attendance setup, contract execution, asset assignment, training, probation, orientation, candidate accounts, document upload/storage for requirements, or notifications (none explicitly assigned to this workstream in the frozen plan) — all remain later workstreams' scope or explicitly out of the frozen plan entirely.

- **W57 — Offers — Complete.** A versioned offer envelope per application (`offers` + `offer_versions`), taken through a single-step approval chain (`offer_approvals`, mirroring W47's `requisition_approvals` shape exactly, per §9's own "mirrors" instruction) before being issued — the final Recruitment stage this phase reaches. **The key architectural finding this workstream made — the first deliberate exception to "every Recruitment write stays admin-only" this phase:** §7's matrix marks `offer.manage`'s Assigned column "✔ manage draft" — real write capability, not just narrower visibility, unlike every prior write permission this phase (`application.pipeline.move`/`.manage`, `candidate.manage`, `interview.manage`, ...), which stayed org_admin/hr_manager-only regardless of the matrix's own assigned-tier markings, since no delegated recruiter/hiring-manager role exists in this codebase's actual three-role model (org_admin/hr_manager/employee). For offers there is genuinely no other mechanism through which an assigned recruiter or hiring manager (a plain "employee" role holder, identified only via the linked requisition's `recruiterEmployeeId`/`hiringManagerEmployeeId`) could ever exercise it, so `offer.read`/`offer.manage` are seeded broadly to every role — the first genuine precedent-break. Because `offer.manage` is now held by every employee, it can no longer serve as the org-wide-reach signal (unlike every prior resource, where the write permission itself implied admin-ness) — org-wide reach is instead signaled by holding any of the three narrow admin-only actions (`offer.approve`/`.issue`/`.withdraw`, none seeded to employee). Every `offer.manage`-gated write function independently re-verifies the caller is actually the assigned recruiter/hiring manager (via the existing `application → vacancy → requisition` three-hop resolution, applicationPipeline.ts's own established pattern) or org-wide — holding the permission key alone never lets an unrelated employee write an unrelated offer. **Versioning (§14):** editing a `draft` version updates that row in place (`PATCH .../offers/:id`); editing an `approved`/`issued` version instead creates a brand-new draft version and marks the prior one `superseded` in the same transaction (`POST .../offers/:id/versions`, a dedicated endpoint per §10's own naming, not a single dynamically-branching PATCH). §14 is silent on editing while `pending_approval` — this workstream treats that as not editable at all until a decision lands, mirroring W55 scorecards' "submitted = frozen" precedent. **No "reject" action exists** — §10 names only submit-for-approval/approve/issue/withdraw. `withdraw` is therefore the single general abort action, reachable from `pending_approval`, `approved`, or `issued` alike, doing double duty as both "pull back an issued offer" and "reject a pending one." `offer_approvals.decision` still carries the same three-value `pending`/`approved`/`rejected` enum as `requisition_approvals` (§9's own "mirrors" instruction), so `rejected` is a structurally valid but currently API-unreachable value — reserved, not invented from nothing. A withdrawn version's outstanding `offer_approvals` row (if any) is deliberately left `pending`, un-cleaned-up — the same precedent `cancelJobRequisition` already established for a requisition cancelled mid `pending_approval`. **Single-offer-per-application:** §9 marks `offers.applicationId` unique "where policy disallows multiple active offers," conditioned on an org policy flag (§17's `multipleActiveOffersAllowed`) that does not exist in this codebase's actual `recruitment_settings` table (already diverged from §9's prose before this workstream, per W44/W47's own documented gaps) — rather than inventing a new settings column, this workstream enforces the frozen default unconditionally: a plain, non-partial unique index, always one offer per application. Approval is similarly unconditionally required (no `offerApprovalRequired` toggle exists either) — a single, fixed required step, exactly mirroring `requisitionApprovals.ts`'s own no-config-gate precedent. **Reserved, not consumed:** `letterTemplateId`/`generatedDocumentStorageKey` are nullable columns per §9's own field list, with no offer-letter-template table and no document-generation logic built in this three-table frozen scope (§15: "offer letters are generated as stored HTML/text documents... no e-signature integration" — but no template-rendering mechanism is named to build it with). `generatedDocumentStorageKey` is stripped from every API response via a `PublicOfferVersion` DTO (the same discipline as W55's `externalInterviewerToken` and W56's `documentStorageKey`, applied proactively even though this workstream's own routes never populate it) — verified by a dedicated regression test; `letterTemplateId` stays (a harmless, always-null FK-shaped integer). Terminal states `accepted`/`declined`/`expired` are schema-valid but have no routes here — no candidate-facing acceptance/decline flow and no expiry scheduler exist anywhere in this codebase — a deliberate scope boundary. `compensationSummary` is an unvalidated, caller-supplied jsonb object (recruitment-scoped fields only — base salary, currency, bonus, benefits summary — never a full payroll structure, per CLAUDE.md's no-payroll-dependency principle), mirroring `candidates.experienceSummary`'s own precedent; never embedded in audit metadata. **Permissions:** `offer.read`/`.manage` seeded org_admin/hr_manager/employee (broad, per the finding above); `offer.approve`/`.issue`/`.withdraw` org_admin/hr_manager only, organization-wide only, no assigned tier at all — the same admin-only rollout every other Recruitment approval/finalization action this phase uses. **API:** `GET`/`POST .../applications/:id/offers`, `GET`/`PATCH .../offers/:id`, `POST .../offers/:id/versions`, `POST .../offers/versions/:id/submit-for-approval`, `GET .../offers/versions/:id/approvals`, `POST .../offers/versions/:id/approve`, `POST .../offers/versions/:id/issue`, `POST .../offers/versions/:id/withdraw` — new OpenAPI schemas (`Offer`, `OfferVersion`, `OfferDetail`, `OfferListItem`, `OfferListResponse`, `OfferApproval`, `CreateOfferInput`, `UpdateOfferVersionInput`, `ApproveOfferVersionInput`, `WithdrawOfferVersionInput`) under a new `offers` tag, codegen verified zero-diff across two consecutive runs (one nullable-object-via-`allOf` schema for `OfferListItem.currentVersion` hit the same broken orval pattern W55 already documented — fixed the same way, with a plain inlined `type: ["object", "null"]` shape). **Frontend:** `/offers` (list, org-wide + assigned visibility) and `/offers/:id` (full lifecycle: edit draft, create new version, submit, approve, issue, withdraw, version history, approval history) per §11's own frontend-impact line, plus a new "Offer" section on `/applications/:id` (create/view, mirroring the Reference/Background Checks sections' subcomponent style) and an "Offers" nav link (gated the same `isHrCapable`-only way every other Recruitment nav entry already is — an assigned employee still reaches their own offer via the application-detail deep link, the same UX precedent `/interviews` already established). **Audit:** `offer.created`, `offer_version.updated`/`.superseded`/`.submitted`/`.approved`/`.issued`/`.withdrawn` — the individual `offer_approvals` chain-row append is self-documenting (§20's own exemption list explicitly names `offer_approvals` alongside `application_stage_history`/`requisition_approvals`/finalized `interview_scorecards`), but the resulting offer-version status transition still gets its own `recordAuditEvent` call, mirroring `requisitionApprovals.ts`'s exact precedent. **Database impact:** migration `0032_burly_molecule_man.sql` (three new tables + three new enums, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied — confirmed zero further drift after generation. Backend: 34 new tests (`offers.test.ts`, covering the application→vacancy→requisition visibility chain, the assigned-recruiter real-write exception, duplicate-offer 409, draft-edit-in-place vs. new-version-on-approved/issued, submit/approve/issue/withdraw transitions and their invalid-state 400/409s, approval-history visibility, and the generatedDocumentStorageKey-never-leaks regression on two separate response shapes). Frontend: 19 new tests (6 `offers.test.tsx`, 10 `offer-detail.test.tsx` covering per-status action visibility across every non-terminal state plus the terminal no-actions case, 3 added to `application-detail.test.tsx` for the new Offer section's empty/populated/403-silent states). Full suite: 634 backend + 205 frontend tests pass. No live browser walkthrough was performed — this environment has no provisioned dev database, the same limitation every prior Recruitment workstream in this session has had; verification relied on the automated test suites, typecheck, lint, and production build only. Does not implement offer letter generation/e-signature, candidate-facing offer acceptance/decline, automated expiry, multi-step/delegated approval chains, or employee conversion — all remain later workstreams' scope or explicitly out of the frozen plan entirely.

- **W56 — Reference & Background Checks — Complete.** Status-tracked checks, no vendor integration — informs recruitment decisions, never makes them: never automatically rejects an application, moves the pipeline, creates an offer, or hires/converts a candidate. Two deliberately distinct business processes, kept in two separate tables and two separate lib/route files rather than merged just because both track a status. **Scope boundary — the biggest reconciliation this workstream made:** §7's permission matrix has **no dedicated `reference_check.*` permission at all** — only "Background checks" gets its own row. Reference checks therefore reuse the existing `application.read`/`.manage` pair (the same tier as viewing/scoring the application itself, W52's own precedent for `application.manage` gating an assessment action), directly importing `applicationPipeline.ts`'s `resolveApplicationVisibilityContext`/`getVisibleApplicationById` rather than re-deriving the candidate→vacancy→requisition chain a second time. Background checks are the opposite extreme — §7 marks that row org-wide only with **no assigned tier at all** ("the narrowest permission in the matrix," §23's own verification line): holding `application.read`/`.manage` (or even `candidate.read`) never implies background-check access, verified by a dedicated 403 test. **Status model (§4.7, shared by both tables):** `requested → in_progress → (completed | flagged | unable_to_complete)` — no "cancelled"/"expired" state exists in the frozen model; a check that can't be completed is recorded as `unable_to_complete`. Terminal once completed/flagged/unable_to_complete — no further status change, no result changes after finalization, enforced by re-checking the existing row's status before every update (400 otherwise). **Reference checks:** column list is exactly §9's row — `applicationId`, `refereeName`/`Contact`/`Relationship`, `status`, `notes`, `completedAt` — no separate "verification actor" or "supporting document" column exists in the frozen model, so neither was built. Duplicate-active-check prevention is a service-layer check (no unique constraint exists on the table), keyed on `(applicationId, refereeContact)`. **Background checks:** column list is exactly §9's row — `applicationId`, `checkType` (free text — no Master Data domain exists in this frozen scope, so criteria/types are never hard-coded, e.g. no Ghana-specific provider list), `status`, `vendorReference` (a plain manually-entered string — "an integration boundary," never a real vendor API call), `resultSummary`, `documentStorageKey`. Duplicate-active-check prevention is keyed on `(applicationId, checkType)`. **A real DTO leak caught and fixed during this workstream's own security review, before commit (the same class of issue W55 caught for its reserved token columns):** `documentStorageKey` was being spread straight from the raw DB row into every API response; fixed with a `PublicBackgroundCheck` shape that omits it entirely in favor of a computed `hasEvidence` boolean, so the frontend can show a download link without the server ever handing back an internal storage reference — verified by a dedicated regression test. **Consent:** deliberately not gated — no `consentReference`/consent-linkage column exists anywhere in the frozen `background_checks` row, so no check-specific consent verification was implemented; W49's existing `candidate_consents` (captured once, at application submission) is not silently reinterpreted as blanket permission for every future check, but no second, W56-specific consent record was invented either, since the frozen plan doesn't call for one — a documented limitation, not an oversight. **Documents:** evidence reuses `documentValidation.ts`'s existing signature-verified upload (same PDF/JPEG/PNG/DOCX/XLSX/10MB policy as every other upload in this codebase) and `fileStorage.ts`'s private, org-scoped `"background-checks"` subdirectory — never a public URL, never the candidate's own `candidate_documents` row. A second upload replaces the first (deleting the old file), and any upload is rejected once the check is terminal. Evidence download streams the file through an authenticated, `background_check.read`-gated route (`res.set("Content-Type", "application/octet-stream")` + `Content-Disposition: attachment`), mirroring `employees.ts`'s existing profile-picture-download precedent — never a static/guessable path. **Permissions:** `background_check.read`/`.manage` seeded org_admin/hr_manager only, no employee-role grant at all (same org-wide-only rollout as `talent_pool.read`/`.manage`) — reference checks need no new permission keys. **API:** `GET`/`POST .../applications/:id/reference-checks`, `PATCH .../reference-checks/:id`; `GET`/`POST .../applications/:id/background-checks`, `PATCH .../background-checks/:id`, `POST`/`GET .../background-checks/:id/evidence` — new OpenAPI schemas (`ReferenceCheck`, `CreateReferenceCheckInput`, `UpdateReferenceCheckStatusInput`, `BackgroundCheck`, `CreateBackgroundCheckInput`, `UpdateBackgroundCheckStatusInput`, `ReferenceBackgroundCheckStatus`) under new `reference-checks`/`background-checks` tags, codegen verified zero-diff across two consecutive runs. **Frontend:** two new sections on `/applications/:id` (§11's own frontend-impact line) — Reference Checks (list, request dialog, Record Outcome dialog, hidden once terminal) and Background Checks (list, request dialog, Record Result dialog, evidence attach/replace/download, all write actions hidden once terminal) — the Background Checks section silently renders nothing on a 403 rather than showing a scary error card, since lacking `background_check.read` is an expected, permission-appropriate state, not a failure. **Audit:** `reference_check.created`/`.status_changed`, `background_check.created`/`.status_changed`/`.evidence_attached` — metadata deliberately excludes refereeContact, notes, resultSummary, vendorReference, and documentStorageKey; only status/checkType/applicationId are recorded. **Database impact:** migration `0031_premium_wolfsbane.sql` (two new tables + one new shared enum, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied — confirmed zero further drift after generation. Backend: 24 new tests (`referenceChecks.test.ts` — 10, reusing the full W51 application-visibility mock chain; `backgroundChecks.test.ts` — 14, a much smaller flat-permission harness) covering permission gating (including the general-permissions-never-bypass-background_check-access proof), tenant isolation, assigned/org-wide application visibility for reference checks, duplicate-check prevention for both resources, status transitions, terminal immutability, evidence upload/download/signature-validation, and a no-pipeline-side-effect proof. Frontend: 7 new tests added to `application-detail.test.tsx` covering both sections' empty/populated/terminal states and the silent-403 behavior for background checks. Full suite: 600 backend + 186 frontend tests pass. No live browser walkthrough was performed — this environment has no provisioned dev database, the same limitation every prior Recruitment workstream in this session has had; verification relied on the automated test suites, typecheck, lint, and production build only. Does not implement external background-check provider/government-database integrations, automated identity/criminal-record verification, a referee public portal, candidate accounts, offers, hiring decisions, employee conversion, or AI risk scoring — all remain later workstreams' scope or explicitly out of the frozen plan entirely.
- **W55 — Interview Scorecards — Complete.** Independent, lockable evaluations — one per panel member per interview — never redesigns W54 (interviews/panel membership are read-only inputs here, never written to) and never mixes with W52's `application_scores` (a distinct business process: screening vs. interview evaluation, never combined into one rollup). A scorecard informs later decisions; it never moves the W51 application stage or triggers any offer/hiring action itself. **Scope boundary — the single biggest reconciliation this workstream made:** the frozen §9 table plan defines exactly two tables (`interview_scorecards`, `interview_scorecard_responses`) with no template/criteria table, no `weight` column, and no rating scale anywhere in the model — so, despite the session prompt's own extensive "scorecard template," "criteria weighting," and "template builder UI" language (all explicitly hedged as "expected concepts may include"), none of that was built. `criterion` is plain free text supplied per submission (not organization-configured), and `rating` is an unconstrained integer, mirroring `application_scores.score`'s own unconstrained-numeric precedent — a documented limitation, not an oversight. **Lifecycle (§9's own "draft until submittedAt, immutable after"):** draft (`submittedAt` null) → submitted (`submittedAt` set, immutable from the owning interviewer's side) → finalized (`finalizedAt` set, an HR/recruiter-only lock, §7 — no "own" tier exists for finalize at all, so an interviewer can never lock their own scorecard). §10 lists no separate submit/reschedule-style route, so the one `POST .../interviews/:id/scorecards` upserts the caller's own draft and, given `submit: true`, transitions it to submitted in the same call; any further call once submitted is rejected outright (400) — "a submitted scorecard cannot be silently replaced." **Visibility — a direct, explicit rule from §7's own integrity note, not a rollout judgment call this time:** `scorecard.submit` never implies `scorecard.read_all` — an interviewer must never see a colleague's scorecard, submitted or not, through this API; only a `scorecard.read_all` holder (org_admin/hr_manager) sees every panel member's scorecard plus a computed (never stored) panel-completion summary (submitted/pending counts, recommendation tally — same discipline as W52's `scoreRollup`). Panel membership is re-verified fresh against `interview_panel_members` on every single write, never cached, so a since-removed panel member is rejected going forward without any special-case code. `conflictDeclared` (W54) carries no automated restriction in this workstream — informational only, no rule anywhere in the frozen plan attaches behavior to it. **A real leak caught and fixed during this workstream's own security review, before commit:** the reserved, not-yet-consumed `externalInterviewerToken`/`externalInterviewerTokenExpiresAt` columns (present in §9's schema for a later workstream to wire up external-panel evaluation — §10 names no such route here, so it is explicitly deferred, mirroring `vacancies.publicId`'s own "reserved, not consumed" precedent) were being spread straight from the raw DB row into every API response; fixed by explicitly omitting both fields before any scorecard ever reaches a DTO, with a dedicated regression test asserting neither field is ever present even when the underlying row has a token set. **Permissions:** `scorecard.submit` seeded to every role (any employee could be an assigned panel interviewer, same broad rollout as `interview.read`); `scorecard.read_all` and `scorecard.finalize` are org_admin/hr_manager-only with no assigned tier at all — directly stated in §7's matrix itself for these two keys, not an inferred rollout choice. The GET route's own permission gate is manual (not the standard single-key middleware), since either `scorecard.submit` or `scorecard.read_all` alone should grant entry — a bug caught by this workstream's own cross-org test failing during development, fixed before commit. **Audit — deliberately none:** §20's Audit Plan explicitly names finalized `interview_scorecards` among the tables that are "routine, already-self-documenting movements" not duplicated into `audit_events` (alongside `application_stage_history`/`requisition_approvals`/`offer_approvals`) — the row's own `interviewerMembershipId`/`submittedAt`/`finalizedAt` columns already record who and when. **API:** `GET .../interviews/:id/scorecards`, `POST .../interviews/:id/scorecards`, `POST .../scorecards/:id/finalize` — new OpenAPI schemas (`InterviewScorecard`, `InterviewScorecardResponse`, `ResponseInput`, `SaveInterviewScorecardInput`, `InterviewScorecardListResponse`, `RecommendationCounts`) under a new `interview-scorecards` tag, codegen verified zero-diff across two consecutive runs (one nullable-object-via-`allOf` schema pattern was tried first, produced a broken `(unknown | null) & X` TypeScript type via orval, and was replaced with a plain inlined `type: ["object", "null"]` shape instead). **Frontend:** a dedicated `/interviews/:id/scorecard` route (per §11's own frontend-impact line) — a draft/submit evaluation form (recommendation select, overall comment, dynamic criterion/rating/comment rows) for panel members, a submit-confirmation dialog warning about immutability, a read-only view once submitted, and — for `scorecard.read_all` holders — a panel-completion-summary card plus every evaluator's scorecard with a per-row Finalize action; a minimal "Scorecard" link was added to `interview-detail.tsx` (W54) without otherwise touching that page. Draft-form state is lazily initialized from props in a dedicated `MyEvaluationCard` subcomponent (not a `useEffect`-driven sync), avoiding this codebase's `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint rule entirely. **Database impact:** migration `0030_wide_doctor_octopus.sql` (two new tables + one new enum, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied — confirmed zero further drift after generation. Backend: 15 new tests (`interviewScorecards.test.ts`) covering permission gating, tenant isolation, not-a-panel-member rejection, cancelled-interview rejection, draft upsert, submit-then-immutable, post-removal rejection, read_all-vs-own-only visibility (including a submitted colleague's scorecard staying invisible to a submit-only caller), panel-summary computation, finalize/double-finalize/finalize-a-draft rejection, and the externalInterviewerToken-never-leaks regression. Frontend: 8 new tests (`interview-scorecard.test.tsx`) covering loading/error/cancelled states, blank-draft rendering, the submit-confirmation warning, read-only rendering of a submitted scorecard, and the panel-summary/finalize-button visibility (including hiding Finalize once already finalized). Full suite: 576 backend + 179 frontend tests pass. No live browser walkthrough was performed — this environment has no provisioned dev database, the same limitation every prior Recruitment workstream in this session has had; verification relied on the automated test suites, typecheck, lint, and production build only. Does not implement scorecard templates/criteria configuration, weighted scoring, external-panel-member evaluation submission (deferred — reserved columns only), recommendations beyond the frozen four values, hiring decisions, offer generation, calendar/video integrations, or notifications — all remain later workstreams' scope or explicitly out of the frozen plan entirely.
- **W54 — Interviews & Scheduling — Complete.** Scheduling and panel composition only — this workstream never evaluates an interview (scores/recommendations/hiring decisions are W55's `interview_scorecards`, a wholly separate table this workstream never touches). Built on top of W51's existing `applications` records, never redesigning them. **Status model (§4.5):** `scheduled -> completed | cancelled | no_show`. "Rescheduled" is not a stored status — realized entirely through the one PATCH route (§10 lists no separate reschedule/complete/no-show endpoint): supplying `scheduledAt` cancels the existing row and creates a brand new one (never mutating a scheduled interview's date in place, the same "never edit history in place" discipline as `application_stage_history`); omitting `scheduledAt` performs an ordinary in-place update, including a status transition to completed/no_show or a full panel replace. **Visibility — a genuine departure from every prior Recruitment resource's pattern:** §7's Interviews row marks the assigned tier "own scheduled interviews as interviewer," not the recruiter/hiring-manager-on-linked-requisition chain every other resource (candidates, vacancies, applications) uses — an interview carries no such column at all. Resolved instead by a direct check of whether the caller's own membership appears as an `interviewerMembershipId` on that specific interview's panel; org-wide reach is still signaled by holding `interview.manage`, the same pattern as everywhere else. **Permissions:** §7's Interviews row is a single `interview.read`/`.manage` pair (unlike every prior resource's two-key read/write split) with the assigned marker against that one pair — kept consistent with this codebase's established rollout rather than treated as a new exception: `interview.read` is seeded to every role (any employee could be an assigned panel interviewer); `interview.manage` (schedule/update/reschedule/cancel/panel changes — no dedicated panel-management permission, per §7's own "avoid permission explosion" note) stays org_admin/hr_manager-only, the same admin-only rollout every other Recruitment write action uses regardless of a resource's read-side assigned tier. **Panel:** new `interview_panel_members` table — `interviewerMembershipId` nullable for an external interviewer, captured by name/email only (§17 decision 13 — no external-interviewer portal, no login); a partial unique index on `(interviewId, interviewerMembershipId)` where not null lets several external interviewers coexist freely on one interview while preventing the same internal interviewer being added twice; `conflictDeclared` is a plain self-declared flag with no automated behavior attached in this workstream. Panel composition is fully replaced (delete + reinsert) on every update that supplies a `panelMembers` array, mirroring `vacancies.ts`'s locations/questions embedded-array convention, rather than incremental add/remove endpoints. **Reconciliation — two top-level routes beyond §10's literal nested text:** `GET .../interviews` and `GET .../interviews/:id` were added at the organization root, not nested under one application, to back the frozen §11 frontend route `/interviews` ("calendar/list of scheduled interviews for the caller") and its detail page — the same top-level-list precedent W51 already established for `/organizations/:organizationId/applications` despite every application belonging to one vacancy. Scheduling/updating remain nested under `.../applications/:id/interviews[/:id]` and cancel stays a top-level `.../interviews/:id/cancel`, exactly matching §10's literal text. **Database:** two new tables — `interviews` (`applicationId`, `interviewType` enum `phone`/`virtual`/`in_person`, `scheduledAt` `timestamptz`, `durationMinutes`, `location`/`meetingLink`, `status`, `outcome` — column list is exactly §9's row; no `notes`/`timezone`/separate-end-time field exists there, so none were added — `scheduledAt` is inherently timezone-aware, an end time is derived from `scheduledAt + durationMinutes`, never stored) and `interview_panel_members`. **API:** `GET`/`GET .../interviews/:id`, `POST .../interviews/:id/cancel` (top-level); `GET`/`POST .../applications/:id/interviews`, `PATCH .../applications/:id/interviews/:id` (nested) — new OpenAPI schemas (`Interview`, `InterviewPanelMember`, `PanelMemberInput`, `InterviewListResponse`, `ScheduleInterviewInput`, `UpdateInterviewInput`) under a new `interviews` tag, codegen verified zero-diff across two consecutive runs. **Frontend:** `/interviews` (calendar/list, status filter), `/interviews/:id` (detail: type/schedule/location/meeting-link/outcome, panel list resolving internal panelists' names via the existing organization-members endpoint, reschedule/mark-completed/mark-no-show/cancel actions, a panel-edit dialog with internal-member-select and external name/email capture) — both gated by `<ModuleGate moduleKey="recruitment">`, a new "Interviews" nav link shown to `org_admin`/`hr_manager`. Additive tie-in: `application-detail.tsx` (W51) gains an Interviews card (§11 names interviews as part of `/applications/:id`) listing that application's interviews with a Schedule Interview dialog, linking each row to `/interviews/:id`. **Audit:** `interview.scheduled`/`.rescheduled`/`.cancelled`/`.panel_updated`/`.status_changed` recorded on every mutation — per §20's own audit plan, `interviews`/`interview_panel_members` are notably *not* among the tables explicitly exempted from `audit_events` duplication (unlike finalized `interview_scorecards`), so this workstream audits directly rather than relying on the rows' own columns alone. **Database impact:** migration `0029_flawless_captain_midlands.sql` (two new tables + three new enums, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied — confirmed zero further drift after generation. Backend: 19 new tests (`interviews.test.ts`) covering schedule/permission gating, cross-org 404s, the own-panel-membership-vs-org-wide visibility split (including proof that org-wide `candidate.manage` does *not* grant interview access), reschedule-as-new-row (old row cancelled, new row a genuinely different id, panel carried over), status transitions to completed/no_show, full panel replace, double-cancel 400, and panel-member validation (internal-and-external-both rejected, cross-org membership rejected). Frontend: 13 new tests (`interviews.test.tsx`, `interview-detail.test.tsx`) covering loading/error/empty states, status badge rendering, action-visibility gating by status (scheduled vs. completed), and panel-member name resolution (internal vs. external, conflict-declared flag) — plus the existing `application-detail.test.tsx` mock was extended for the two new hooks it now calls, with all 13 of its prior assertions still passing unchanged. Full suite: 561 backend + 171 frontend tests pass. No live browser walkthrough was performed — this environment has no provisioned dev database, the same limitation every prior Recruitment workstream in this session has had; verification relied on the automated test suites, typecheck, lint, and production build only. Does not implement interview scorecards, scoring, recommendations, hiring decisions, offer generation, candidate accounts, calendar integrations (Google/Microsoft 365/Zoom/Teams/Meet), or email reminders beyond this workstream's own audit trail — all remain W55's or later workstreams' scope.
- **W53 — Candidate Notes, Tags, and Talent Pools — Complete.** Collaboration and passive-sourcing features on top of W49's existing `candidates`/`applications` records — never redesigns the candidate record itself (no `PATCH /candidates/:id` was built, despite appearing in the consolidated §10 API Plan: this workstream's own compact §23 scope line names exactly four new tables and nothing else, the same precedent every prior workstream has followed of treating §23 as the scope boundary and §7/§9/§10/§11 as field/permission detail only). Notes, tags, and talent pools are three deliberately independent abstractions — no shared "collaboration" table or merged model. **Candidates (internal read, new this workstream):** new `lib/candidates.ts` — `GET .../candidates` (paginated, name/email search) and `GET .../candidates/:id`, visibility-resolved via the same three-hop batched traversal pattern as every other Recruitment resource (candidate → its applications → their vacancies → linked requisitions), org-wide for `candidate.manage` holders or assigned via a matching `recruiterEmployeeId`/`hiringManagerEmployeeId` on any reachable requisition; a non-visible candidate 404s identically to a nonexistent one. **Notes:** new `candidate_notes` table (`candidateId`, nullable `applicationId` distinguishing a candidate-level note from an application-scoped one, `authorMembershipId`, `note`) — create-only, no update/delete path, mirroring `candidate_consents`'/`application_scores`' append-only precedent; a supplied `applicationId` is validated server-side to actually belong to the candidate (400 otherwise). Never exposed to an applicant — no candidate-session read path exists anywhere in this codebase (W50 still deferred). **Tags:** new `candidate_tags` table, plain free-text `tag` column (deliberately never an enum or Master Data domain, per instruction that tag values are never hard-coded), unique on `(candidateId, tag)`; add/remove only, duplicate-insert races translated via the existing `isUniqueViolation` helper. **Talent pools:** new `talent_pools` (org-scoped, unique name per org, active/archived, mirrors `recruitment_workflows.ts`'s exact archive/reactivate shape) and `talent_pool_members` (unique on `(talentPoolId, candidateId)`) tables — a pool is never tied to a single vacancy or requisition, so candidates remain reusable across future campaigns. **Permissions — a deliberate split discovered while reconciling this workstream's own permission matrix row against established precedent:** `candidate.read` and `candidate.notes.read` are both seeded to every role (same reasoning as `vacancy.read`/`application.read` — any employee could turn out to be the assigned recruiter/hiring manager, resolved per-record in the service layer); `candidate.manage` and `candidate.notes.write` are org_admin/hr_manager-only, since every Recruitment *write* action in this codebase stays administrative-only regardless of a resource's read-side assigned tier (mirrors `application.manage`/`application.pipeline.move` over `application.read`'s exact precedent) — `candidate.manage` also gates tag add/remove, avoiding a dedicated tags permission. `talent_pool.read`/`.manage` are org-wide only, org_admin/hr_manager-only with no assigned tier at all (§7 — a pool isn't reachable through any one requisition's recruiter/hiring-manager), mirroring `leave_type.manage`'s rollout; verified by a test asserting an assigned recruiter without `talent_pool.manage` still gets 403. **API:** `GET .../candidates`, `GET .../candidates/:id`, `GET`/`POST .../candidates/:id/notes`, `GET`/`POST .../candidates/:id/tags`, `DELETE .../candidates/:id/tags/:tagId`, `GET`/`POST .../talent-pools`, `GET`/`PATCH .../talent-pools/:id`, `POST .../talent-pools/:id/archive`, `POST .../talent-pools/:id/reactivate`, `GET`/`POST .../talent-pools/:id/members`, `DELETE .../talent-pools/:id/members/:candidateId` — new OpenAPI schemas (`Candidate`, `CandidateListResponse`, `CandidateNote`, `CreateCandidateNoteInput`, `CandidateTag`, `AddCandidateTagInput`, `TalentPool`, `CreateTalentPoolInput`, `UpdateTalentPoolInput`, `TalentPoolMember`, `AddTalentPoolMemberInput`) under new `candidates`/`talent-pools` tags, codegen verified zero-diff across two consecutive runs. **Frontend:** `/candidates/:id` (candidate info, tags with inline add/remove, notes list with an add-note form, distinguishing application-scoped notes) and `/talent-pools` (pool list, create dialog, archive/reactivate, a members-management dialog with candidate search-to-add) — both gated by `<ModuleGate moduleKey="recruitment">`, a new "Talent Pools" nav link shown to `org_admin`/`hr_manager`. Deliberately no standalone `/candidates` directory page (only `/candidates/:id` and `/talent-pools`, per this workstream's own frontend-impact line, even though the consolidated §11 plan separately lists a directory page) — a minimal additive tie-in links `application-detail.tsx`'s (W51) candidate name to its new `/candidates/:candidateId` profile. **Audit:** `candidate_note.created`, `candidate_tag.added`/`.removed`, `talent_pool.created`/`.updated`/`.archived`/`.reactivated`/`.member_added`/`.member_removed` recorded on every mutation. **Database impact:** migration `0028_simple_onslaught.sql` (four new tables, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied — confirmed zero further drift after generation. Backend: 22 new tests (`candidatesTalentPools.test.ts`) covering candidate visibility (org-wide/assigned/cross-org-isolation), notes gating (`.read` alone insufficient to write) and applicationId-ownership validation, tag add/list/remove, and talent-pool CRUD/archive/reactivate/membership including the no-assigned-tier permission proof. Frontend: 16 new tests (`candidate-detail.test.tsx`, `talent-pools.test.tsx`) covering loading/error/empty states, tag and note rendering, and the members-management dialog. Full suite: 542 backend + 158 frontend tests pass. Does not implement interviews, offer management, employee conversion, candidate accounts/login, email campaigns, AI ranking, resume parsing, or external recruitment integrations — all remain later workstreams' or W50's scope.
- **W52 — Screening Questions & Scoring — Complete.** Answer capture (into W49's existing apply endpoint) plus internal scoring, on top of W47's `vacancy_questions` and W51's application pipeline — neither W49 nor W51 was redesigned, both were extended. **Reconciliation (per the handoff's own instruction to check the compact §23 entry against the fuller spec before building):** §23's terse W52 entry ("Answer capture, knockout evaluation, scoring rollup") omits two load-bearing rules that only exist in §12 (Screening, Scoring, and Pipeline Notes) — (1) knockout evaluation happens **at submission time**, server-side, and a failed knockout only **flags** the answer row for recruiter review, it **never auto-rejects** the application; (2) `applications.score` (defined since W49, always null) "is a computed, non-authoritative rollup ... recomputed from `application_scores` on read, not trusted as stored state" — mirroring `leave_balance_entries`' ledger precedent — so this workstream never writes to that column, only computes an equivalent rollup at read time.
