Missing either rule would have produced a materially wrong implementation (auto-rejecting candidates on a miskeyed expected answer; persisting a stale score). **Answer capture:** `submitPublicApplication` (W49, `lib/candidateApplications.ts`) now accepts an optional `answers: {vacancyQuestionId, answerText}[]`, sent as a JSON-encoded string field alongside the resume file in the same multipart apply request (multipart/form-data has no native array-of-objects support) — a `vacancyQuestionId` not belonging to the vacancy is silently ignored, never an error, since answers are optional and a mismatched id carries no security implication. Knockout evaluation (`computeKnockoutFailed`) applies only to `yes_no`/`multiple_choice` questions with a non-null `expectedAnswer` (§12 — free-text/numeric have no single canonical comparison); a normalized (trim+lowercase) mismatch sets `knockoutFailed = true` on the new `application_answers` row and nothing else. The public vacancy detail DTO (`lib/publicCareers.ts`) now also returns a `questions: PublicVacancyQuestion[]` array — deliberately excluding `isKnockout`/`expectedAnswer`, since a candidate must never learn which questions are knockout-screened or what answer is "correct." **Scoring:** new `lib/applicationScoring.ts`, append-only — `submitApplicationScore` inserts a new `application_scores` row every time (never an edit), gated by a new `application.manage` permission (already named, unused, in the frozen §7 matrix) rather than W51's `application.pipeline.move`, since scoring evaluates an application without ever changing its stage. `computeScoreRollup` (a pure function over the score list, never persisted): an explicit `overall`-type entry wins outright if one exists; otherwise the average of whichever of `screening`/`interview` have a latest entry; `null` when no scores exist. Deliberately never imports from `applicationPipeline.ts` (which imports this module's rollup helpers for the detail DTO) — a new, distinct `ApplicationScoreTargetNotFoundError` is defined here instead, mirroring `requisitionApprovals.ts`'s established precedent for keeping a mutual two-file dependency one-directional rather than circular. **A visibility gap found and fixed during this workstream:** the org-wide visibility check for applications (`resolveApplicationVisibilityContext`, W51) originally keyed only off `application.pipeline.move`, so a caller holding only the new `application.manage` (in principle, a scoring-only role) could submit a score but then get a null response back, since they couldn't see the application they'd just legitimately acted on. Fixed to treat either administrative permission as org-wide — caught by this workstream's own tests before it shipped, not discovered later. **Database:** two new tables — `application_answers` (`applicationId`, `vacancyQuestionId`, `answerText`, `knockoutFailed`, unique on `(applicationId, vacancyQuestionId)`, write-once) and `application_scores` (`applicationId`, `scoredByMembershipId`, `scoreType` enum `screening`/`interview`/`overall`, `score` `numeric(5,2)`, `notes`, no unique constraint — multiple entries per type are expected as independent reviewers score separately) — both append-only, no update/delete path anywhere. **API:** the existing `POST /careers/:orgSlug/jobs/:vacancyPublicId/apply` gains the `answers` field (no new public route); new `POST /organizations/:organizationId/applications/:id/scores` (`application.manage`-gated, returns the refreshed `ApplicationDetail`); `GET .../applications/:id` (W51) now also returns `answers[]` (with each question's text resolved for display), `scores[]` (newest first), and the computed `scoreRollup` — new OpenAPI schemas (`ApplicationAnswerSummary`, `ApplicationScoreEntry`, `SubmitApplicationScoreInput`, `PublicVacancyQuestion`), codegen verified zero-diff across two consecutive runs. **Frontend:** `careers-apply.tsx` (W49) now renders the vacancy's screening questions (a `Select` for `yes_no`, a typed `Input` for the rest) and JSON-encodes answered questions into the submission; `application-detail.tsx` (W51) gains a Screening Answers card (rendered only when answers exist, flags a failed knockout) and a Scoring card (score list, a rollup badge, an inline submit-score form shown only while the application is non-terminal). No Kanban/scorecard UI beyond this — interview scorecards remain a later workstream's scope. **Audit:** `application.scored` recorded on every score submission, mirroring the existing `application.*` event naming from W51. **Database impact:** migration `0027_violet_ozymandias.sql` (two new tables + one new enum, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied — confirmed zero further drift after generation. Backend: 9 new tests (3 in `publicCareers.test.ts` covering answer capture, the non-rejecting knockout flag, and cross-vacancy question filtering; 6 in `applicationPipeline.test.ts` covering score submission, the `application.manage` permission gate, the visibility-gap fix, the overall-wins/average rollup rules, and cross-org 404). Frontend: 8 new tests (3 in `careers-apply.test.tsx` covering question rendering and the JSON-encoded answers payload; 5 in `application-detail.test.tsx` covering answer/knockout rendering, score rendering with the rollup badge, and the score form's terminal-vs-non-terminal visibility). Does not implement interview scheduling/scorecards, assessments beyond this scoring primitive, offers, hiring decisions, employee conversion, talent pools, background checks, recruiter messaging, or reporting dashboards. Does not touch W50 (still deferred) or close the outstanding W39 ESS scope, which remains open.
- **W51 — Application Pipeline & Stage Movement — Complete.** The start of the ATS: internal review of applications already created by W49's public apply flow, and controlled movement through the organization's own configured recruitment workflow. Never redesigns W49 — reuses its `candidates`/`applications`/`candidate_documents` records unchanged, and W44's existing `recruitment_stages` unchanged as the stage model. **Stage model:** stages are W44's own org-configurable `recruitment_stages`, not a new hardcoded enum — every stage maps to one of the frozen plan's eight fixed categories (`applied`/`screening`/`interview`/`assessment`/`offer`/`hired`/`rejected`/`withdrawn`, §4.3); system logic keys off category, never an organization's own stage label. `hired`/`rejected`/`withdrawn` are terminal — no further movement except the dedicated `reopen` action. Every W49-submitted application starts with `currentStageId = null` (stage assignment was explicitly deferred to this workstream); the first movement's history row has `fromStageId = null`, which the schema anticipated. A never-triaged application (`currentStageId` null) is treated as the `applied` category for display purposes only — never written to the row. **Database:** one new table, `application_stage_history` (`applicationId`, `fromStageId` nullable, `toStageId` not-null, `movedByMembershipId`, `reason` — the optional comment, `movedAt`) — append-only, mirrors `requisition_approvals`'/`candidate_consents`' immutability discipline; no separate comments table (the frozen plan's own scope/database-impact lines name exactly one new table). **Transitions:** `moveApplicationStage` validates the target stage server-side against the application's own vacancy's workflow stage list — never a client-supplied "next stage" trusted as-is (§10) — rejecting a target outside that workflow, a target whose category is itself terminal (terminal categories are reachable only through their own dedicated actions below), or any movement while the application is already in a terminal stage. `rejectApplication`/`withdrawApplication` resolve the workflow's own `rejected`/`withdrawn`-category stage (lowest `displayOrder` if more than one is configured) and set `applications.rejectionReasonCode`/`withdrawalReasonCode` (both already existed, unused, since W49) — 400 if the org hasn't configured a matching-category stage, or the application is already terminal. `reopenApplication` moves a terminal application back to the workflow's `applied`-category stage, clearing both reason codes — 400 if the application isn't currently terminal, or no `applied`-category stage exists. Every transition is one `db.transaction` (history insert + `applications.currentStageId` update), so history and the current-stage pointer can never diverge. **Deliberately not enforced:** `reapplicationWaitingDays`/`duplicateCandidatePolicy` (W44) still have no terminal-state-triggered behavior to act on in this workstream — rejection/withdrawal exist now, but re-application after one is a distinct future capability, not built here. **Visibility:** same two-tier model as W47's vacancies — organization-wide (via holding `application.pipeline.move`) or assigned (via the application's vacancy's linked requisition's `recruiterEmployeeId`/`hiringManagerEmployeeId`, two hops out, batched not N+1); no candidate-session ("own") tier — anonymous applicants never reach these routes, consistent with W50's deferral. A not-visible application returns 404, identical to nonexistent, on both list-exclusion and direct `GET .../:id`. **Permissions:** `application.read` (seeded to every role, mirrors `vacancy.read`/`requisition.read`'s reasoning), `application.pipeline.move` (gates move-stage/reject/withdraw/reopen — org_admin/hr_manager/super_admin only, mirrors `vacancy.publish`/`.close`'s exact rollout; the permission matrix's "Assigned" column is realized as a visibility tier only, not a broader role grant, consistent with every prior Recruitment workstream's precedent). **API:** `GET .../applications`, `GET .../applications/:id` (embeds `documents[]` and immutable `history[]` directly — no separate `/history` route, mirroring W47's vacancy-embeds-locations/questions convention; the frozen API list names no such route either), `POST .../applications/:id/move-stage`, `.../reject`, `.../withdraw`, `.../reopen` — new OpenAPI paths/schemas (`ApplicationSummary`, `ApplicationDetail`, `ApplicationListResponse`, `ApplicationDocumentSummary`, `ApplicationStageHistoryEntry`, `MoveApplicationStageInput`, `RejectApplicationInput`, `WithdrawApplicationInput`, `ReopenApplicationInput`) under a new `applications` tag, codegen verified zero-diff across two consecutive runs. Internal DTOs freely expose internal ids (unlike W49's public DTOs) — this route requires authentication and `application.read`; candidate documents are surfaced as metadata only (filename/category/mime/size) — this workstream does not add a file-download/streaming endpoint. **Frontend:** `/applications` (list, candidate/stage filters, search, empty/error/loading states), `/applications/:id` (candidate info, current stage, move-stage control, reject/withdraw dialogs with optional reason code + comment, reopen action when terminal, document list, immutable history timeline), `/pipeline` (board view per selected vacancy — per the frozen plan's own frontend-impact line for this workstream — columns are the vacancy's own workflow stages via W44's existing `GET .../recruitment-workflows/:id/stages`, click-based card movement calling the same `move-stage` endpoint, not drag-and-drop: the frozen plan's own text explicitly allows "drag-or-click ... either way," and click avoids a new drag-and-drop dependency for one page). All three gated by `<ModuleGate moduleKey="recruitment">`, nav links ("Applications," "Pipeline Board") shown to `org_admin`/`hr_manager`. **Audit:** `application.stage_changed`/`.rejected`/`.withdrawn`/`.reopened` events recorded on every transition, mirroring the existing `job_requisition.*`/`vacancy.*` naming convention. **Database impact:** migration `0026_previous_adam_destine.sql` (one new table, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied — confirmed zero further drift after generation. Backend: 22 new tests (`applicationPipeline.test.ts`) covering assigned/org-wide visibility tiers, tenant isolation, every transition (first-movement-from-null, regression, terminal-block, cross-workflow-stage rejection, missing-category-stage config errors, double-terminal 400s, reopen clearing reason codes), and permission/module gating. Frontend: 15 new tests (`applications.test.tsx`, `application-detail.test.tsx`) covering loading/error/empty states, candidate/stage rendering, status-conditional action visibility (non-terminal vs. terminal), and history/document rendering. Does not implement interview scheduling/scorecards, assessments, offers, hiring decisions, employee conversion, talent pools, background checks, recruiter messaging, or reporting dashboards — all remain later workstreams' scope. Does not touch W50 (still deferred) or close the outstanding W39 ESS scope, which remains open.
- **W50 — Candidate Accounts & Verification — Deferred, not completed (approved product decision, 2026-07-30).** Documentation-only reconciliation, no source changes — see `docs/PHASE_3A_RECRUITMENT_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s own W50 section for the full reasoning and the preserved original scope, marked there as `STATUS: DEFERRED — NOT IMPLEMENTED`. **Reason:** W49 (below) already delivers the minimum useful public recruitment product — public listing/detail, anonymous application submission, candidate persistence, consent capture, CV upload, and signed-token status checking. Candidate accounts (password auth, verification tokens, candidate sessions, password recovery, account-support obligations, anonymous-application linking) are not required to continue internal candidate/application processing, and would add meaningful authentication surface and support burden without a demonstrated need yet. **Existing candidate experience (unchanged, remains active):** every application is anonymous; there is no candidate account, password, login, or session anywhere in this codebase; a candidate checks their own application status only via the signed, time-limited `statusCheckToken` link emailed at submission (W49) — never a bare email lookup. **Deferred scope (preserved, not built):** `candidate_accounts` table, candidate-session middleware, a verification token flow, `/careers/:orgSlug/login`/`register`, `/candidate/*`. **Dependency reconciliation:** inspected the next workstream in the frozen plan's own ordering, W51 ("Application Pipeline & Stage Movement") — its `Dependencies` line already names **W49**, not W50, so it operates directly on W49's `candidates`/`applications`/`candidate_consents`/`candidate_documents` records and needs no candidate-account concept; no dependency language anywhere in the frozen plan required correction. W50 is not cancelled — it may be resumed later as its own workstream, triggered by demonstrated demand for reusable candidate profiles, authenticated dashboards, multi-application management from one account, self-service, saved jobs, or account-based communication preferences. **Architecture decision:** not separately recorded — this repository has no established ADR log or architecture-decision-record convention to append to (only informal, uncatalogued "ADR-NNN" citations appear inline within the phase-plan documents themselves, with no canonical source); the decision and its consequences are captured here and in the frozen plan's W50 section instead, per instruction not to invent a new documentation framework for one decision. **Next active workstream:** W51 — Application Pipeline & Stage Movement (frozen plan §23), unstarted.
- **W49 — Public Careers Portal (Read + Apply Shell) — Complete (full frozen scope; see scope note).** Unauthenticated public listing/detail plus a real, anonymous (no-account) application-submission pipeline. **Scope note — a deliberate escalation over this session's own initial instruction, confirmed with you first:** this session's own prompt initially described a narrower "shell" (no persistence, no file upload, no candidate data) and separately said "the frozen plan controls" on conflict. The frozen plan's actual W49 Objective/Scope (§23) is unambiguous — "the apply-submission pipeline," four new tables (`candidates`, `candidate_consents`, `candidate_documents`, `applications`), and §6's full security model (rate limiting, honeypot, real file upload through `documentValidation.ts`, consent capture, signed status-check tokens) — a real submission pipeline, not a shell. Asked directly which to build, you chose the full frozen scope; this entry reflects that. **Numbering:** per this session's explicit instruction, labeled "W49" to match the frozen document directly — see the Current Phase note above for why there is no session "W48." **Public tenant resolution:** `resolvePublicOrganization(slug)` (new `lib/publicCareers.ts`) resolves an organization by its own `organizations.slug` only — never a numeric ID — and collapses "doesn't exist," "suspended," and "careers not enabled" into the identical null/404 outcome, so no public request can distinguish which applied (§6's cross-tenant-disclosure rule extended to org existence itself). "Careers enabled" reuses W44's existing `recruitment_settings.enabled` + `externalRecruitmentEnabled` (read via the existing `getRecruitmentSettings`, unchanged) rather than the platform Module Registry's `organization_modules` — per the frozen plan's own §8 instruction that public routes check the former, not `requireModuleEnabled`. No `careersSlug` field exists on `recruitment_settings` (W44 never built one — the org's own `slug` already serves that purpose per §6's literal text), so none was added. **Vacancy eligibility** (`isVacancyPubliclyEligible`, applied identically to both the list and detail reads): `status = "published"`, `visibility != "internal"`, `openDate` not in the future, and `closeDate` not in the past — evaluated fresh on every read, never by mutating the stored vacancy (no background scheduler exists to flip status on close, W47's documented limitation, so a stale-but-still-`published` row past its own `closeDate` must still be filtered out live). The `openDate` check is a deliberate defense-in-depth addition beyond what internal `status` alone guarantees: W47's manual-flip publish action lets a staff operator deliberately go live internally before `openDate`; the public boundary enforces its own independent gate regardless, so that override affects internal/staff visibility only, never public. Public DTOs (`PublicVacancySummary`/`PublicVacancyDetail`) are hand-built, field-by-field projections — never the internal `Vacancy` type reused — structurally excluding the internal serial id, `requisitionId`, and salary (vacancies carry no salary field at all, and `job_requisitions.salaryRangeMin/Max/Currency` is explicitly documented "org-internal only, never public"); `departmentName`/`employmentType`/`workplaceType` are resolved through a batched (not N+1) lookup of the linked requisition and department, never exposing the requisition's own id or any other of its fields. **Public identifiers:** every public reference is `vacancies.publicId` (W47) or a new `applications.publicId` — both random tokens via the existing `generateToken()` convention (`auth.ts`), never the row's serial PK; `applications` also carries a *separate* `statusCheckToken`/`statusCheckTokenExpiresAt` (90-day TTL) — an ephemeral, re-emailable secret for the anonymous status-check link, deliberately distinct from the permanent `publicId`. **Apply pipeline** (new `lib/candidateApplications.ts`, `submitPublicApplication`): finds-or-creates a `candidates` row deduped by `(organizationId, lower(email))` (a functional unique index), then atomically (one `db.transaction`) inserts `applications` (unique on `(candidateId, vacancyId)` — a repeat submission from the same email to the same vacancy is idempotent, returning the existing row with no second write, exactly per §6), `candidate_consents` (privacy-notice-version + a text snapshot, immutable/append-only, never reinterpreted against a later version), and `candidate_documents` for the uploaded resume (`fileStorage.ts`'s existing `writeOrgFile` under a new `"candidate-documents"` org-scoped subdirectory, validated first by the same `documentValidation.ts` signature/size/type check every other upload in this codebase uses — PDF/JPEG/PNG/DOCX/XLSX, 10MB). `applicationLimitPerCandidate` (W44) is enforced by a scoped-`WHERE`-then-`.length` count in application code, not a SQL `count()` aggregate — mirrors this codebase's established "scoped WHERE, filter/reduce in JS" convention (the same reasoning W40's dashboard metrics already documented) over introducing a new query pattern. On a genuinely new submission, a status-check email is sent via the existing Resend `EmailProvider` (reused unchanged from the forgot-password flow, W19); delivery failure is logged, never surfaced to the caller (mirrors `requestPasswordReset`'s exact discipline). **Deliberately not enforced in this workstream:** `reapplicationWaitingDays` and `duplicateCandidatePolicy` (both W44 settings) are read and stored but have no terminal application state to act on yet — nothing in this workstream can mark an application rejected/withdrawn (that begins with W51, Application Pipeline & Stage Movement) — so both are wired through for a later workstream to start honoring, not silently dropped. `applications.currentStageId` is likewise defined now (matching the frozen table's full shape) but always `null` — resolving an initial workflow stage is pipeline logic, not submission logic; every application in this workstream reports a simple "submitted" status, not a stage-category-derived one. **Security:** the apply endpoint is rate-limited (5/15min per IP, stricter than `/auth/login`'s 10/15min) given the larger file-upload payload; a hidden honeypot field ("website") returns the identical success response with nothing persisted when filled, never revealing detection to an automated submitter; the anonymous status-check endpoint is separately rate-limited (20/15min) against token brute-forcing and is strictly scoped to the organization resolved from the URL's own slug — a token belonging to a different organization is indistinguishable from an unknown one (closed as a real gap found and fixed during this workstream, before any test was written against it). No public route ever accepts or returns a numeric organization/vacancy/application ID. **Database:** migration `0025_dashing_wolverine.sql` (`candidates`, `candidate_consents`, `candidate_documents`, `applications` — four new tables, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied — confirmed zero further drift after generation. **API:** `GET /careers/:orgSlug`, `GET /careers/:orgSlug/vacancies` (search/department/location/employmentType/workplaceType filters, bounded `pageSize` ≤ 50), `GET /careers/:orgSlug/jobs/:vacancyPublicId`, `POST /careers/:orgSlug/jobs/:vacancyPublicId/apply` (multipart/form-data), `GET /careers/:orgSlug/application-status/:token` — none composes `requireAuth`/`requireMembership`/`requireModuleEnabled` (the first routes in this codebase that don't); new OpenAPI paths/schemas (`PublicOrganization`, `PublicVacancySummary`/`PublicVacancyDetail`, `PublicVacancyListResponse`, `ApplicationSubmittedResponse`, `ApplicationStatusResponse`) under a new `public-careers` tag, codegen verified zero-diff across two consecutive runs. **Frontend:** new standalone pages (no `<AppShell>`, never wrapped in `SecureRoute`, mirroring `reset-password.tsx`/`invite-accept.tsx`'s existing precedent) — `/careers/:orgSlug` (branding header, search/filters, vacancy cards, pagination, empty/error/loading states), `/careers/:orgSlug/jobs/:vacancyPublicId` (public-safe detail, Apply CTA), `/careers/:orgSlug/jobs/:vacancyPublicId/apply` (the form: name/email/phone/resume upload/consent checkbox/hidden honeypot, re-resolves the vacancy server-side, shows a real, non-misleading confirmation only after the API confirms persistence — this is not a no-op shell), `/careers/:orgSlug/status/:token` (anonymous status lookup). Also fixed a genuine pre-existing test-infrastructure gap while building these: added a `ResizeObserver` stub to the shared frontend test setup (`src/test/setup.ts`), alongside the existing Pointer Events stubs — no prior test had mounted a Radix `Checkbox` outside a closed dialog, so nothing had exercised the gap before this page. **Audit/Notifications:** no audit events for anonymous page views (none required by the frozen scope); the status-check email is the only outbound message, reusing existing infrastructure exactly as the frozen plan specifies, not a new notification system. Backend: 33 new tests (`publicCareers.test.ts`) covering org resolution (unknown/suspended/careers-disabled, all collapsing to the same 404), every vacancy-hidden case (draft/scheduled/paused/closed/archived/internal-only/future-open-date/past-close-date), cross-tenant isolation on both the vacancy list and the status-check token, public DTO field exclusion, the honeypot no-op, resume signature validation, the idempotent-duplicate-submission path, `applicationLimitPerCandidate` enforcement, and rate-limiter wiring. Frontend: 23 new tests (`careers-landing`/`careers-vacancy-detail`/`careers-apply`/`careers-status.test.tsx`) covering loading/empty/error states, branding, public-safe field rendering (asserting private fields are absent), the apply form's required-field gating, the honeypot field's presence and hidden/non-tabbable state, and confirming no authenticated app-shell navigation ever renders on any of the four pages. Does not implement candidate accounts/authentication/login (W50), ATS pipeline, screening, scoring, interviews, offers, employee conversion, recruitment dashboards, or SMS/WhatsApp delivery. Does not close the outstanding W39 ESS scope, which remains open.
- **W47 — Vacancy Management Foundation — Complete (scope note below).** The postable unit produced from an approved job requisition — internal CRUD and lifecycle only, no public careers exposure, candidate, or application logic exists yet, per the frozen plan's exclusion list for this workstream. **Scope note:** per this session's explicit numbering, this "W47" corresponds to the frozen document's own §23 W48 ("Vacancies") — the numbering shift recorded in the four prior sessions' notes (W43/W44/W45/W46) continues consistently; the next workstream (session "W48") corresponds to the frozen document's own W49 ("Public Careers Portal (Read + Apply Shell)"). **Status model — deliberate, transparent simplification:** the frozen plan's §4.2 vacancy status model is `draft -> pending_approval (if the org requires vacancy-level approval, otherwise skipped) -> scheduled -> published -> paused <-> published -> closed -> archived`, plus `cancelled` as a terminal exit from any pre-closed state. The frozen `recruitment_settings` field list (§9) has no `vacancyApprovalRequired` toggle — only `requisitionApprovalRequired`/`offerApprovalRequired` exist — mirroring the same kind of gap W46 already documented for its own delegated-approver tier, so `pending_approval` is omitted entirely here. The frozen compact workstream entry and its own API list expose only publish/pause/close/archive, no separate cancel action, so `cancelled` is folded into `closed` — the single terminal exit from any pre-closed state (draft/scheduled/published/paused), reached via the one `close` action the API plan defines. **Publish semantics:** the initial publish from `draft` weighs `openDate` — goes straight to `published` when unset or not in the future, otherwise becomes `scheduled` (no background scheduler exists in this phase, per the frozen plan's own note). Calling publish again on an already-`scheduled` vacancy always flips it live immediately (the manual operator flip), and the same action resumes a `paused` vacancy straight back to `published` — reusing one action for both directions since the frozen API plan defines no separate resume action. **Database:** new `vacancies` table (`requisitionId` restrict-FK to `job_requisitions`, `workflowId` nullable FK to `recruitment_workflows`, `publicId` — a unique token via the existing `generateToken()` convention from auth.ts, reserved for a later public careers workstream and not consumed here — `title`, `visibility` enum internal/external/both, `status` enum per above, `openingsCount`, `filledCount` always 0 and never client-settable, `openDate`/`closeDate` as `timestamptz`, `jobDescription`/`responsibilities`/`requirements`/`preferredQualifications`, `seoTitle`/`seoDescription`, `featured`), `vacancy_locations` (`vacancyId` cascade, nullable `branchId` for an existing branch or free-text `label` for e.g. "Remote"), `vacancy_questions` (`vacancyId` cascade, `questionText`, `questionType` enum text/yes_no/multiple_choice/numeric, `isKnockout`, nullable `expectedAnswer`, `displayOrder`, active/archived) — `organizationId` denormalized on both child tables, mirroring `recruitment_stages.ts`'s established convention for tables scoped only via a parent FK. **Locations/questions are nested fields on the vacancy payload, not separate CRUD endpoints** — a deliberate scope narrowing: the frozen plan's §10 API Plan lists only `GET/POST/PATCH .../vacancies[/:id]` plus the four lifecycle actions, no `.../locations` or `.../questions` sub-routes, and the frontend plan describes one editor page ("description, questions, locations, publish controls"), not separate sub-resource screens — create/update replaces the full location/question set transactionally when provided. **Creation gating:** a vacancy can only be created from a requisition whose `status = "approved"` — every other status (draft/pending_approval/rejected/cancelled/closed/partially_filled/filled) is rejected with 400; the requisition's organization ownership is independently re-verified via `assertBelongsToOrganization` (never trusted from the request body alone), matching Architecture Principle 5. **Visibility:** the frozen plan's §7 permission matrix marks only "Assigned" and "Org-wide" for Vacancies (no "Own" tier, unlike Requisitions) — a vacancy carries no `recruiterId`/`hiringManagerId` of its own, so the assigned tier is resolved through the linked requisition's `recruiterEmployeeId`/`hiringManagerEmployeeId` (a batch lookup, not N+1); a vacancy invisible to the caller returns 404 for both list-exclusion and direct `GET .../:id`, identical to Requisitions' non-probeable-403-vs-404 discipline. **Permissions:** `vacancy.read` (seeded to every role, mirrors `requisition.read`'s reasoning — any employee could be the assigned recruiter/hiring manager on the linked requisition), `vacancy.manage` (create/update), `vacancy.publish` (publish/pause), `vacancy.close` (close/archive) — the latter three org_admin/hr_manager/super_admin only, mirroring `requisition.create`/`.update`/`.cancel`'s exact rollout. **API:** `GET/POST .../vacancies`, `GET/PATCH .../vacancies/:id` (PATCH only while `status = draft`, mirroring `job_requisitions`' exact PATCH-only-draft convention — `status`/`filledCount`/`publicId`/audit fields structurally absent from the request schema), `POST .../vacancies/:id/publish`, `.../pause`, `.../close`, `.../archive` — all 404/400-on-invalid-transition, new OpenAPI paths/schemas (`Vacancy`, `VacancyListResponse`, `CreateVacancyInput`, `UpdateVacancyInput`, `VacancyLocation(Input)`, `VacancyQuestion(Input)`), codegen verified zero-diff after generation. **Frontend:** new `/vacancies` (list, filters, create-from-approved-requisition dialog) and `/vacancies/:id/edit` (single editor + detail page: read-only details/locations/questions cards, a draft-only edit dialog covering description/openings/featured/locations/questions, and status-conditional publish/pause/close/archive actions) pages, `<ModuleGate moduleKey="recruitment">`, nav link ("Vacancies") shown to `org_admin`/`hr_manager`. **Audit:** `vacancy.created`/`.updated`/`.published`/`.paused`/`.closed`/`.archived` events recorded on every write and lifecycle transition, mirroring the existing `job_requisition.*` event naming convention. **Database impact:** migration `0024_salty_wither.sql` (three new tables + three new enums, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied — confirmed zero further drift after generation. Backend: 37 new tests (`vacancies.test.ts`) covering creation gating on every non-approved requisition status, cross-org requisition/workflow/branch reference validation, nested location/question creation, assigned/org-wide visibility tiers, tenant isolation, draft-only PATCH, every lifecycle transition (including the scheduled-vs-published openDate branching and the paused-resume direction), concurrent double-publish/double-close 400s, and permission/module gating. Frontend: 15 new tests (`vacancies.test.tsx`, `vacancy-editor.test.tsx`) covering the list's loading/error/empty states and detail linking, and the editor's status-conditional action visibility (draft/published/paused/closed/archived) and locations/questions rendering. Does not implement vacancy-level approval, public careers exposure, candidates, applications, ATS, interviews, offers, employee conversion, recruitment dashboards/reports, or any later workstream's scope. Does not close the outstanding W39 ESS scope, which remains open.
- **W46 — Requisition Approval Workflow — Complete (scope note below).** Controls the transition of a submitted job requisition from `pending_approval` to `approved`/`rejected`. **Scope note:** per this session's explicit numbering, this "W46" corresponds to the frozen document's own §23 W47 ("Requisition Approval Workflow") — the numbering shift recorded in the three prior sessions' notes (W43/W44/W45) continues consistently; the next workstream (session "W47") corresponds to the frozen document's own W48 ("Vacancies"). **Approval model — deliberate, transparent simplification:** the frozen plan's §7 permission matrix names both an "assigned" tier (recruiter/hiring manager, "where explicitly delegated") and an organization-wide tier for `requisition.approve`. No delegated-approver assignment mechanism exists anywhere in Recruitment (W43–45 built none), so this workstream implements the organization-wide tier only — any `requisition.approve` holder (`org_admin`/`hr_manager`/`super_admin`) may decide any pending requisition in the organization; a future workstream can add a delegated tier without changing the table shape. A single required approval step per requisition (sequence 1) is used, mirroring W35's single-level transactional decision pattern exactly — not a generic multi-step workflow engine. **Database:** new `requisition_approvals` table (`requisitionId`, `sequence` — always 1 today, present for a future multi-step extension without a schema change — `approverMembershipId` resolved at decision time not pre-assigned, `decision` enum pending/approved/rejected, `decidedAt`, `comment`), `(requisitionId, sequence)` unique (structural duplicate-instance guard), indexed for pending-lookup. Rows are mutated in place from pending to a final decision and never touched again — immutable once decided, enforced structurally (the conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE decision = 'pending'` finds nothing once decided). **Submission integration:** `jobRequisitions.ts`'s `submitJobRequisition` now creates the single pending approval step inside the same `db.transaction` as the `draft → pending_approval` status update — a requisition can never be `pending_approval` without an active approval instance, and the existing conditional-update + unique-index pair together prevent a concurrent or repeated submit from ever creating two. **Decisions:** `approveJobRequisition`/`rejectJobRequisition` (new `lib/requisitionApprovals.ts`) each run a transactional conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE decision = 'pending'` on the approval row followed by a conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE status = 'pending_approval'` on the requisition row, both inside one transaction — a concurrent double-decision, or an approve/reject race, sees zero rows affected on the second attempt and fails as `RequisitionApprovalNotPendingError` (409), never double-processing; approval is immediately final since there is only one step. Rejection reason/comment is optional on both actions, mirroring W35's `rejectionReason` precedent exactly (not a frozen requirement to make it mandatory). No reopening/resubmission of a rejected requisition — out of this workstream's scope, per instruction. **Permissions:** one new key, `requisition.approve`, seeded to `org_admin`/`hr_manager`/`super_admin` — same rollout as `requisition.update` (mirrors the org-wide-only approver model above); approval history reuses the existing `requisition.read` permission plus the same visibility tiers `GET .../job-requisitions/:id` already established (own/assigned/department/branch/organization-wide) — a requisition invisible to the caller returns 404 for its approval history too, same non-probeable-403-vs-404 discipline. **API:** `GET .../job-requisitions/pending-approvals` (org-wide inbox, `requisition.approve`-gated, registered before the `/:id` route so Express doesn't swallow the literal segment as an `:id` param), `GET .../job-requisitions/:id/approvals` (immutable history, `requisition.read`-gated + same visibility), `POST .../job-requisitions/:id/approve`, `POST .../job-requisitions/:id/reject` (both `requisition.approve`-gated, optional `comment` body, 404/409 responses) — new OpenAPI paths/schemas (`RequisitionApproval`, `ApproveJobRequisitionInput`, `RejectJobRequisitionInput`), codegen verified zero-diff after generation. **Frontend:** new `/requisition-approvals` inbox page (list, approve, reject-with-optional-comment dialog, empty/loading/error states), nav-gated to `org_admin`/`hr_manager` (UX convenience; the server-side permission is the real gate); `/requisitions/:id` gains Approve/Reject actions (visible only while `pending_approval`) and an Approval History card (rendered only when history exists), both reusing the page's existing conflict-toast pattern for a 409. **Audit:** `job_requisition.approved`/`job_requisition.rejected` events recorded on decision (mirrors the existing `job_requisition.*` event naming already used for created/updated/submitted/cancelled/archived) — no separate "workflow created"/"completed" events, since a single-step chain's creation and completion are already implicit in the existing `job_requisition.submitted` event and the new approved/rejected events respectively. **Database impact:** migration `0023_rich_romulus.sql` (one new table + one new enum, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied — confirmed zero further drift after generation. Backend: 18 new tests (`requisitionApprovals.test.ts`) covering pending-approvals inbox scoping/tenant-isolation, approval-history visibility/404 parity, approve/reject success, immutable-decision and duplicate/concurrent-decision 409s (including an approve/reject race), and permission/module gating; `jobRequisitions.test.ts` updated (submit now creates the pending approval step transactionally) with a `db.transaction` mock added, all 23 existing tests still passing. Frontend: 10 new tests (`requisition-approvals.test.tsx`, plus additions to `requisition-detail.test.tsx`) covering the inbox's loading/error/empty states, approve/reject actions, status-conditional action visibility, and approval-history rendering. Does not implement a delegated-approver tier, multi-step sequential chains, reopening/resubmission, vacancies, candidates, applications, or any later workstream's scope.
- **W45 — Job Requisition Foundation — Complete.** An organization-scoped request/authorization to recruit for one or more openings — the record and its lifecycle only; no approval execution, vacancy, candidate, or application logic exists yet, per the frozen plan's exclusion list for this workstream. **Scope note:** per this session's explicit numbering, this "W45" corresponds to the frozen document's original W46 ("Job Requisitions") — the numbering shift recorded in the prior two sessions' notes (W43/W44) continues consistently; the next workstream (session "W46") corresponds to the frozen document's original W47 ("Requisition Approval Workflow"). **Database:** new `job_requisitions` table (`title`, `requisitionType` — new_role/replacement/temporary/internship/volunteer/contract/ministry — `positionId`/`departmentId`/`branchId`/`hiringManagerEmployeeId`/`recruiterEmployeeId`, all nullable; `requestedHeadcount`, `filledCount` always 0 and never client-settable; `employmentType` reuses the existing `employees.employmentType` enum unchanged; a new `workplaceType` enum — onsite/remote/hybrid; `expectedStartDate`; recruitment-scoped `salaryRangeMin`/`Max`/`Currency`, never a payroll dependency; `replacementEmployeeId`; a full 8-value `status` enum defined now — draft/pending_approval/approved/rejected/partially_filled/filled/cancelled/closed — even though this workstream only ever writes draft/pending_approval/cancelled/closed, so a later ALTER TYPE is never needed once approval execution and fulfillment land; `cancellationReason`; `createdBy`/`updatedBy`, doubling as "requested by" — no separate column, same as `employees.createdBy` already does). **Lifecycle:** create (always `draft`) → submit (`draft → pending_approval`, a conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE status = 'draft'` mirroring W35's exact idempotency shape — a concurrent double-submit sees zero rows affected and fails, never double-processing) → cancel (`draft`/`pending_approval` → `cancelled`, with an optional reason) → archive (`draft`/`cancelled` → `closed`, this workstream's "Closure," per the frozen plan's §3.2 JOB REQUISITIONS field list). General `PATCH` is only permitted while `status = draft`; `filledCount`/`status`/audit fields are structurally impossible to set through it (absent from the request schema entirely, not merely ignored). **Validation:** every FK (`position`/`department`/`branch`/`hiringManager`/`recruiter`/`replacementEmployee`) independently re-verified to belong to the caller's organization via `assertBelongsToOrganization` (unchanged); `requestedHeadcount` must be positive; `filledCount` (always 0 today) validated to never exceed it, ready for when a later workstream starts incrementing it; salary min/max ordering and currency-required-with-range; `replacementEmployeeId` required if and only if `requisitionType = replacement`. **Authorization/visibility:** new `requisition.read` (seeded to every role, mirrors `leave_request.approve`'s precedent — any employee could be a requisition's assigned recruiter or hiring manager) plus `requisition.create`/`.update`/`.cancel` (org_admin/hr_manager only, mirrors `leave_type.manage`'s rollout). Read visibility is tiered in the service layer using W43's authorization primitives unchanged: organization-wide for `requisition.update` holders; otherwise scoped to requisitions the caller requested (`createdBy`), is the assigned recruiter or hiring manager on (`isAssignedRecruitmentActor`), or shares a department/branch with (`isSameDepartmentScope`/`isSameBranchScope`) — a requisition that exists but isn't visible returns 404, identical to one that doesn't exist, so visibility can never be probed via a 403-vs-404 difference. **API:** `GET/POST .../job-requisitions`, `GET/PATCH .../job-requisitions/:id`, `.../submit`, `.../cancel`, `.../archive` — list supports `status`/`requisitionType`/`department`/`branch`/`hiringManager`/`recruiter`/`search` filters plus `page`/`pageSize` (visibility-filtered in application code, then paginated, mirroring `leaveCalendar.ts`'s established "scoped WHERE, filter/reduce in JS" convention — no new query pattern). **Frontend:** new `/requisitions` (list, filters, create dialog) and `/requisitions/:id` (detail, edit-while-draft, submit/cancel/archive actions gated by current status) pages, `<ModuleGate moduleKey="recruitment">`, nav links shown to `org_admin`/`hr_manager`. **Database impact:** migration `0022_productive_talkback.sql` (one new table + three new enums, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied — confirmed zero further drift after generation. Backend: 23 new tests (`jobRequisitions.test.ts`) covering module/permission gating, every validation rule, all four visibility tiers, tenant isolation, and every lifecycle transition including the concurrent-double-submit idempotency case. Frontend: 12 new tests (`requisitions.test.tsx`, `requisition-detail.test.tsx`) covering loading/error/empty states, list rendering and detail linking, and status-conditional action visibility. Does not touch approval execution, vacancies, candidates, applications, interviews, offers, talent pools, reporting, or employee conversion. Does not close the outstanding W39 ESS scope, which remains open.
- **W44 — Recruitment Configuration Foundation — Complete.** How every organization configures Recruitment before any requisition or vacancy exists. **Scope reconciliation:** this session's W44 combines the frozen plan's original W43 database/API scope (deferred here from the prior session) with the settings/workflow admin UI the frozen plan originally called W45 — the frozen document's own W43/W44/W45 titles are unchanged (no rewrite), this note and the prior session's W43 note together record the actual execution order; the next workstream is W45 (Job Requisition Foundation) per this session's explicit numbering. Three new tables: `recruitment_settings` (one row per org — a dedicated table, not the generic Organization Configuration Engine namespace, mirroring `leave_policies`' reasoning over Master Data), `recruitment_workflows` (named pipelines, at most one default per org enforced by a partial unique index), and `recruitment_stages` (ordered stages within a workflow; `category` is a fixed system enum — `applied/screening/interview/assessment/offer/hired/rejected/withdrawn` — so an organization's own stage renaming/reordering never breaks later logic that depends on "is this stage terminal"; `isTerminal` is derived server-side from `category`, never independently settable). No candidate movement, requisition, vacancy, or application logic exists yet — configuration only, per the frozen plan's explicit exclusion list for this workstream. **Services:** `lib/recruitmentSettings.ts` (get-with-defaults/update, mirrors `organizationConfig.ts`'s "no row created on read" behavior against a real table instead of a JSON namespace), `lib/recruitmentWorkflows.ts` (CRUD + archive/reactivate, mirrors `leaveTypes.ts`; `setDefaultRecruitmentWorkflow` atomically unsets the previous default and sets the new one inside one transaction), `lib/recruitmentStages.ts` (CRUD + archive/reactivate, workflow-scoped). **Authorization:** reuses W43's `RECRUITMENT_MODULE_KEY` and the existing `requireModuleEnabled`/`requireMembership`/`requirePermission` composition unchanged — no new authorization mechanism. Every route (`routes/recruitmentSettings.ts`, `routes/recruitmentWorkflows.ts`) is gated by `recruitment_settings.read`/`.manage` (W43's permission pair) — workflows and stages reuse the same pair rather than inventing separate ones, since they're part of the same "recruitment configuration" administrative surface, not yet-unbuilt operational entities with their own access tiers. `defaultWorkflowId` on settings is independently re-verified to belong to the caller's organization (`assertBelongsToOrganization`, extended to cover `recruitmentWorkflowsTable` for the first time) before being saved — never trusted from the request body alone. **API:** `GET/PATCH .../recruitment-settings`; `GET/POST/PATCH .../recruitment-workflows[/:id]`, `.../archive`, `.../reactivate`, `.../set-default`; `GET/POST/PATCH .../recruitment-workflows/:workflowId/stages[/:id]`, `.../archive`, `.../reactivate` — all new OpenAPI paths/schemas, codegen verified zero-diff after generation. **Frontend:** new "Recruitment Settings" page (`/recruitment-settings`, `<ModuleGate moduleKey="recruitment">`) with a settings form, a workflow list (create/edit/archive/reactivate/set-default), and an expandable per-workflow Stages panel (mirrors `leave-types.tsx`'s expand-to-nested-panel pattern exactly) — nav link shown to `org_admin`/`hr_manager` (UX convenience; the server-side permission is the real gate). No dashboard, no recruitment operations UI, per the frozen scope. **Validation:** duplicate workflow name and duplicate stage name/display-order within a workflow both rejected with 409 (database unique constraints, translated the same way `isUniqueViolation` already is elsewhere); a negative `reapplicationWaitingDays` or non-positive retention/offer-expiry/application-limit value rejected with 400; single-default-workflow invariant enforced at the database level, not just the service layer. No system-defined default workflow/stage templates are seeded in this workstream, so "immutable system defaults" doesn't yet apply to anything. **Database impact:** migration `0021_grey_ravenous.sql` (three new tables + two new enums, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied — confirmed zero further drift after generation. Backend: 16 new tests (`recruitmentConfiguration.test.ts`) covering settings defaults/validation/cross-org `defaultWorkflowId` rejection, workflow CRUD/duplicate-name/tenant-isolation/set-default atomicity, and stage CRUD/terminal-derivation/duplicate-order/cross-org-workflow-404. Frontend: 6 new tests (`recruitment-settings.test.tsx`) covering loading/error states, settings-form rendering, the empty-workflow state, workflow list rendering (default badge, set-default visibility), and the Stages panel expansion. Does not touch requisitions, vacancies, candidates, applications, interviews, offers, talent pools, reporting, or employee conversion. Does not close the outstanding W39 ESS scope, which remains open.
- **W43 — Recruitment Foundation — Complete (scope note below).** Established the module's shared authorization foundation only — no recruitment business table, route, or frontend page. **Scope note:** the frozen plan's own W43 section additionally scopes `recruitment_settings`/`recruitment_workflows`/`recruitment_stages` tables and their CRUD API; this execution pass deliberately narrowed W43 to permissions/module-gating/authorization primitives first, so that the settings/workflow tables and their routes ship already permission-gated from their first commit — consistent with how every Phase 2B route was built (`requireMembership` + `requireModuleEnabled` + `requirePermission` together, never added after the fact) and with CLAUDE.md's Security First principle. The frozen document's W43 database/API scope is **not implemented yet** and is expected to land as the next workstream; the frozen plan's own text is unchanged (no rewrite), this note records the actual execution order. **Module registration:** the `recruitment` module already existed in the registry (W3, `status: "hidden"`) with no `requiredModuleKeys` — confirmed, not modified, matching the exact precedent that `leave`/`attendance`/`employee_self_service` also stayed `"hidden"` through their entire build-out (registry status only affects catalog visibility, never per-org enablement). Backend (`requireModuleEnabled`) and frontend (`<ModuleGate>`) gating for the `recruitment` key were already generically covered by pre-existing tests (`moduleGating.test.ts`, `module-gate.test.tsx`, both already using `"recruitment"` as their literal example since W5/W6) — reconfirmed passing, no new module-gating test needed. **Permissions:** two new keys, `recruitment_settings.read`/`recruitment_settings.manage` (`lib/db/src/seed/seed-roles-permissions.ts`), seeded to `org_admin`/`hr_manager`/`super_admin` only — mirrors `leave_type.read`/`.manage`'s exact rollout; the base `employee` role gets neither, since recruitment settings administration isn't a self-service concern (unlike `leave_type.read`, which employees need to see options when submitting a request). No other Recruitment permission key (requisition/vacancy/candidate/etc.) was added — those remain scoped to their own owning workstreams per the frozen plan's five-tier model. **Authorization foundation:** new `artifacts/api-server/src/lib/recruitmentAuthorization.ts` — `RECRUITMENT_MODULE_KEY` constant; a `RecruitmentVisibilityScope` type (`own | assigned | department | branch | organization_wide`, matching §7 of the frozen plan); `resolveRecruitmentActorEmployeeId` (a thin, named wrapper over W33's existing `resolveOwnEmployeeId` — no second "which employee is me" mechanism); `hasOrgWideRecruitmentAccess` (thin wrapper over the existing `hasPermission`); and three pure comparison helpers (`isAssignedRecruitmentActor`, `isSameDepartmentScope`, `isSameBranchScope`) plus `resolveRecruitmentVisibilityScope`, which combines them in the frozen plan's priority order (org-wide > assigned > department > branch). None of these query any recruitment business table — every later workstream's requisition/vacancy/candidate/application queries will resolve their own record's `recruiterId`/`hiringManagerId`/`departmentId`/`branchId` and pass them into these pure functions, not the other way around. Candidate-session ("own") authorization is explicitly out of scope for this resolver — it is a separate authorization path (a candidate session, not an organization membership), documented but not built here (no candidate account exists yet). **Database impact:** none — no migration generated, no schema file touched. **API impact:** none — no new endpoint, no OpenAPI change, no client regeneration needed. **Frontend impact:** none — no route, nav item, or page, per the frozen plan's own explicit "Frontend impact: none yet" for this workstream. Backend: 9 new tests (`recruitmentAuthorization.test.ts`) covering the module-key constant and every pure comparison/resolution function, including priority-order and no-match cases. Does not touch requisitions, vacancies, candidates, applications, interviews, offers, talent pools, reporting, or employee conversion — all remain W46 onward, unimplemented. Does not close the outstanding W39 ESS scope (employment-history/skills aggregation), which remains open.

## Phase 3A Completion Report (W63)

All 19 executed frozen workstreams (W43–W47, W49, W51–W62; W50 formally deferred, not executed) are complete, verified in W62, and closed out by this report. Checking against `docs/PHASE_3A_RECRUITMENT_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §24's Phase Acceptance Criteria, item by item:

- **Every workstream (W43–W62) satisfies its own acceptance criteria.** Yes — each entry in the Phase 3A Progress section above states what was built and how it was verified; W62 re-confirmed all of it still holds together as a whole.
- **Documentation matches implementation.** Yes, as of this report — that is this workstream's (W63's) job.
- **The repository builds successfully and all tests pass.** Yes — W62: 706 backend + 234 frontend tests pass; `api-server`, `hrms`, and `mockup-sandbox` production builds all succeed.
- **No destructive migration risk remains; every migration generated, none applied without your explicit approval.** Yes — migrations `0021`–`0034` (thirty new tables total, detailed below) are purely additive, each with a hand-authored `.down.sql`; none have been applied to any database. W60 and W61 correctly generated no migration at all (pure read models / reuse of already-shipped tables).
- **OpenAPI and generated clients are synchronized.** Yes — W62: zero-diff `orval codegen` across two consecutive runs; all 63 Recruitment routes reconciled 1:1 against `openapi.yaml`, no route missing either direction.
- **Multi-tenant isolation, the permission matrix (§7), module gating (§8), and own-resource authorization are verified for every new entity and route.** Yes — W62's dedicated verification pass, detailed in the Security Verification section below.
- **Public careers routes are confirmed rate-limited, non-leaking on failure, and incapable of cross-tenant disclosure.** Yes — W62 re-read `publicCareers.ts` directly: every route independently resolves its organization by slug, every not-found/not-eligible case returns a generic 404 rather than distinguishing "doesn't exist" from "exists but not visible," and `applyRateLimiter`/`statusCheckRateLimiter` remain in place from W49.
- **The `recruitment` module is live-verified: an enabled org sees the feature, a disabled org gets the designed unavailable state, never a partial or broken page, and disabling the module never deletes or corrupts existing recruitment data.** Verified at the code level for every workstream (W62: `requireModuleEnabled("recruitment")` composed on every non-public internal route, `<ModuleGate moduleKey="recruitment">` on every internal frontend route, ESS's internal-vacancies/My Applications sections degrade independently per W60) — **no live browser walkthrough was performed**, since no provisioned development database or browser environment exists in this environment; this is a code-level, test-suite-backed confirmation, not a live-environment one.
- **The Phase 3A Completion Report (this report) declares zero remaining blockers, or lists them explicitly, and explicitly does not claim the outstanding W39 scope (§22) as resolved.** Declared below.

### Executive Summary

Phase 3A delivered a complete, configurable Recruitment & Hiring module end to end: requisition → approval → vacancy → public/internal application → screening/scoring → pipeline movement → interviews → scorecards → reference/background checks → offers → pre-employment requirements → employee conversion, plus talent pools, candidate notes/tags, an internal (ESS) application path, and read-only dashboard/reporting — on top of the completed Foundation (W1–W21), Phase 2A Core Employee Domain (W22–W31), and Phase 2B HR Operations (W32–W42). Nineteen workstreams (W43–W47, W49, W51–W61) each shipped independently reviewed and tested; W62 verified the phase as a whole; W63 (this report) closes it. W50 (Candidate Accounts & Verification) was a deliberate, approved product decision to defer, not a skip — the anonymous-applicant/signed-status-check experience W49 already delivers remains the fully supported path, and nothing built in W51–W61 needed anything from W50.

**Numbering note (preserved from the Phase 3A Progress section above):** sessions building W45–W47 used a numbering one behind the frozen document's own; a later session was explicitly instructed to align directly with the frozen document's numbering — "W49," not "W48" — so there is no session "W48": the frozen document's own W48 ("Vacancies") is exactly this phase's W47. From W49 onward, session numbering and the frozen document's numbering are identical.

### Workstreams Completed

- **W43 — Recruitment Foundation.** Shared authorization primitives (`recruitmentAuthorization.ts`), module confirmation, two settings permissions — no business table yet.
- **W44 — Recruitment Configuration Foundation.** `recruitment_settings`/`recruitment_workflows`/`recruitment_stages`, admin UI, single-default-workflow invariant.
- **W45 — Job Requisition Foundation.** `job_requisitions`, full lifecycle enum defined now (only draft/pending_approval/cancelled/closed actually written by this phase's pipeline), five-tier visibility.
- **W46 — Requisition Approval Workflow.** Single required approval step, transactional status transition, idempotent double-submit protection.
- **W47 — Vacancy Management Foundation.** `vacancies` (the frozen document's own "W48"), publish/pause/close/archive lifecycle, public-facing `publicId` reserved for W49.
- **W49 — Public Careers Portal.** Unauthenticated slug-scoped listing/detail/apply/status-check, rate-limited, honeypot bot defense, `candidates`/`applications` created here.
- **W50 — Candidate Accounts & Verification — DEFERRED.** Approved product decision; no candidate login/session/account system exists anywhere in this codebase.
- **W51 — Application Pipeline & Stage Movement.** Org-configurable stage movement keyed by system `category`, terminal-state enforcement, immutable `application_stage_history`.
- **W52 — Screening Questions & Scoring.** `vacancy_questions`, knockout auto-scoring, append-only `application_scores` with a computed (never stored) rollup.
- **W53 — Candidate Notes, Tags & Talent Pools.** Assigned/org-wide notes, free-text tags, org-wide-only talent pools.
- **W54 — Interviews & Scheduling.** Scheduling and panel composition only — no evaluation logic (that's W55).
- **W55 — Interview Scorecards.** One scorecard per panel member per interview, draft→submitted→finalized lockable lifecycle, `scorecard.submit` never implies `.read_all` (bias-prevention integrity rule).
- **W56 — Reference & Background Checks.** Reference checks reuse `application.read`/`.manage`; background checks get their own dedicated, organization-wide-only permission pair given highly sensitive result content.
- **W57 — Offers.** Versioned offer envelope, single-step approval, first Recruitment write permission (`offer.manage`) seeded broadly with independent service-layer re-verification.
- **W58 — Pre-Employment Requirements.** Freely bidirectional pending/satisfied/waived checklist, computed `readyForConversion` summary.
- **W59 — Employee Conversion.** `candidate_employee_links` provenance table; extracted the codebase's one authoritative `createEmployee` service; dual-permission gate (`candidate.convert_to_employee` AND `employee.write`).
- **W60 — Employee Self-Service Internal Applications.** `/me/internal-vacancies`, `/me/applications`; reuses W49's pipeline unchanged, zero new tables.
- **W61 — Recruitment Dashboard & Reporting.** Live-computed dashboard + 9-report registry, `recruitment.reports.read`, assigned-vs-organization-wide visibility, zero mutation.
- **W62 — Phase 3A Verification.** Full repo-wide verification pass; zero defects found, zero source changes.

### Features Delivered

**External recruitment lifecycle:** job requisition creation and single-step approval → vacancy publishing (internal/external/both visibility) → public careers listing/detail → anonymous application with resume upload and optional screening answers → internal pipeline review and stage movement → interview scheduling and panel composition → independent, lockable interview scorecards → reference and background check tracking → versioned offer creation, approval, and issuance → pre-employment requirement checklists → controlled conversion to a real employee record via the platform's one authoritative employee-creation service.

**Internal recruitment lifecycle:** an authenticated employee browses internal-visibility vacancies and applies from inside Employee Self-Service, entering the exact same W51 pipeline as an external applicant (tagged `source: "internal_ess"`) — never a second pipeline, never an automatic employee-record change or conversion.

**Collaboration & organization:** candidate notes and tags, reusable organization-wide talent pools.

**Management visibility:** a live Recruitment Dashboard (open requisitions/vacancies, total applicants, pipeline-stage breakdown, recruiter/hiring-manager workload) and a nine-report Recruitment Reports catalog (applicants by vacancy/source, time-to-fill, time-to-hire, age-in-stage, conversion funnel, interview-to-offer ratio, offer-acceptance rate, rejection-reason breakdown) with CSV export — all computed live, nothing cached or stored.

**Candidate-support model:** anonymous application is the fully supported path; a signed, emailable status-check token lets an anonymous applicant check their own application status without any account; no candidate login/session/account system exists (W50 deferred).

### APIs

**New endpoints:** 63 Recruitment routes across recruitment-settings/workflows/stages, job-requisitions (+ submit/cancel/archive/approvals/approve/reject), vacancies (+ publish/pause/close/archive), public careers (`/careers/:orgSlug`...), applications (+ move-stage/reject/withdraw/reopen/scores), candidates (+ notes/tags), talent-pools (+ members), interviews (+ cancel), interview scorecards (+ finalize), reference-checks, background-checks (+ evidence), offers (+ versions/submit-for-approval/approve/issue/withdraw), pre-employment-requirements, employee conversion, ESS internal-vacancies/applications, and recruitment dashboard/reports.

**OpenAPI additions:** matching paths/schemas for every endpoint above, under 17 dedicated tags (`recruitment`, `vacancies`, `applications`, `public-careers`, `candidates`, `talent-pools`, `interviews`, `interview-scorecards`, `reference-checks`, `background-checks`, `offers`, `pre-employment-requirements`, `employee-conversion`, `self-service` (reused for ESS), `recruitment-reporting`, plus the pre-existing `reports` tag reused for the recruitment report registry rows).

**Generated client status:** synchronized — W62 confirmed zero diff between `openapi.yaml` and the generated `lib/api-client-react`/`lib/api-zod` clients, across two consecutive codegen runs.

### Permissions

**Broad-read-tier, service-layer-narrowed (seeded to `org_admin`/`hr_manager`/`employee`, since any employee could be an assigned recruiter or hiring manager):** `requisition.read`, `vacancy.read`, `application.read`, `candidate.read`, `candidate.notes.read`, `interview.read`, `scorecard.submit`, `offer.read`, `offer.manage`, `recruitment.reports.read`.

**Organization-wide administrative writes (`org_admin`/`hr_manager` only):** `recruitment_settings.read`/`.manage`, `requisition.create`/`.update`/`.cancel`/`.approve`, `vacancy.manage`/`.publish`/`.close`, `application.pipeline.move`/`.manage`, `candidate.manage`/`.notes.write`, `talent_pool.read`/`.manage`, `interview.manage`, `scorecard.read_all`/`.finalize`, `background_check.read`/`.manage`, `offer.approve`/`.issue`/`.withdraw`.

**Dual-permission gate:** `candidate.convert_to_employee` — requires **both** this key and the platform's existing `employee.write` on the actual conversion route; neither alone is sufficient, so recruitment cannot silently gain a second employee-creation authority.

**Assigned tiers, resolved in the service layer, never by the permission grant alone:** recruiter/hiring-manager assignment (via `job_requisitions.recruiterEmployeeId`/`hiringManagerEmployeeId`, resolved through the application→vacancy→requisition chain), interview panel membership, an offer's own draft-management exception (the first Recruitment write permission this phase seeded broadly, with independent service-layer re-verification that the caller really is the assigned recruiter/hiring manager or organization-wide), and W61's reporting scope (organization-wide reach signaled by `requisition.update`, everyone else scoped to their own requisition assignment).

**No dedicated recruiter/hiring-manager role exists anywhere in this platform's three-role model** (`org_admin`/`hr_manager`/`employee`) — every "assigned" tier above is resolved via an explicit `recruiterEmployeeId`/`hiringManagerEmployeeId`/panel-membership column comparison, never a role name.

### Module Gating

`recruitment` — seeded in the Foundation's Module Registry (W3) as `status: "hidden"`, live-consumed throughout this phase. Every non-public internal Recruitment route composes `requireModuleEnabled("recruitment")` (verified in W62 across all 15 route files: every route pairs it with a specific `requirePermission` call — module gating never substitutes for authorization). Every internal frontend route wraps in `<ModuleGate moduleKey="recruitment">`. Public careers routes are never module-gated in the request-authorization sense (no membership exists to check) — they independently check `recruitment_settings.enabled` and degrade to a generic "not available" response. ESS's Internal Vacancies/My Applications sections check `recruitment` module accessibility independently within the ESS page (gated only by `employee_self_service` itself), degrading to a controlled message rather than a broken page when `recruitment` is disabled.

### Security Verification

Per W62's dedicated verification pass:

- **Tenant isolation:** every Recruitment table/query scoped by `organizationId`; cross-organization access fails safely (404, never distinguishing "doesn't exist" from "exists but not visible"); public careers routes resolve organization solely by slug, independently, per request.
- **Module gating:** `recruitment` confirmed backend- and frontend-gated on every internal route/page; never used as a substitute for permission checks (paired with `requirePermission` on every route, verified by direct count across all 15 route files).
- **Permission model & assigned/organization-wide scope:** the full permission matrix re-read against `seed-roles-permissions.ts`; assigned recruiter/hiring-manager/panel/offer-draft/reporting scopes all resolve through the shared `recruitmentAuthorization.ts` primitives or an equivalent per-resource resolver — no unrelated employee can reach organization-wide reach without holding the specific admin-only signal for that resource.
- **Public boundary:** `publicCareers.ts` DTOs confirmed to return only `publicId`s, labels, dates, and text — never an internal numeric row id, salary range, or recruiter/hiring-manager identity.
- **Sensitive-data isolation:** no candidate PII, referee contact, background-check result, note, scorecard comment, or storage key appears in any dashboard/report DTO (grep-confirmed); offer `generatedDocumentStorageKey` and scorecard `externalInterviewerToken` remain stripped from every response per their own workstreams' precedent.
- **Candidate account boundary (W50):** confirmed no candidate login/session/account code exists anywhere — only the reserved, unused `requireCandidateAccount` settings toggle.
- **Employee conversion boundary:** confirmed the dual `candidate.convert_to_employee` + `employee.write` gate remains enforced, `createEmployee` (the platform's one authoritative service) is reused unchanged, and no conversion route touches payroll, leave, attendance, onboarding, or user-account provisioning.
- **Read-only reporting:** `lib/recruitmentReporting.ts` confirmed to contain zero insert/update/delete calls — every dashboard/report figure is computed live per request, never cached or stored.
- **Immutability/history:** `application_stage_history`, `requisition_approvals`, `offer_approvals`, and finalized `interview_scorecards`/`interview_scorecard_responses` remain append-only/locked, matching each workstream's own design; `candidate_employee_links` remains immutable once created.
- **Idempotency/concurrency:** key unique constraints (`applications_candidate_vacancy_unique`, `offers_application_unique`, `pre_employment_requirements_application_code_unique`, `candidate_employee_links_application_unique`/`_employee_unique`) confirmed present in the actual migration SQL.

This is a code-level and automated-test-suite verification, **not** a penetration test or a production security certification.

- **W50 — Candidate Accounts & Verification — deferred, not completed.** An approved product decision (candidate accounts aren't required for the minimum useful recruitment product W49 already delivers). Remains open pending separate approval and scheduling.
- **W39 — employment-history and skills/qualifications/certifications aggregation into Employee Self-Service — deferred, not completed.** Unchanged since Phase 2B; none of W43–W61 touched or closed this item, since no Recruitment workstream's frozen scope assigns it anything beyond internal-vacancy visibility/application (W60).
- **Migrations `0021`–`0034` remain unapplied**, awaiting your explicit approval before being run against any database.
- **No live browser/database walkthrough was performed for any Phase 3A workstream**, including this verification — this environment has never had a provisioned development database. Live functional QA remains required once a real environment exists, before any production rollout.

### Technical Debt

None beyond the two explicitly deferred items above (W50, W39), which are scope exclusions rather than defects, and the known, pre-existing pipeline gap already documented in the W61 entry: no route anywhere in this codebase currently transitions a `job_requisitions.status` to `filled`/`partially_filled`, or an `offer_versions.status` to `accepted`/`declined` — both are schema-valid, frozen-plan-defined states with no route built to reach them yet (fulfillment tracking and candidate-facing offer acceptance were never in any Phase 3A workstream's scope). W61's `time_to_fill`/`offer_acceptance_rate` reports are honestly empty/null as a result, not a defect — no workaround, shortcut, or quality compromise was introduced anywhere in W43–W62.

Nothing in the Definition of Complete / §24 Phase Acceptance Criteria remains outstanding**, aside from the two explicitly deferred items above (W50, W39), migrations `0021`–`0034` awaiting your explicit approval, and live functional QA once a real database/environment exists. No implementation is proposed here.

- **W40 — HR Operations Dashboard — Complete.** Extends `GET /dashboard/summary` (W18) with a new `leaveMetrics` field — real, tenant-scoped aggregates computed live from W33/W34/W37's existing tables (`leave_requests`, `leave_balance_entries`, `public_holidays`), no new table, no cache. New `lib/leaveDashboardMetrics.ts` (`getLeaveDashboardMetrics`) mirrors `leaveCalendar.ts`'s (W36) read-model convention — candidate rows fetched via a scoped `WHERE`, reduced in application code — rather than introducing raw SQL aggregate functions as a new pattern. Seven figures, exactly the frozen scope: `employeesOnLeave` (distinct employees with an approved request spanning today), `upcomingApprovedLeave` (approved requests starting within a shared 30-day window, not yet started), `pendingApprovalCount` (reuses W35's existing `listPendingApprovals` unchanged), `upcomingPublicHolidays` (reuses W37's existing `listHolidayOccurrencesInRange`, org-wide by definition), `leaveUtilizationPercent` (ledger usage ÷ ledger opening_balance+accrual+carry_forward credits, reconstructed the same way `getEmployeeBalances`/W34 reconstructs a balance — never stored), `expiringCarryForwardBalances` (carry-forward ledger entries expiring within the window, counted only under a policy whose `carryForwardExpiryMonths` is actually set — the frozen plan's explicit exclusion), and `requestsByStatus` (a `{pending,approved,rejected,cancelled}` breakdown). Scoping mirrors W35/W36's existing own+managed/org-wide model exactly (`resolveLeaveDashboardMetrics` in `routes/users.ts`): an HR admin (`leave_request.manage`) sees every figure org-wide; anyone else sees their own + direct reports' (the same `reportingManagerId`-derived scope `leaveCalendar.ts`'s `visibleEmployeeIds` already establishes) — verified directly that a manager cannot see another manager's team. `leaveMetrics` is `null` (never a zero-filled placeholder) when the `leave` module is disabled for the organization, or when there's no resolvable active membership — verified directly; an org with the module enabled but no leave data yet gets real, non-null zeros.
