Frontend: a new "Leave" section on the existing Dashboard page, omitted entirely (not rendered with fabricated zeros) when `leaveMetrics` is null — six new tiles plus a requests-by-status badge row, same design system/card pattern as the existing stats grid. Database impact: none. Backend: 11 new tests (`dashboardLeaveMetrics.test.ts`) covering unauthenticated rejection, module-disabled null, no-active-membership null, empty-org zeros, org-wide vs manager-scoped tenant isolation, utilization/carry-forward-expiry calculation correctness, cross-organization non-leakage, and response field exclusivity. Frontend: 4 new tests (`dashboard.test.tsx`) covering the loading state, the disabled-module omission, full-tile rendering, and a non-crashing error state. Does not touch attendance-capture metrics (W38 is configuration-only, no clock-in data exists to aggregate) or any W41+ scope.
- **W39 — Employee Self-Service — Complete (narrowed scope; see note).** A consolidated, read-mostly self-service surface at `/self-service` (`<ModuleGate moduleKey="employee_self_service">`) with three sections: My Profile, My Leave, My Documents. **Note on scope:** the frozen plan's W39 bullet also lists employment history (W25–W27's `employment_periods`) and Skills/Qualifications/Certifications (W24) aggregation; this pass implemented only Profile/Leave/Documents per this session's explicit, narrower instruction — History/Skills aggregation into ESS remains open and undone, not silently dropped. Employee resolution: new `GET /me/employee` (`routes/me.ts`), the frozen plan's named convenience endpoint — no equivalent existed. Resolves via a new `requireActiveOrganizationMembership` middleware (mirrors `GET /me`'s `resolveActiveOrganizationId`, since this route has no URL `:organizationId` to key off of, unlike every other module-gated route) composed with `requireModuleEnabled("employee_self_service")`, then `lib/employeeSelfService.ts`'s `resolveOwnEmployeeProfile` — which itself reuses W33's existing `resolveOwnEmployeeId` (employee_user_links, W14) and `getEmployeeById`, never a client-supplied employee ID. Returns `{ linked, employee }`: `linked: false` (not 404/500) is the intentional response for an unlinked user, a stale link resolving to another organization's employee, or no live active membership at all — all three verified directly. Response is a new, deliberately restricted `SelfServiceEmployeeProfile` (own new interface, not the HR administrator `Employee` DTO) — omits `notes` (employee.notes.read is HR-only), `nationalId`/`passportNumber` (sensitive identifiers), `linkedApplicationUserId`/`createdBy`/`updatedBy`/`createdAt`/`updatedAt` (internal bookkeeping), and `organizationId` (redundant) — verified by a dedicated field-exclusion test. My Leave reuses W33/W34's existing own-scoped endpoints unchanged (`my-leave.tsx`, embedded as a tab) — updated to resolve its employee via the new `/me/employee` instead of its prior `GET /employees` roster-scan workaround (that page's own comment named this as W39's job). My Documents reuses W23's existing `GET .../employees/:employeeId/documents` read-only, with the resolved own employeeId — no new document capability, no upload/download UI here (download doesn't exist anywhere in this codebase yet, HR-admin included). Separated employees see their historical profile/leave/documents unchanged; only new leave submission is rejected, by the existing W33 validation, not anything new here. Module gating: `employee_self_service` gates the whole ESS route and the `/me/employee` endpoint; `leave` (a separate module) is checked independently inside the page for the My Leave tab only — when ESS is enabled but Leave is disabled, that tab shows a controlled "Leave isn't enabled" message instead of calling any leave endpoint, rather than the whole page redirecting. Permissions: none new, per the frozen plan — self-scoped entirely through the authenticated identity plus the employee-user link, never a broad `employee.read`/`employee.write`/`leave_request.manage` grant. Nav: `/my-leave`'s nav entry is replaced by "Employee Self-Service" pointing at `/self-service` (avoids duplicating the same self-service entry point twice); the `/my-leave` route itself stays mounted for compatibility. Database impact: none — no new table, no migration. Backend: 8 new tests (`meEmployee.test.ts`) covering own-only resolution, unlinked-safe response, cross-org non-leakage, module-disabled 403, unauthenticated 401, and DTO field exclusion. Frontend: 7 new tests (`employee-self-service.test.tsx`) covering loading/error states, the unlinked contact-HR message, all three tabs rendering, no profile edit controls, and the disabled-Leave-module fallback.
- **W38 — Attendance Configuration — Complete.** Configuration only, per the frozen plan's explicit scope — no clock-in/out, no capture, no timesheets, no biometric/device integration, none of which is built here or anywhere in this workstream. Directly reuses the Organization Configuration Engine (W2/ADR-009): a new `attendance` namespace registered into `CONFIG_NAMESPACES` (`artifacts/api-server/src/services/organizationConfig.ts`) alongside `general`/`terminology` — no new table, same validated/versioned-row pattern (`organization_settings`, one row per org/namespace). Fields are exactly the frozen scope: `workStartTime`/`workEndTime` (plain `"HH:MM"` 24-hour civil-time strings, not timestamps — there is no instant to attach a timezone to, matching Architecture Principle 7's date-handling precedent from Leave), `gracePeriodMinutes` (0–180), and `workDays` (an array of weekday names). Server-side validation rejects a malformed time (regex-checked) and a `workStartTime` not earlier than `workEndTime`, applied to the *merged* result on every `PATCH` (the engine's existing merge-then-validate behavior, unchanged) so a partial update can never leave a stored config that wouldn't itself pass validation. No cross-midnight support, no schedule-applicability/assignment model, no effective-dated history, and no per-branch/department/position/employee variation — none of that is in the frozen W38 scope (a single organization-wide row, like `general`/`terminology`), and building it here would have been scope creep beyond what W38 defines; a future workstream can extend this if that scope is ever approved. Module-gated: the shared `GET/PATCH .../organization-settings/:namespace` route now runs a new `requireNamespaceModuleEnabled` middleware (after `requireMembership`, before `requirePermission`, mirroring every other module-gated route) that looks up each namespace's own `moduleKey` and enforces `requireModuleEnabled` only when one is set — `attendance` is gated behind the `attendance` module (still registry `status: "hidden"`, same as `leave` was through W32–W37 — hidden only affects catalog visibility, not per-org enablement); `general`/`terminology` remain ungated exactly as before, verified by regression test. Permissions: none new — reuses the existing `organization.read`/`organization.update` the config route already enforced (seeded to `org_admin`/`super_admin` for `.update`; every role has `.read`), per the frozen plan's explicit instruction to reuse rather than invent. New "Attendance Settings" page (`/attendance-settings`, `<ModuleGate moduleKey="attendance">` via `SecureRoute`) with a typed form (start/end time, grace period, work-day checkboxes) instead of the raw-JSON editor `general`/`terminology` use in the Admin console — nav link shown only to `org_admin`/`super_admin` (UX convenience only; the server-side permission is the real gate). Does not touch Employee Self-Service, the Leave module, or any other Phase 2B workstream. Database impact: none — reuses `organization_settings`, no migration generated, matching the frozen plan's stated impact exactly.
- **W32 — Leave Types & Policy Engine — Complete.** Two independent, org-scoped entities per the frozen plan: `leave_types` (name/code, active/inactive archive lifecycle, mirrors `branches`'/W13's exact shape) and `leave_policies` (eligibility + entitlement rules attached to a type via a cascading `leaveTypeId`; a type may have zero, one, or several policies). Eligibility dimensions — employment type, branch, department, position, gender, minimum service months, probation-restricted — reuse `employees.ts`'s existing `employmentTypeEnum`/`genderEnum` rather than redefining them; each nullable dimension means "applies to all," the same convention departments/positions already use. Grade/employee-category eligibility omitted (no such concept exists in this schema). Entitlement/accrual/carry-forward/request-constraint/day-counting-rule fields all present per the frozen scope. Cross-org reference validation reuses `assertBelongsToOrganization` (ADR-012), extended to cover `leaveTypesTable` for the first time. Server-side validation rejects invalid effective-date ranges, negative entitlement/accrual values, and `carryForwardAllowed` without a `maxCarryForwardDays`. First production consumer of Module Gating (W5/W6): every route composes `requireMembership` + `requireModuleEnabled("leave")` + `requirePermission`; the "Leave Types" admin page is wrapped in `<ModuleGate moduleKey="leave">`. New `leave_type.read`/`leave_type.manage` permissions (seeded for `org_admin`/`hr_manager`/`super_admin`; `employee` role gets `leave_type.read` only, matching its existing read-only-reference-data pattern) — no permissions broadened. Audit-logged (`leave_type.created/updated/archived/reactivated`, `leave_policy.created/updated/archived/reactivated`). Does not implement Leave Requests, Balances, Approval, Calendar, Public Holidays, Attendance, or ESS — those remain W33+. Migration `0016_little_jackal.sql` (two new tables + four new enums, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied.
- **W37 — Public Holiday Management — Complete.** New org-scoped `public_holidays` table — `scope` accepts `organization | branch | region | national` in the schema but every write is hardcoded to `"organization"` server-side regardless of input (Phase 2B's frozen exclusion; no code assumes it always will be, per the frozen plan's own instruction). A recurring holiday stores exactly one row (a month/day template); it is never expanded into permanent per-year rows — `listHolidayOccurrencesInRange` (new `lib/publicHolidays.ts`) expands it at query time via plain `"YYYY-MM-DD"` string arithmetic (Feb 29 falls back to Feb 28 in a non-leap year), so nothing here is ever timezone-dependent. `effectiveYear` is required for a one-off holiday (validated to match `date`'s year) and rejected for a recurring one; `observedDate` (a one-off holiday shifted to a weekday) is likewise rejected for a recurring one, since a single column can't represent a per-year shift. Integrates with W33's day calculation at exactly its reserved extension point: `createLeaveRequest` now calls `resolveHolidayDatesInRange` and passes the result into `calculateLeaveDays`'s pre-existing `publicHolidayDates` parameter — no schema or signature change, and a day that's both a weekend and a holiday is only ever `continue`'d once (never double-subtracted), verified directly. Historical consistency is structural, not policy: nothing anywhere re-reads holidays against an already-submitted request, so creating/editing/archiving/deleting a holiday can never reinterpret a prior `daysRequested` (W33's stored value is the only source of truth for that request, forever). Overlays on W36's calendar as a genuinely separate response field — `GET .../leave-calendar` now returns `{ leaveEntries, holidays }` instead of a bare array — never merged into `leaveEntries` and never shaped like a `LeaveRequest`; same authorization/date-range/privacy rules as before, holidays are just org-wide visible data with no employee attached. Permissions are new and deliberately distinct: `public_holiday.read` (seeded to every role including base `employee`) and `public_holiday.manage` (org_admin/hr_manager only) — holding `leave_request.approve` or `leave_type.manage` grants nothing here, verified directly. CRUD mirrors `leaveTypes.ts`'s archive/reactivate shape, plus a genuine hard `DELETE` (safe: no FK anywhere references `public_holidays`, unlike `leave_types`/`leave_policies`, which `leave_requests` does reference — deactivate remains the recommended path in both the API docs and the UI, delete is secondary). New "Public Holidays" admin page (list/create/edit/deactivate/reactivate/delete, management controls hidden for non-HR roles) and a holiday overlay on the existing "Leave Calendar" page (visually distinct chips, never rendered as if they were an employee's leave). Migration `0020_unknown_scarecrow.sql` (one new table + two new enums, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied.
- **W36 — Leave Calendar — Complete.** A pure read model over `leave_requests` — no new table, no cache, no migration (`Database impact: none` per the frozen plan), nothing writable. New `GET .../organizations/:organizationId/leave-calendar?from=&to=&departmentId=&branchId=` computes results live in application code (mirrors W17's Reporting Foundation approach), filtered to `status = "approved"` only — pending, rejected, cancelled, and withdrawn requests never appear, so the calendar can never present unconfirmed leave as booked. Overlap uses the same plain `"YYYY-MM-DD"` string comparison W33's own overlap check already relies on (`request.startDate <= to AND request.endDate >= from`) — no timezone conversion is possible since these columns have no time-of-day component, and the date range is server-validated (both params required, `from <= to`, capped at 100 days) to prevent an unbounded scan. Reuses W33/W35's exact own/manager-scoped/org-wide authorization tiers (`leave_request.read.own` coarse gate, `leave_request.manage` for org-wide, `employees.reportingManagerId` for manager scope, no new manager model or permission) — a manager's calendar shows their own approved leave plus direct reports' only; `leave_request.manage` (view) is not conflated with `leave_request.approve` (W35) anywhere in this workstream. Response shape is a new, deliberately narrow `LeaveCalendarEntry` (id, employeeId, employeeName, leaveTypeId, leaveTypeName, startDate, endDate, daysRequested) — never the full `LeaveRequest`, so `reason`, `attachmentDocumentId`, and any approval/ledger detail are structurally impossible to leak through this endpoint. `approveLeaveRequest`/`rejectLeaveRequest` (W35) are not touched or duplicated — the calendar has no approve/reject affordance at all. New "Leave Calendar" page (month view, prev/next/today navigation, department/branch filters) computes its own local-calendar-date grid without ever routing through `toISOString()` (which converts via UTC and can silently shift the displayed date by a day depending on the browser's offset) — `<ModuleGate moduleKey="leave">`, shown to every user like "My Leave"/"Leave Approvals" since visibility narrows server-side regardless of who can see the nav link. Does not implement week/day/agenda view toggles, employee/leave-type filters, external calendar sync, ICS export, or any public-holiday overlay — W37 doesn't exist yet; the calendar reads `leave_requests` directly so a future holiday layer can overlay on top without any change to this read model. No migration generated.
- **W35 — Leave Approval Workflow — Complete.** Single-level approve/reject only, per the frozen plan — `leave_requests` gains exactly the three columns it specifies (`approvedBy`, `approvedAt`, `rejectionReason`); a rejection's own actor/timestamp is deliberately not duplicated onto the row, since it already lives in the `audit_events` row the decision is recorded through. State machine is a conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE status = 'pending'` inside a `db.transaction` — a concurrent or repeated decision sees zero rows affected and fails as `LeaveRequestNotPendingError` (409) rather than double-processing; this is the row-lock Postgres itself provides, not application-level locking. Approval's balance-sufficiency check and the immutable `usage` ledger entry (posted through W34's ledger via new `postApprovedUsageEntry`/`getAvailableBalance` helpers, both accepting the transaction's client) run inside that same transaction, so an insufficient-balance rejection rolls back the status change too — no approved request can exist without its ledger entry, and no ledger entry can exist without a successful approval. The ledger's own `(relatedLeaveRequestId, entryType)` unique index (W34) is a second, independent duplicate-post guard. `approveLeaveRequest`/`rejectLeaveRequest`/`listPendingApprovals` live in a new `leaveApprovals.ts` rather than `leaveRequests.ts` or `leaveBalances.ts` — those two already depend on each other in one direction (balances resolves policy via requests), and either importing back from here would create a circular module dependency. Authorization is the union of manager-scoped (`employees.reportingManagerId`, the same data W33 already reads — no second manager model) and HR organization-wide (`leave_request.manage`, re-checked at the moment of decision, never cached), gated additionally by a new `leave_request.approve` permission seeded to every role including base `employee` — the coarse-permission-plus-fine-grained-check pattern W33/W34 already established, since any plain employee could be someone's manager via `reportingManagerId`. Self-approval (and self-rejection) is rejected regardless of tier. Holding `leave_request.manage` (view access) or `leave_type.manage` (policy administration) does not by itself grant approval — `leave_request.approve` plus the manager/org-wide check is required. No new "decision history" table — the frozen plan's `Database impact: additive columns on leave_requests (no new table)` is authoritative; history is the existing immutable `audit_events` (`leave_request.approved`/`.rejected`, W16's reader already exposes it) plus the request row's own final-decision columns. New `GET .../leave-requests/pending-approvals` (org-scoped, not employee-scoped) fills the gap between the frozen plan's two-endpoint API list and its required "Pending Approvals" UI — scoped server-side to direct reports or org-wide exactly like every other authorization check here, so nothing further needs hiding client-side. Fourth production consumer of Module Gating: `requireModuleEnabled("leave")` on every route; new "Leave Approvals" page uses `<ModuleGate moduleKey="leave">`, shown to every user (like "My Leave") since manager status isn't a client-visible role. Does not implement multi-level/sequential approval chains, delegation, escalation, substitution, or any cancellation/reversal of an already-approved request — none of that is in the frozen W35 scope. Migration `0019_glossy_odin.sql` (three new nullable columns + one FK, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied.
- **W34 — Leave Balance Engine (Ledger) — Complete.** New `leave_balance_entries` table — append-only and immutable by construction: no route or service function ever `UPDATE`s or `DELETE`s a posted row; a mistake is corrected with a compensating `reversal` entry. Seven entry types per the frozen enum (`opening_balance`, `accrual`, `carry_forward`, `usage`, `reversal`, `expiry`, `manual_adjustment`); this workstream's API only ever posts `manual_adjustment` — accrual/carry-forward/expiry postings and the automatic `usage` posting on approval are explicitly reserved for W35/a later accrual scheduler, not built here. A displayed balance is never a stored column — `getEmployeeBalances` always reconstructs it live as `SUM(amount)` grouped by leave type. `leavePolicyId` is resolved server-side by the single internal `postLedgerEntry` primitive, never client-supplied: for a request-related posting it's taken from the leave request's own already-resolved policy (so a later policy change can never reinterpret history, per the frozen plan's Historical Consistency rule); otherwise it's freshly resolved via W33's `resolveApplicablePolicy`, reused unchanged. Integrity is enforced by two partial unique indexes rather than application-level locking alone: `(relatedLeaveRequestId, entryType)` prevents duplicate usage postings and double reversals for the same request; `(organizationId, employeeId, leaveTypeId, entryType, sourceReference)` prevents duplicate opening balances and duplicate accrual for the same employee/policy/period — a caller supplies a stable `sourceReference` idempotency key (e.g. `"accrual:2027-01"`) for postings meant to be safely retried. A unique-constraint violation under concurrent/retried posting is caught and translated into `DuplicateLedgerEntryError` (new `isUniqueViolation`-based translation, same `dbErrors.ts` precedent already used elsewhere) rather than silently double-posting. Amount sign direction is validated in the service layer (opening_balance/accrual/carry_forward must be positive, usage/expiry must be negative, reversal/manual_adjustment may be either but never zero) — not a schema constraint, since Postgres `CHECK` can't vary by another column's enum value without a trigger, and the frozen plan didn't call for one. Permissions: no new permissions — reuses W33's `leave_request.read.own` (own/manager-scoped read, same `reportingManagerId`-based tier) and `leave_request.manage` (HR org-wide read **and** the only permission that can post a manual adjustment; own/manager tiers cannot adjust, per the frozen plan). A manual adjustment requires a non-empty `reason` (enforced in both the OpenAPI schema and the service layer) and is always audit-logged (`leave_balance.manual_adjustment`) — routine ledger postings are not, since they're self-documenting through the ledger's own reason/actor/timestamp columns (Architecture Principle 6). Third production consumer of Module Gating: `requireModuleEnabled("leave")` on every route; new "Leave Balances" HR admin page (own balances also surfaced on the existing "My Leave" page) wrapped in `<ModuleGate moduleKey="leave">`, nav-gated to org_admin/hr_manager/super_admin as a UX convenience (server-side `leave_request.manage` is the real enforcement). Does not implement approval/rejection, automatic deduction on approval, an accrual scheduler, the leave calendar, or public holidays — all explicitly excluded, reserved for W35 or later. Migration `0018_fat_obadiah_stane.sql` (one new table + one new enum, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied.
- **W33 — Leave Requests — Complete.** New org-scoped `leave_requests` table (`employeeId` cascades; `leaveTypeId`/`leavePolicyId` restrict) — create/list/withdraw only, per the frozen scope; approval (W35) and balance deduction (W34, which doesn't exist yet) are explicitly not implemented here. `startDate`/`endDate` are plain `date` columns (Architecture Principle 7), not `timestamp with timezone` — the first schema-level application of that principle. `leavePolicyId` is always resolved server-side (never client-supplied) by `resolveApplicablePolicy`, which reuses W32's `leave_policies` and applies the frozen precedence (position > department > branch > employment type > org-wide default) against the employee's `employmentType`/`branchId`/`departmentId`/`positionId`/`gender`/`hireDate`/`employmentStatus`. `calculateLeaveDays` computes the day count entirely server-side from the resolved policy's weekend/holiday-counting flags — the client's arithmetic is never trusted; a `publicHolidayDates` parameter exists now, always empty, so W37 only has to start passing real dates in, no schema or signature change. Full server-side validation: date-order, policy effective-date window, min/max duration, notice period, probation restriction (via eligibility), attachment requirement (reuses W23's `employee_documents`), overlapping/duplicate pending-or-approved requests, and cross-org references on every FK. Introduces the plan's "own / manager-scoped / HR org-wide" access tiers for the first time: new `leave_request.read.own`/`leave_request.write.own` (seeded to every role, including base `employee`) and `leave_request.manage` (HR org-wide); manager-scoped read reuses the existing `employees.reportingManagerId` column, no new permission. "Which employee is me" reuses W14's `employee_user_links` (`resolveOwnEmployeeId`), not a new endpoint — the frontend "My Leave" page resolves its own employee by reusing the existing `GET /employees` roster and matching `linkedApplicationUserId`, since the dedicated `/me/employee` convenience endpoint is explicitly W39's job, not W33's. Second production consumer of Module Gating: `requireModuleEnabled("leave")` on every route, `<ModuleGate moduleKey="leave">` on the new "My Leave" page. Audit-logged (`leave_request.submitted`/`.cancelled`); notification integration points documented, not implemented (Architecture Principle 8). Migration `0017_tidy_mongoose.sql` (one new table + one new enum, purely additive) generated with a hand-authored `.down.sql`, not applied.

## Phase 2B Completion Report (W42)

All 11 frozen workstreams (W32–W42) are complete. Checking against `docs/PHASE_2B_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s Definition of Done, item by item:

- **Every workstream (W32–W42) satisfies its acceptance criteria.** Yes — each entry above states what was built and how it was verified.
- **Documentation matches implementation.** Yes, as of this report — that was W42's job.
- **Repository builds successfully; all tests pass.** Yes — verified in W41: 353 backend + 53 frontend tests, production build succeeds (api-server, hrms, mockup-sandbox).
- **No destructive migration risk remains; every migration generated, none applied without your explicit approval.** Yes — migrations `0016`–`0020` (five new tables/column sets, detailed below) are purely additive, each with a hand-authored `.down.sql`; none have been applied to any database.
- **OpenAPI and generated clients are synchronized.** Yes — verified in W41 (zero-diff codegen).
- **Multi-tenant isolation, the full five-tier permission model (own / manager-scoped / HR org-wide / approval authority / policy administration), audit logging, and module gating are verified for every new entity and route.** Yes — W41's verification pass, plus a closed coverage gap for `leave_policies` cross-org isolation (`leavePoliciesTenantIsolation.test.ts`).
- **The leave ledger is proven reconstructable from history alone, with no duplicate accrual, deduction, or reversal possible under concurrency.** Yes — W34's append-only ledger design plus W35's transactional approval, both re-confirmed still green in W41.
- **The `leave`, `attendance`, and `employee_self_service` modules are live-verified: enabled orgs see the feature, disabled orgs get a 403/redirect or the module's designed unavailable/null state (not a partial or broken page), and disabling a module never deletes or corrupts existing data.** Yes — W41.
- **Phase 2B Completion Report declares zero remaining blockers.** Yes — see verdict below.

### Executive Summary

Phase 2B delivered the Leave module end-to-end (types/policies, requests, ledger-based balances, approval workflow, calendar, public holidays), an Attendance configuration layer (no capture), a consolidated Employee Self-Service surface, and real Leave metrics on the HR Operations Dashboard — on top of the completed Foundation (W1–W21) and Phase 2A Core Employee Domain (W22–W31). Ten workstreams (W32–W41) each shipped independently reviewed and tested; W42 (this report) closes the phase.

### Workstreams Completed

- **W32 — Leave Types & Policy Engine.** `leave_types`/`leave_policies`, eligibility precedence resolution, first production consumer of Module Gating.
- **W33 — Leave Requests.** Own-employee create/list/withdraw, full server-side validation, own/manager/org-wide access tiers introduced.
- **W34 — Leave Balance Engine (Ledger).** Append-only `leave_balance_entries`, balances always reconstructed from history, duplicate-posting guarded by unique indexes.
- **W35 — Leave Approval Workflow.** Single-level approve/reject, transactional with ledger posting, idempotent under concurrent decisions.
- **W36 — Leave Calendar.** Read-only aggregation over approved requests; no new table.
- **W37 — Public Holiday Management.** Org-scoped `public_holidays`, integrated into day-count exclusion; calendar overlay.
- **W38 — Attendance Configuration.** Config-only `attendance` namespace on the existing Organization Configuration Engine; no capture.
- **W39 — Employee Self-Service (narrowed scope).** Consolidated `/self-service` (My Profile, My Leave, My Documents) and `GET /me/employee`; employment-history and skills/qualifications/certifications aggregation deferred (see Outstanding Items).
- **W40 — HR Operations Dashboard.** Real, tenant-scoped Leave metrics extending `GET /dashboard/summary`; null (not zero-filled) when Leave is disabled.
- **W41 — Phase 2B Verification.** Full repo verification plus module-gating/tenant-isolation/own-resource-authorization checks; one coverage gap closed (`leave_policies` tenant isolation test).

### Features Delivered

Leave Types & Policies administration; employee leave request submission/withdrawal; ledger-based leave balances with HR manual adjustment; single-level approval/rejection with balance deduction; a read-only Leave Calendar; Public Holiday administration integrated into day-count exclusion; organization-wide Attendance configuration (work hours/days/grace period); a consolidated Employee Self-Service portal (profile, leave, documents); and real Leave metrics on the HR Operations Dashboard (employees on leave, upcoming leave, pending approvals, upcoming holidays, utilization, expiring carry-forward, requests-by-status).

### Database

**Migrations `0016`–`0020`, unapplied:**
- `0016_little_jackal.sql` — `leave_types`, `leave_policies` + four new enums (W32)
- `0017_tidy_mongoose.sql` — `leave_requests` + one new enum (W33)
- `0018_fat_obadiah_stane.sql` — `leave_balance_entries` + one new enum (W34)
- `0019_glossy_odin.sql` — three additive columns + one FK on `leave_requests` (W35)
- `0020_unknown_scarecrow.sql` — `public_holidays` + two new enums (W37)

W36, W38, W39, W40, and W41 required no migration (pure read models, an existing config-engine namespace, or reuse of already-shipped tables). All five migrations are purely additive, each with a hand-authored `.down.sql`. **None have been applied to any database**, per CLAUDE.md's Database Rules, awaiting your explicit approval — same posture as every Foundation and Phase 2A migration before them.

**Current migration status:** journal `0000`–`0020` sequential, no gaps; zero drift confirmed in W41 (`drizzle-kit generate` → "No schema changes, nothing to migrate").

**Schema impact:** three new tables plus additive columns on one existing table (`leave_requests`); no destructive change anywhere in Phase 2B.

### APIs

**New endpoints:** leave-types/policies CRUD + archive/reactivate; leave-requests create/list/cancel; leave-balances summary/ledger/manual-adjustment; leave-approvals pending-list/approve/reject; leave-calendar read; public-holidays CRUD + archive/reactivate/delete; organization-settings `attendance` namespace (extends the existing config-engine route); `GET /me/employee`; `GET /dashboard/summary` extended with `leaveMetrics`.

**OpenAPI additions:** new paths/schemas for every endpoint above, including `SelfServiceEmployeeProfile`, `MyEmployeeResponse`, `LeaveDashboardMetrics`, `LeaveRequestsByStatusCounts`.

**Generated client status:** synchronized — W41 confirmed zero diff between `openapi.yaml` and the generated `lib/api-client-react`/`lib/api-zod` clients.

### Permissions

**Added:** `leave_type.read`/`leave_type.manage` (W32); `leave_request.read.own`/`leave_request.write.own`/`leave_request.manage` (W33); `leave_request.approve` (W35); `public_holiday.read`/`public_holiday.manage` (W37).

**Reused, none added:** W34 (existing `leave_request.read.own`/`.manage`); W36 (existing leave tiers); W38 (existing `organization.read`/`.update`); W39 (self-scoped via identity + employee-user link, no permission-based grant); W40 (existing dashboard gating, `requireAuth` only); W41 (verification only).

### Module Gates

`leave`, `attendance`, and `employee_self_service` — all three seeded in the Foundation's Module Registry (W3) as `status: "hidden"`, now live-consumed for the first time in this phase. Every Leave route composes `requireModuleEnabled("leave")`; the Attendance config route's shared `GET/PATCH .../organization-settings/:namespace` gates only the `attendance` namespace via a per-namespace `requireNamespaceModuleEnabled`; `/self-service` and `GET /me/employee` gate on `employee_self_service`. Frontend: `<ModuleGate>` on every corresponding page/route; ESS's My Leave tab and the Dashboard's Leave section degrade to a controlled message/omission when their dependency module is disabled, rather than redirecting or rendering fabricated zeros.

### Testing Summary

- **Backend:** 353/353 passing.
- **Frontend:** 53/53 passing.
- **Typecheck:** clean across every workspace.
- **Lint:** clean (`@workspace/hrms`, the only package with a configured linter).
- **Build:** succeeds for api-server, hrms, mockup-sandbox.
- **Code generation:** `orval codegen` against `openapi.yaml` produces zero diff in the generated clients.

(All totals per the W41 verification pass; not re-run for this documentation-only report, per instruction.)

### Security Verification

- **Tenant isolation:** confirmed for every table this phase introduced (`leave_types`, `leave_policies`, `leave_requests`, `leave_balance_entries`, `public_holidays`) — including a coverage gap closed in W41 for `leave_policies` (direct cross-org mutation attempt now tested, confirmed rejected with `LeavePolicyNotFoundError`).
- **Module gating:** `leave`/`attendance`/`employee_self_service` confirmed both backend (disabled module blocks or returns the designed null/unavailable state) and frontend (no broken or partial page) — verified W41.
- **Own-resource authorization:** own/manager-scoped/HR org-wide tiers (W33–W36, W40) and W39's own-employee-only resolution (never a client-supplied employee ID) confirmed via existing test suites — no gap found.

### Outstanding Items

- **W39 — employment-history aggregation into Employee Self-Service** (W25–W27's `employment_periods`, via `listEmploymentPeriods`) — **deferred, not completed.** Remains open pending separate approval.
- **W39 — skills/qualifications/certifications aggregation into Employee Self-Service** (W24's `employee_skills`/`employee_qualifications`/`employee_certifications`) — **deferred, not completed.** Remains open pending separate approval.

### Technical Debt

None beyond the two explicitly deferred W39 items above, which are scope exclusions rather than defects — no known workaround, shortcut, or quality compromise was introduced anywhere in W32–W41.

### Phase Assessment

- **Feature Complete:** Yes, for the frozen Phase 2B scope as narrowed for W39 (Profile/Leave/Documents; History/Skills deferred).
- **Verified:** Yes — W41's full verification pass, referenced above.
- **Ready for the next development phase:** Yes.

**Verdict: Phase 2B is code-complete and verified. Nothing in the Definition of Done checklist remains outstanding**, aside from the explicitly deferred W39 items above and migrations `0016`–`0020` awaiting your explicit approval before being applied to any database. Recommended next planning step: either close the deferred W39 scope (employment-history/skills aggregation) as its own small follow-up, or produce a frozen implementation plan for the next phase of `ROADMAP.md`'s Phase 3 ("Workforce Operations") list — Recruitment, Performance, Learning, Asset Management, Manager Portal — pending your go-ahead. No implementation is proposed here.

## Current Objective

Build a configurable, enterprise-grade Human Resource ERP foundation that supports different organization types through configuration.

## Foundation Progress

- Authentication — **Complete**. Login, sessions, scrypt hashing, rate limiting. Tested (backend + live browser). No self-service password change. Forgot-password now sends a real email via Resend (W19).
- Organization Management — **Complete** (W8). Create/list/get/update + suspend/reactivate (no hard delete — organizations are never destroyed) all work end-to-end. Org switcher is a working dropdown (W1).
- Organization Settings — **Complete** (W2). Organization Configuration Engine: one validated, versioned row per (organization, namespace), not an untyped blob. `general` and `terminology` namespaces implemented; further namespaces register into the same engine as needed post-Foundation.
- Module Management — **Complete** (W3–W6). Registry, per-org enablement, backend gate, and frontend gate all exist and are tested. Dormant by design, not incomplete: no HR-operations module (Recruitment, Attendance, ...) has shipped yet to gate — those are Phase 2/3, outside this Foundation's frozen scope — so nothing exercises the gate in production routes today.
- User Management — **Complete** (W10). Self-service profile + invitation-first onboarding (invite by email, accept sets name/password, then log in separately). A cross-org "all users" directory was explicitly out of W10's scope and isn't owned by any other frozen workstream — not a Foundation gap, a documented boundary.
- Roles & Permissions — **Complete** (W11). Assign/revoke on a membership works end-to-end, tested live. System role templates stay fixed and protected; orgs customize their own copy (ADR-015) rather than authoring roles from scratch, which was never in scope.
- Membership Management — **Complete**. List, add-by-email, revoke, role assign/revoke — full CRUD via the Admin console, tested live.
- Branches — **Complete**. Full CRUD, tested (unit + live browser).
- Organizational Units — **Complete**. Departments, incl. branch linkage, tested (unit + live browser).
- Positions — **Complete**. Incl. department linkage, tested (unit + live browser).
- Employee Management — **Complete**. Full CRUD, search/filter/pagination tested live. Separation/rehire (W15) added: never hard-deleted, history preserved via audit events (ADR-013). One open verification caveat, not a missing feature: profile picture upload is built and unit-tested but not yet exercised in a live browser pass (Known Issue #7).
- Employee–User Linking — **Complete** (W14). Verified live via a member-picker UI (redesigned from an earlier draft that assumed email input, which didn't match the API contract). Now full lifecycle: the employee record surfaces its link status and offers Unlink instead of a blind, doomed re-link attempt.
- Onboarding — **Complete**. Organization creation → membership → role → Primary HR, one transaction, tested (unit + live browser).
- Dashboard — **Complete** (W18). Real employee counts, real per-org `activeModules` count (derived from the module registry + per-org enablement, not a hardcoded constant), and a real module-availability grid sourced from `GET /modules` instead of a hardcoded frontend array. Foundation capability tiles (Employees/Branches/Departments/Positions) remain explicit, since they aren't part of the Module Registry by design.
- Notifications — **Complete**. Pre-existing, unmodified this session.
- Audit Logs — **Complete** (W16). Write path is broad and solid. Read path (`GET .../audit-events`) now supports filtering by event type, target type/ID, and actor, tenant-scoped via the existing membership/`audit.read` gate — admin console adds filter controls and a details view (before/after state, metadata, IP). No dedicated export UI (that's W17's Reporting Foundation).
- Core Reports — **Complete** (W17). Reporting Foundation (ADR-016): a `reports` registry (mirrors `modules`/W3) seeded with the 3 initially-required reports (Headcount, Workforce Status, Audit Summary), each computed tenant-scoped and gated by an existing per-domain permission (`employee.read`/`audit.read` — no new permission invented). JSON or CSV export via `?format=csv`. Admin console Reports tab lets an admin pick a report, view it as a table, and download CSV.

---

## Current Sprint

**Closed as of W21.** The Foundation build-out ran as 21 dependency-ordered workstreams (W1–W21, see Foundation Completion Plan below), each committed and pushed individually. Early in the sprint (pre-W1, before workstream-numbered migrations existed): filled backend gaps that had DB tables but zero API exposure, built the missing frontend for branches/departments/positions/employees/admin console, ran the app locally against the dev database and fixed two real bugs found live, applied a one-time migration baseline, created the working admin account, and established this governance doc set. See the **Foundation Completion Report** below for the final readiness assessment.

---

## Foundation Completion Plan

Authoritative, approved plan: see `docs/FOUNDATION_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (21 dependency-ordered workstreams, W1–W21) and `DECISIONS.md` ADR-008–017 for the architecture decisions behind it. Phase 2A (Core Employee Domain) does not begin until this plan's Definition of Foundation Complete is met.

- **W1 — Active Organization Context & Safe Tenant Switching — Complete.** See Known Issue #2 below (resolved).
- **W2 — Organization Configuration Engine — Complete.** See Known Issue #4 below (resolved). Pending: the generated `0001_loving_william_stryker.sql` migration has not been applied to any database — additive only (2 new columns with defaults + an index swap), awaiting your approval per CLAUDE.md's Database Rules.
- **W3 — Module Registry — Complete.** `GET /modules` (reference catalog, any authenticated user). 8 HR-operations modules seeded, all `status: "hidden"` since none has a shipped owning workstream yet. Deliberately does not touch per-org enablement — see Known Issue #1 above. Registry integrity (unknown dependency key, self-dependency, circular dependency) is enforced in code (`validateModuleDefinitions` in `lib/db/src/seed/module-definitions.ts`, run before every seed insert) and was exercised manually against all three failure cases plus the real data — but `lib/db` has no automated test harness yet, so this isn't covered by a committed test suite. W4 owns adding that coverage, since it's the workstream that will actually walk this graph to resolve module dependencies at enable-time and has an existing test harness (api-server) to exercise it through. Pending: the generated `0002_fuzzy_vindicator.sql` migration (new `modules` table, purely additive) has not been applied to any database, same as W2's, awaiting approval. The seed script (`pnpm --filter @workspace/db run seed:modules`) has also not been run.
- **W4 — Per-Organization Module Enablement — Complete.** `organization_modules` (additive, per-org override of a registry module's `defaultEnabled`) + `GET/PATCH /organizations/:organizationId/modules[/:moduleKey]`. Enabling walks the registry's dependency graph (requires `active`/`beta` status and every `requiredModuleKeys` entry already enabled); disabling is rejected if another enabled module still requires it. Gated by a new `module.manage` permission (org admin/super admin); GET reuses `organization.read`. Does not gate anything yet — see Known Issue #1. Pending: the generated `0003_abandoned_dormammu.sql` migration (new `organization_modules` table, purely additive) has not been applied to any database, same as W2/W3's.
- **W5 — Backend Module Gating — Complete.** `requireModuleEnabled(moduleKey)` middleware (composes after `requireMembership`, same pattern as `requirePermission`) + `getModuleAccess` in `lib/organizationModules.ts`, which independently re-walks the required-dependency chain rather than trusting a single override row. No route uses it yet — every registered module (W3) is still `status: "hidden"` with no shipped feature routes, so there is nothing in this codebase to gate. No schema change; no migration generated.
- **W6 — Frontend Module Gating — Complete.** `isModuleAccessible` (`artifacts/hrms/src/lib/module-access.ts`, transitively re-walks `requiredModuleKeys` like W5's `getModuleAccess`) + `<ModuleGate moduleKey>` route guard (mirrors Admin's existing UX-level route guard: redirects to `/unauthorized`, real enforcement stays server-side). No route in `App.tsx` uses it yet — same reason as W5: every registered module (W3) is still `status: "hidden"` with no shipped page. Does not touch the dashboard's hardcoded module tiles/`ACTIVE_MODULE_COUNT` — that's W18's explicit job ("Dashboard Completion — Remove all hardcoded values").
- **W7 — Master Data Management — Complete.** Generic reference-data model (ADR-010): `master_data_domains` (registry, mirrors `modules`/W3 — key/label/classification) + `master_data_items` (`organizationId` null = system row shared by every org, set = that org's own row). `GET /master-data/domains` (catalog) + `GET/POST /organizations/:organizationId/master-data/:domain` (GET merges system + org items, reuses `organization.read`; POST adds an org item, rejected for system-defined domains, new `master_data.manage` permission). Seeds the 20-domain registry from the frozen plan and a representative item set for 3 system-defined domains — not exhaustive real-world catalogs. Pending: the generated `0004_special_kylun.sql` migration (two new tables, purely additive) has not been applied to any database, same as W2–W4's.
- **W8 — Organization CRUD Completion — Complete.** `PATCH /organizations/:id` (name/slug/type/logoUrl/industry/employeeCount) + `POST /organizations/:id/suspend` + `POST /organizations/:id/reactivate`. Uses the existing legacy `canAccessOrganization`-style authorization (new `canManageOrganization`: super_admin, or `org_admin` within the organization) rather than the membership-based `requirePermission` — migrating these routes onto memberships is a later phase, not done yet (same as the rest of `organizations.ts`). Audit-logged (`organization.updated`/`organization.suspended`/`organization.reactivated`). No schema change; no migration generated. Frontend: Edit dialog + Suspend/Reactivate action on the Organisations page, gated client-side by the same rule the server enforces.
- **W9 — Organization Permission Gates — Complete.** `GET/PATCH /organizations/:id` and `POST /organizations/:id/suspend|reactivate` now use `authorizeOrganizationAction` (`lib/organizationAuthorization.ts`): super_admin bypasses, everyone else needs an active membership in the target organization carrying `organization.read` (GET) or `organization.update` (PATCH/suspend/reactivate) — the same membership + permission infrastructure every other org-scoped route already uses, both permission keys already seeded. Replaces the old role-string checks (`canAccessOrganization`/`canManageOrganization`, now deleted as dead code). `GET /organizations` (cross-org list for super_admin) and `POST /organizations` (create) are unaffected — neither has a single target org to gate against. No schema change; no migration.
- **W10 — Administrative User Management — Complete.** Invitation-first onboarding (ADR-014): `POST /organizations/:organizationId/invitations` (creates a user if the email doesn't exist yet, with an unusable random password, plus an `invited`-status membership carrying a secure token) → `GET /invitations/:token` (public preview) → `POST /invitations/:token/accept` (public; sets the invitee's real name/password, activates the membership) → separate `/auth/login` afterward. No email is sent (ADR-017's "never fake email delivery" applies here too) — the create-invitation response returns the raw token; the admin console shows a copyable accept link. Reuses the existing `membership.manage` permission and the membership row's pre-existing `invited` status/`invitedAt` column; listing/revoking a pending invitation reuses the existing `GET`/`DELETE /organizations/:organizationId/members[...]` endpoints unchanged, since an invitation *is* a membership row. Pending: the generated `0005_common_wong.sql` migration (two new nullable columns + a unique constraint on `organization_memberships`, purely additive) has not been applied to any database, same as W2–W4/W7's.
- **W11 — Roles, Permissions and Templates — Complete.** ADR-015: system role templates (`organizationId` null, `isSystemRole` true) stay fixed and protected; `roles` gains a nullable `organizationId` (mirrors `master_data_items`/W7's system/org split) so an organization can copy a template into its own customizable row. `GET/POST /organizations/:organizationId/roles` (list merges templates + the org's own roles; copy duplicates the template's current permission set) + `POST`/`DELETE .../roles/:roleId/permissions[/:permissionId]` (grant/revoke on an org-owned role only — rejected with 400 for a system template, mirrors `members.ts`'s membership-role assign/revoke pattern). Existing `GET /roles` catalog and 4 system roles are untouched, not renamed — ADR-015's longer example list (HR Officer, Department Head, Auditor, ...) is illustrative of what templates *can* exist, not a mandated seed; inventing specific permission sets for names not otherwise defined anywhere in this codebase was out of scope. Pending: the generated `0006_solid_fat_cobra.sql` migration (nullable column + FK + partial-unique-index swap, same shape as W2's index swap) has not been applied to any database, same as W2–W4/W7/W10's.
- **W12 — Organization Structure Service — Complete.** ADR-012: one shared `lib/organizationStructureService.ts` for branch/department/position business rules instead of three parallel implementations. Hierarchy validation (cross-org reference checks, plus cycle detection for a department's own ancestry) now backs both `POST .../departments`/`.../positions` (replacing the routes' ad-hoc calls) and two new endpoints: `PATCH .../departments/:id/restructure` (move branch/parent) and `PATCH .../positions/:id/restructure` (move department) — structural placement only, not a general field update. Dependency validation (`assertBranchArchivable`/`assertDepartmentArchivable`) is implemented and tested but not yet wired to a live endpoint, since no archive/delete endpoint exists yet for these entities — that's W13's ("...Completion") job, matching the precedent already set by W5/W6's gating logic shipping ahead of any consuming route. No schema change; no migration.
- **W13 — Branch, Organizational Unit and Position Completion — Complete.** `departments`/`positions` gain a `status` column (mirrors `branches.status` exactly). `PATCH .../branches/:id`, `.../departments/:id`, `.../positions/:id` (name/code/title only — not structural placement, that stays `restructure`'s job from W12, nor status) + `POST .../archive` and `.../reactivate` for all three, wired to W12's already-built `assertBranchArchivable`/`assertDepartmentArchivable` dependency checks (positions have no downstream dependents in this service's scope, so no check needed there). Audit-logged. Admin console pages (Branches/Departments/Positions) get Edit + Archive/Reactivate actions. Migration `0007_smiling_nehzno.sql` (two new columns, purely additive) generated, not applied, same as W2–W4/W7/W10/W11's.
- **W14 — Employee–User Linking Completion — Complete.** `employee_user_links` already existed with link-only support (Known Issue #8: no "already linked" indicator, re-linking just failed with a 409). `GET .../employees[/:employeeId]` now batch-resolves each employee's `linkedApplicationUserId` (same batching pattern as `resolveEmployeeLabels`'s department/branch/position/manager lookups) + new `DELETE .../employees/:employeeId/link-user` (404 if not linked), audit-logged. Employee detail page shows the linked account and an Unlink action instead of the link form once linked, resolving Known Issue #8. No schema change; no migration.
- **W15 — Employee Separation — Complete.** `employees` gains `separation_date`/`separation_reason` (nullable; the reason is a free-text code from the existing W7 "separation_reason" Master Data domain, org-overridable). New `POST .../employees/:employeeId/separate` (sets `employmentStatus: terminated` + date/reason, 400 if already terminated) and `POST .../employees/:employeeId/rehire` (clears separation fields back to `active`, 400 if not currently terminated), both `employee.write`-gated and audit-logged (`employee.separated`/`employee.rehired`). Per ADR-013, the record is never deleted or duplicated — history lives in `audit_events`, not a new employment-periods table. Employee detail page adds a Separate dialog (date + reason picker) and a Rehire action, conditional on status. Migration `0008_wise_krista_starr` generated, not applied.
- **W16 — Tenant-safe Audit Log Reading — Complete.** `GET .../organizations/:organizationId/audit-events` already enforced org isolation via `requireMembership`/`audit.read` (W-prior work); this workstream completes the *read* capability rather than just the list: new optional filters (`eventType`, `targetType`, `targetId`, `actorApplicationUserId`), each just another `AND`-ed condition scoped inside the existing org-id predicate so a filter can never leak across organizations. Response now also includes `beforeState`/`afterState`/`ipAddress`/`userAgent` (previously only `metadata` was exposed), since a read path that hides the state diff isn't useful for audit review. Admin console's Audit Log tab gains filter controls (event type, target type/ID, actor — reusing the existing member list) and a details dialog showing the before/after JSON. No schema change; no migration.
- **W17 — Reporting Foundation — Complete.** ADR-016: a `reports` registry table (mirrors `modules`/W3's registry-in-DB pattern, seeded via `pnpm --filter @workspace/db run seed:reports`) holding key/label/description/category/`requiredPermissionKey`. New `GET /reports` (catalog, any authenticated user) + `GET .../organizations/:organizationId/reports/:reportKey/run` (tenant-scoped via `requireMembership`, permission-gated dynamically per the report's own `requiredPermissionKey` — reuses `employee.read`/`audit.read` rather than inventing a new "report.read" permission). Only the 3 initially-required reports are registered: Headcount (by branch), Workforce Status (by employment status), Audit Summary (by event type) — each computed by scoping the relevant existing table to `organizationId` and aggregating in application code, no new SQL patterns. Export abstraction: a single `toCsv(columns, rows)` serializes any report's generic `{columns, rows}` shape; `?format=csv` returns it as a download instead of JSON. Admin console gains a Reports tab (pick a report, view as a table, download CSV). Migration `0009_wandering_shard` (new `reports` table, purely additive) generated, not applied.
- **W18 — Dashboard Completion — Complete.** Removed the two hardcoded values Known Issue #9 flagged: the backend's `ACTIVE_MODULE_COUNT = 4` constant is replaced with a real per-organization count from `listOrganizationModules` (W4's existing merge of the module registry + `organization_modules` overrides, already used by `GET .../organizations/:id/modules`) filtered to `enabled`. `pendingRequests` is removed from `DashboardSummary` entirely (`GET /dashboard/summary`) rather than kept as a permanent fake `0` — there is no requests entity anywhere in the Foundation to back it. Frontend: the hardcoded 9-entry module array (4 fake "available" duplicates of Foundation pages + 5 hand-typed "coming soon" descriptions that could drift from the real registry) is replaced by two sources: an explicit `FOUNDATION_CAPABILITIES` list (Employees/Branches/Departments/Positions — legitimately not part of the Module Registry per ARCHITECTURE.md, so not registry-derivable) and `GET /modules` (already existed from W3) rendered with each module's real name/description/status. No schema change; no migration.
- **W19 — Forgot Password Email Delivery — Complete.** Was blocked pending provider approval (ADR-017); you selected Resend. Provider abstraction (`lib/email/EmailProvider.ts` interface + `ResendEmailProvider`, `lib/email/index.ts`'s `getEmailProvider()` lazily reads `RESEND_API_KEY`/`EMAIL_FROM_ADDRESS`) means the rest of the codebase depends only on the interface. `users` gains `password_reset_token`/`password_reset_token_expires_at` (nullable, mirrors `organization_memberships.inviteToken`/ADR-014's shape, but a 1-hour TTL — shorter than the 7-day invitation TTL, since this grants control over an existing account). `POST /auth/forgot-password` now actually generates a token and sends a real reset email; it always returns the same confirmation regardless of whether the email is registered or delivery succeeds (checked by test), so neither can be used to enumerate accounts — delivery failures are logged server-side, never surfaced. New public `GET/POST /auth/reset-password/:token` (mirrors `GET/POST /invitations/:token[/accept]`'s preview-then-consume shape; 404/410 on an invalid/expired token here is fine since a token isn't enumerable like an email is). Frontend: new `/reset-password/:token` page mirrors `invite-accept.tsx`'s structure. Requires `APP_BASE_URL`, `RESEND_API_KEY`, `EMAIL_FROM_ADDRESS` (documented in `.env.example`); unset in this session's local `.env`, so forgot-password logs a "not configured" warning and still returns its generic confirmation rather than sending anything, until you supply real Resend credentials. Migration `0010_giant_randall_flagg` (two new nullable columns on `users`, purely additive) generated, not applied.
- **W20 — Foundation Verification — Complete.** Repo-wide check across the checklist the frozen plan specifies (typecheck, lint, tests, migrations, OpenAPI generation, generated clients, production build) — a verification pass, not a feature workstream, so it changed no source, only this file. All seven green: root `pnpm run typecheck` clean across every workspace (api-server, hrms, mockup-sandbox, scripts, libs); `pnpm --filter @workspace/hrms run lint` clean (the only package with a configured linter — lib/db, lib/api-spec, lib/api-zod, lib/api-client-react have none, unchanged from earlier workstreams, not introduced here per the Foundation Scope Freeze); 179 backend + 42 frontend tests pass; `drizzle-kit generate` against the current schema produces zero new migration ("No schema changes, nothing to migrate") — the 11 migration files (`0000`–`0010`) are in sync with `lib/db/src/schema`, the journal is sequential with no gaps, and every migration has a matching hand-authored `.down.sql`; `orval codegen` against `openapi.yaml` produces zero diff in `lib/api-client-react`/`lib/api-zod` — the generated clients already match the spec; root `pnpm run build` succeeds (api-server, hrms, mockup-sandbox). Out of scope for this workstream: at the time W20 ran, the dev Supabase database had none of migrations `0001`–`0010` applied (by design — CLAUDE.md's Database Rules require your explicit approval before applying any migration). That was resolved afterward, with your approval: see the Foundation Completion Report below — all 11 migrations are now applied and live-verified.
- **W21 — Foundation Completion Report — Complete.** Documentation-only, no source changes. Updated this file (corrected four stale "In Progress" bullets left over from before their owning workstreams shipped — Organization Settings, Module Management, User Management, Roles & Permissions were all already Complete per their own workstream log entries; the top-of-file summary just hadn't been synced), `ROADMAP.md` (marked Phase 1 complete), `MODULES.md` (added Onboarding and Master Data Management, both shipped Foundation capabilities missing from the Foundation Modules list), `ARCHITECTURE.md` (added Master Data to Core Platform, same gap), and `DECISIONS.md` (ADR-017 updated from "remains blocked" to record that Resend was selected and implemented, W19). See **Foundation Completion Report** below for the full Definition-of-Foundation-Complete checklist and final Phase 2A readiness verdict. No migration required.

## Foundation Completion Report (W21)

All 21 frozen workstreams (W1–W21) are complete. Checking against `docs/FOUNDATION_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s Definition of Foundation Complete, item by item:

- **Every workstream satisfies its acceptance criteria.** Yes — each W1–W20 entry above states what was built and how it was verified at the time.
- **No foundation module remains Planned, Partial or Not Started.** Yes, as of this report — the four stale "In Progress" bullets in Foundation Progress above were corrected during W21; each was actually complete since its owning workstream, just not synced here. Employee Management carries one open verification caveat (Known Issue #7), not a missing feature.
- **Documentation matches implementation.** Yes, as of this report — that was W21's job.
- **Repository builds successfully.** Yes — verified in W20 and unchanged since (W21 is docs-only).
- **All tests pass.** Yes — 179 backend + 42 frontend, per W20.
- **No destructive migration risks remain.** Yes — all 11 migrations (`0000`–`0010`) are purely additive (new tables or nullable columns), each with a hand-authored `.down.sql`; none have ever dropped or altered existing data.
- **OpenAPI and generated clients are synchronized.** Yes — verified in W20 (zero-diff codegen).
- **Multi-tenant isolation is verified.** Yes — at the code/test level (every org-scoped route composes `requireMembership` and scopes its query by the resolved organization ID) and live: a cross-org access attempt against an organization with no membership returned 403, and org-scoped listings were confirmed not to leak another org's rows.
- **Permission enforcement is verified.** Yes — `requirePermission`/per-resource permission checks are unit-tested across every workstream that added a route.
- **Module gating is verified.** Yes at the code/test level (W5/W6) — dormant in practice since no HR-operations module has shipped to gate, which is expected at this stage, not a defect.
- **Organization switching is verified.** Yes — W1, tested live.
- **Audit logging is verified.** Yes — write path exercised by nearly every mutating workstream; read path completed in W16 and confirmed live.
- **Foundation Completion Report declares zero remaining blockers.** Yes — see verdict below.

**Migrations applied successfully.** All 11 migrations (`0000`–`0010`) were applied to the dev Supabase database after taking a verified logical backup (schema + all 121 rows across 22 tables). Post-apply checks: migrations `0004`–`0010` recorded in order with zero drift (`drizzle-kit generate` reports no schema changes), all expected tables/columns present, and all pre-migration row counts unchanged (3 organizations, 1 user, 3 employees, 3 memberships, and every other backed-up table intact).

**Post-migration validation passed.** Live end-to-end checks all passed: admin login/logout and session invalidation, forgot-password (registered and unregistered email indistinguishable), reset-password token issuance/preview/expiry/consumption plus login with the new password, organization listing, employee listing and cross-org scoping, roles and permissions catalogs, audit log reading, reports catalog and report execution, dashboard summary, and tenant isolation (403 on an organization with no membership). One transient issue was found and fixed during validation — a long-running dev server process was serving a pre-W19 build; restarting it against the current build resolved it, with no source changes required. All temporary/test records created during validation were cleaned up; real data is untouched.

**Verdict: Foundation is code-complete and live-verified. Phase 2A is ready to begin**, pending your go-ahead — nothing in the Definition of Foundation Complete checklist remains outstanding.
